# REVIEW — STYXX v8 SPEC, DRAFT v0.1 (2026-09-07): the adversarial pass

Fathom Lab · 2026-09-08 · **A review ledger, not a result.** Reviewed: `STYXX v8 — SPEC · checksums for model behavior · DRAFT v0.1 · 2026-09-07` (the text is in `spec_v8_draft_v0.1.md` beside this file). Method: ten independent lenses (crypto-envelope, transparency-log, noise-floor-statistics, canary-selection, stranger-reproducibility, codebase-reconciliation, doctrine-and-commitments, adversary, internal-consistency, feasibility-and-sequencing) each read the spec, the repository at origin/main 4dba3a72 and the lab's doctrine memory; findings were deduplicated per section; one refuter judged every finding on textual accuracy and materiality; a second, steelman refuter re-judged every blocker; the completeness critic and the synthesis agent did not run (usage limit) — this file is rendered from the journal by a script, and the amended draft was written by hand from it. The fan-out was interrupted three times by a usage limit and resumed from cached results each time.

Counts: 207 raw findings → 147 distinct → **124 stand** (14 blocker, 88 major, 22 minor; 0 contested between the two refuters) · 23 refuted · 0 unverified.

Severity scale: blocker = as written, the Appendix D acceptance test or a §1 principle fails, or a verdict/number would be wrong; major = an unbacked claim, a contradiction, or a real attack; minor = an ambiguity a careful implementer would probably resolve correctly anyway.

## Findings that stand

### S0-02 · blocker · §0 header; §8.1; §8.3; §10; §13; §14
**Entry #0 (the spec's hash), the hourly sub-log STH and the docket summary are not certs of any of the eight types, so they fail the log's own append validation; and a raw hash of the CRLF-committed spec differs Windows vs Linux**

- claim: §8.1 admits only signed JCS certs of the eight §2 types, yet three mandated entries — a markdown document hash, a signed sub-log tree head, and a one-page human summary — are none of them; the spec cannot become binding under its own rule until the log it specifies exists; and because the spec is CRLF-committed markdown, a stranger on Linux computes a different entry-#0 hash.
- evidence: §0/header: "becomes binding when committed and its hash is logged as charon entry #0"; §14: "First entries, in order: #0 this spec's hash"; §8.1: "**Entry** = full cert bytes (JCS) + cert id."; §8.3: "CI validates schema, recomputes id, checks signature, appends"; §2: `"type": "fingerprint | battery | eval | prereg | result | promotion | action | challenge"`; §10: "every hour the sub-log's STH is submitted to charon as a single entry."; §13: "Contents, all as certs in charon: ... 6. **summary** — a one-page human summary"; §14 weeks 1–4: "Charon v0: git-backed log, Merkle tree, STH ...". SCOUT: "Repo is CRLF on Windows ... a raw hash of working-tree bytes differs Windows vs Linux (charon v0.1 E15 lesson)"; styxx/charon.py:134-153 `_content_sha256`; memory charon-v01-ferry-log.md E15.
- consequence: Either entry #0 is a non-cert line (breaking §8.1 and every proof tool that parses entries as certs) or the spec is never binding; the sub-log root has no type, no `issuer`, no `sig` rule and no schema to validate under §8.3, so the "two inclusion proofs" chain in §10 has an undefined middle link; the summary is a 'note' by §0's own rule; and if entry #0 is hashed from the checkout, a Linux mirror recomputes a different hash, App. D step 1 fails on entry #0 itself, and the binding entry reads as tampered.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED on the type set (verdict/type vocabulary changes are operator-gated per doctrine). Option 1 (least change): entry #0 is a `result` cert, `body.kind = "document"` (or `spec`), `body = {path, git_blob_sha1, content_sha256 (LF-normalized UTF-8), commit}`, signed by the lab key; the hourly roll is an `action` cert with `body.action_kind = "roll"` and `body.sublog_sth = {log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp, sig}` whose `sig` uses the sub-log STH domain string; the summary is a `result` kind `document`. Option 2: add a new type `attachment` with body `{ "sha256": "...", "media": "text/markdown | application/json", "role": "spec | sublog_sth | summary" }` and use it for entry #0, the hourly sub-log STH, and the docket summary, and say so in §2. In either option: state the LF-normalization rule in A.1, pin the spec with `.gitattributes` `eol=lf` as the sworn documents already are, and rewrite §0: "becomes binding when committed; its sha256 is logged as charon entry #0 when the log first exists (§14 week 4)."
- sources: crypto-envelope, stranger-reproducibility, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Checked: §8.1 'Entry = full cert bytes (JCS) + cert id', §8.3 'CI validates schema, recomputes id, checks signature', §2 fixes eight types, §7.2 closes result.kind to {confirmatory, pilot, robustness, verify, response}, and §10 closes action_kind to {tool_call, message, commit}; yet line 3 makes the spec binding via 'charon entry #0', §14 mandates '#0 this spec's hash', §10 submits a bare sub-log STH 'as a single entry', and §13 lists the summary among 'all as certs'. The best author-side defense is that #0 is a header line like v0.1's (seq starts at 1 after a header) and so is outside §8.1, and that the summary/roll are just under-specified bodies to be filled in week 1; but §14 literally calls #0 an entry, §8.1 admits no header, and no type/body/issuer/sig rule exists for any of the three, so Appendix D step 1 cannot run on the log's own binding entry and the document's stated binding condition is unsatisfiable under its own rules. The CRLF half is weaker than stated: the draft in scratchpad has zero CR bytes and is not committed, so 'CRLF-committed' is conjecture, though the absence of an LF-normalization rule in A.1 is real and charon.py:_content_sha256 documents exactly this lesson (E15). A spec whose binding event fails its own append validation cannot be frozen as-is; blocker stands, with the fix being small (pick a type, or number the spec hash as the header).

### S10-01 · blocker · §10; §2; §11 `agent roll`
**Action sub-log root has no cert type to carry it; §10 is unappendable under §2 and the two-proof chain is unverifiable**

- claim: The hourly sub-log STH must enter charon 'as a single entry', but every entry is a cert of one of eight types and none has a field for a sub-log root, tree size, or the agent's log key; CI schema validation (§8.3) would reject it. Nothing requires the sub-log to be published or its consecutive roots to be consistency-checked, so the agent can rewrite past actions between rolls.
- evidence: §2: "One schema, eight types." `"type": "fingerprint | battery | eval | prereg | result | promotion | action | challenge"`. §10 `action` body: `context_sha256, readings, action_sha256, action_kind, ts` — no root field. §10: "Per-action certs are stored locally in a **sub-log** (same Merkle structure); every hour the sub-log's STH is submitted to charon as a single entry. Any individual action is then provable with two inclusion proofs: action → sub-log root, sub-log root → charon." §10 only says darkflobi 'publishes its sub-log'; no rule for other emitters. §11: `styxx agent emit | roll`.
- consequence: `agent roll` cannot produce a valid entry and `log append` cannot schema-validate it; if a body is improvised, a client cannot verify 'action → sub-log root' because the sub-log's hashing, key, and availability are unspecified, and an agent can rewrite or drop an unflattering action from its local sub-log before the next roll with no consistency proof to catch it — the exact suppression `styxx/transparency.py:4-8` names as the gap the log exists to close.
- amendment: Add a ninth type `sublog` with body `{ sublog_id: sha256(agent pubkey), tree_size, root_hash, prev_root_hash, prev_tree_size, consistency_proof: [...], count_since_prev, entries_url_or_sha256 }`, signed by the agent's issuer key, refs the previous `sublog` cert so roll entries chain and a size regression is detectable; require the sub-log ndjson to be published (same layout as §8.2) for any `sublog` cert to count as verifiable; define client verification: verify sub-log inclusion under `root_hash`, verify consistency (prev → this), verify charon inclusion of the `sublog` cert.
- sources: transparency-log, adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Checked: §2 'One schema, eight types', the §10 action body carries only context_sha256/readings/action_sha256/action_kind/ts with action_kind closed to tool_call|message|commit, §10 says the sub-log STH is 'submitted to charon as a single entry', §11 ships `agent roll` in the 8.0 CLI, §14 puts action certs and `agent roll` inside the 8.0 build (weeks 8-12), and styxx/transparency.py:4-8 names suppression of unflattering runs as the gap the log exists to close. Author's side: §10 says 'same Merkle structure', darkflobi is told to publish its sub-log, this is the last build phase, and the fix is purely additive (a ninth type or a roll kind), so nothing already specified produces a wrong number. But as written there is no entry that CI can schema-validate for a roll, so §10 and `agent roll` are unimplementable under §8.3, a stranger holding an action cert cannot perform Appendix D step 2 (which §D declares a spec failure), and the spec's threat model (§8.6 'silent rewriting') has no consistency requirement between consecutive sub-log roots. Confidence medium only on severity: the entry-type half overlaps S0-02 and one could call the whole thing a major schema omission in a deferred phase, but the spec claims to be binding on commit and its reference deployment section is unexecutable under its own §2/§8.3, so blocker stands.

### S2-01 · blocker · §2.2; §2.3; §5.1; §5.3; §9; App. D steps 4-5
**Subject S includes hardware/runtime while §2.3 comparability excludes them; the noise floor either never transfers to a stranger or always exits 2**

- claim: §2.2 puts `runtime` and `hardware` in S, §5.3 voids the floor whenever S changes, yet §2.3 makes certs comparable on four recipe fields only, §5.1 varies GPU only 'where available' (this lab has one), §9 requires challengers on distinct hardware, and App. D expects a stranger's re-run on other hardware to be scored against the cert's floor — the two readings are contradictory and both break App. D.
- evidence: §2.2 subject: `"runtime": { "framework": "transformers", "version": "4.x.y", "backend": "torch 2.x cu12x" }, "hardware": { "gpu": "RTX 4090", "driver": "...", "count": 1 }` and `"precision"`. §5.3: "Floors are per (S, B, recipe). Change any of them and the floor is void — `verify` exits 2, not 0." §2.3: "Two certs are **comparable** iff `battery`, `decoding`, `chat_template_sha256`, and `system_prompt_sha256` match exactly." §5.1 step 1: "varying only nuisance factors: batch size, item order, physical GPU / driver where available"; SCOUT.md: "One GPU (per memory)"; §15.1 names a single Alienware. §9: "from independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)". App. D step 5: "If the result exceeds the cert's noise floor on a confirmation run, log a challenge."
- consequence: Reading A (§5.3 governs, hardware in S): every stranger's `verify --ref` exits 2, App. D steps 4-5 are unreachable, challenges from 'distinct hardware' have no applicable floor, and the R-run floor is itself void if any run used a second GPU. Reading B (§2.3 governs): the floor was sampled from same-box runs where greedy decoding is deterministic up to batch-shape effects, so `exact`/`lens` floors are ~0; a stranger's different GPU/cuBLAS/driver produces nonzero greedy divergence on an unchanged model, the confirmation run repeats it, verdict `drift` (exit 1), two such strangers make the cert `disputed`, and a `reproduction` requires bit-identical cross-hardware outputs. A stranger whose GPU lacks bf16 and runs fp16 also passes the recipe check while comparing a different subject. Either way §1 principle 3 ('exceeds what re-running the same model produces') is false because the null was never sampled from the population verify is applied to.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED. First, in all cases: split the subject into identity fields `S_floor = (weights_sha256, config_sha256, tokenizer_sha256, precision)` for weights subjects and `(provider, alias)` for alias subjects, and environment fields (`runtime`, `hardware`) that are recorded and printed but never keyed; add identity fields to the §2.3 comparability rule (mismatch → exit 3, a different subject is never `drift`); rewrite §5.3 as "Floors are per (S_floor, B, recipe_core)" naming exactly which fields void the floor; add `noise_floor.nuisance_varied: ["batch_size", "item_order", "gpu", "driver", ...]` so a reader sees what the floor covers. Then choose: (A) hardware is nuisance — a public fingerprint's floor is valid only if `noise_floor.runs` spans ≥ 2 distinct (gpu, driver) pairs and ≥ 2 runtime versions, recorded as `noise_floor.hardware_span`; otherwise the cert is labelled `floor_scope: single-hardware` and a cross-hardware `verify --ref` can report only `same`/`inconclusive`/`reproduction (hardware not in floor)`, never `drift`; or (B) hardware is identity — a stranger runs their OWN R-run floor on their box and `drift` requires exceeding max(floor_cert, floor_stranger); cross-hardware runs are `challenge`/`reproduction` events and §9's disputed rule is restated on that basis.
- sources: noise-floor-statistics, stranger-reproducibility, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually exact: §2.2 places `runtime`, `hardware`, `precision` inside `subject`; §5.3 voids the floor on any change to S with exit 2; §2.3 comparability tests only four recipe fields; §5.1 lists GPU/driver as nuisance "where available"; §9 requires challengers on distinct hardware; App. D step 5 scores a stranger's run against the cert's floor. The author's strongest reading (S = identity, hardware = recorded nuisance) still fails on the spec's own terms: the floor is sampled on one box (§15.1), canary-v1 selects low-margin items by construction (§4.3–4.4) and its self-declared load-bearing exclusion step (δ2 includes "second GPU if available") cannot be run for hardware, so the null is never drawn from the population `verify --ref` and §9 challenges are applied to. Under the literal reading every stranger's `verify --ref` exits 2 and App. D 4–5 are unreachable. Either way the acceptance test fails and the resolution is an operator-gated design choice; blocker stands.

### S2-02 · blocker · §2.3; §5.2; §5.3; §6
**Harness/runtime/env_lock are recorded but never compared and 'recipe' means four fields for comparability but the whole object for floors — a verifier change reads as model `drift`, the SKEW-vs-DRIFT defect charon v0.1 already fixed**

- claim: §2.3 declares certs comparable on battery/decoding/template/system-prompt only, so `recipe.harness.version/commit`, `runtime.version` and `env_lock_sha256` never gate anything and a styxx or transformers change between reference and confirmation run yields `drift` (exit 1); simultaneously §5.3 keys floors on the whole recipe, so the same harness patch voids every floor (exit 2) while leaving certs comparable — the exit code depends on which sentence the implementer read, and neither path has a `skew` vocabulary.
- evidence: §2.3: `"harness": { "name": "styxx", "version": "8.0.0", "commit": "<sha>" }`, `"env_lock_sha256"`, `"sae"` in the recipe object; "Two certs are **comparable** iff `battery`, `decoding`, `chat_template_sha256`, and `system_prompt_sha256` match exactly. Otherwise `verify` exits 3". §5.3: "Floors are per (S, B, recipe). Change any of them and the floor is void — `verify` exits 2, not 0." §5.2 table has only inconclusive/same/same (transient)/drift/identity. Repo: styxx/charon.py:92 `STATUSES = ("SAME_LINE", "MOVED_VERIFIER", "SKEW", "DRIFT", "UNRESOLVED", "HEAD_MISMATCH", "TAMPER")`, docstring lines 25-28: "``verify`` can then tell SKEW (the line moved and the instrument's bytes moved) from DRIFT (the line moved under the same build)"; papers/charon/SPEC_charon_v01_2026_09_02.md ERRATA E6 "the verifier is a path, not a file ... a change outside it reads as DRIFT"; memory charon-v01-ferry-log.md item 4 ("SKEW saw one file").
- consequence: A styxx 8.0.1 patch that changes float rendering of `seq_logprob` or the −20 fill in Appendix B, or a transformers point release that changes tokenization of a trailing newline, flips every `verify --ref` against 8.0.0 certs to `drift`; CI pipelines keyed on exit 1 fail, the compliance view records a model change that did not happen, the stranger logs a challenge, and the issuer's `response` has no cert vocabulary to say 'verifier moved', so the log accumulates false drift threads. After the same release every existing fingerprint is simultaneously floor-less under §5.3.
- amendment: Define in §2.3 `recipe_core = {battery, decoding, chat_template_sha256, system_prompt_sha256}`; comparability and floors are both keyed on `(S_floor, recipe_core)`; state which recipe fields are comparability-gating (exit 3), which are skew-gating (exit 2: `harness.commit`, `harness.version`, `runtime.version`, `env_lock_sha256`), and which are informational (`sae`, printed only). Add to §5.2 a row and to §6 a verdict: "`recipe.harness.commit`, `runtime.version` or `env_lock_sha256` differs between ref and new, and any channel exceeds floor → `skew` (exit 2, not 1), printed as `skew: harness <a> vs <b>` / `runtime <a> vs <b>` beside the per-channel distances: the instrument moved; re-establish the floor under the new harness before any drift claim." Add `harness.commit` to the fields `verify --diff` prints under mismatch. Rewrite §5.3 to enumerate the voiding fields instead of "any of them".
- sources: stranger-reproducibility, codebase-reconciliation, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Author's best case: §9's challenge body carries `recipe_match` and a separate `environment: {hardware, runtime}`, which shows the intended recipe key is the four-field tuple of §2.3 and harness/runtime/env_lock are reproduction context, not comparison gates — so the 'whole object voids every floor' half is the weaker reading, and the finding's seq_logprob-rendering example is wrong (A.4 compares logprobs numerically, never by hash) and its −20-fill example is a spec change, not a harness change. What survives under the coherent four-field reading is exactly the core claim: a transformers or styxx change between reference and confirmation run (spec §2.2 pins `"version": "4.x.y"`; the box runs 4.57.3) produces a confirmed `drift` (exit 1), which §5 defines as 'the model changed' and §12 files under change tracking, and §5.2/§6 have no `skew` row while `verify` never reads or prints `harness.commit`/`runtime.version`. styxx/charon.py:103 STATUSES and SPEC_charon_v01 status table (SKEW = instrument moved, DRIFT = bytes moved) plus E6 are the lab's own receipted lesson that this conflation is a defect, so v8 regresses a committed distinction; principle 3 ('no drift claim without a measured null' for the *same* model) is violated by construction. Blocker stands because §5.2 and §5.3 do not say what 'recipe' means as a key, two implementers land on different exit codes (1 vs 2), and neither is a correct verdict; adding a `skew` verdict is operator-gated vocabulary, which the amendment should flag.

### S2-04 · blocker · §2.3; §1 principle 2; App. D steps 3-4
**Recipe carries hashes of the template, system prompt and lockfile but not the bytes; a stranger cannot re-run the recipe from the cert**

- claim: The recipe stores `chat_template_sha256`, `system_prompt_sha256` and `env_lock_sha256` only; nothing in the eight cert types carries the template text, the system prompt text, the lockfile, or says where they came from, so the stranger has nothing to feed `verify --ref` and can at best confirm a guess.
- evidence: §2.3: `"chat_template_sha256": "...", "system_prompt_sha256": "..."` and `"env_lock_sha256": "<sha256 of pip freeze / lockfile>"`; §1.2: "A cert contains everything needed to re-run it: model identity, battery, decoding, template, environment."; §2: eight types, none is an artifact/blob type; A.2 hashes only `config.json` and `tokenizer.json`, not `tokenizer_config.json` where HF chat templates live, nor `generation_config.json`; App. D: "If any step is impossible from the cert alone, the cert is defective".
- consequence: A non-empty system prompt or any template not byte-identical to the one in `tokenizer_config.json` at the pinned revision is irrecoverable from the cert; the stranger's run either fails the recipe check (exit 3) or silently runs a different prompt and compares against the issuer's floor. `env_lock_sha256` is a hash of a document that is never published and is platform-specific (`pip freeze` on this box carries `+cu121` locals, Linux CI does not), so no stranger can ever match it — under §0's own rule it is a note, not a claim.
- amendment: Add to §2.3: `"chat_template": "<full text>"` and `"system_prompt": "<full text, may be empty string>"` stored in the clear (hash fields become derived and are checked at append); add `"chat_template_source": "tokenizer_config.json@<revision>" | "inline"`. For the environment: either add a ninth cert type `artifact` (body = `{media_type, bytes_b64 or text}`) and make `env_lock` a ref to it, or rename `env_lock_sha256` to `env_lock_note_sha256` and state in §2.3 that it is informational and never part of comparability. Extend A.2 to hash `tokenizer_config.json`, `special_tokens_map.json` and `generation_config.json`, and state in §2.3 that every generation parameter not listed in `decoding` is taken from the pinned `generation_config.json` and no other source.
- sources: stranger-reproducibility
- verifiers: STANDS: Author's strongest defense: §2.2 pins `hf_repo` + `revision`, and at a pinned revision the stranger receives tokenizer_config.json (confirmed on this box: gemma-2-2b-it's 591-char chat template lives there, alongside generation_config.json with eos_token_id [1,107]), so for the default recipe — repo template, empty system prompt — `chat_template_sha256` is a check, not the only copy, and App. D step 4 is reachable; and env_lock is not among §2.3's four comparability fields, so an unmatched lockfile costs nothing at verify. But the spec never states that default: §2.3 stores only `system_prompt_sha256`, no cert type carries the prompt text, and §1.2 promises the cert 'contains ... template, environment' when it contains hashes of them; any non-empty system prompt is irrecoverable by construction, any alias subject has no revision to fall back on, and A.2 hashes config.json/tokenizer.json but not tokenizer_config.json or generation_config.json, so the files that actually determine the template and stop behaviour are unpinned by the subject block. App. D's own rule ('if any step is impossible from the cert alone ... the spec has failed') fires on the first cert with a system prompt, which the spec nowhere forbids. Blocker holds on the template/system-prompt gap; the env_lock sub-point is minor (informational field, should be labeled so).

### S3-01 · blocker · §3.1; §3.2; App. A.3; App. B
**The `exact` checksum is not computable by a stranger: §3.1 hashes text digests, §3.2 hashes token ids, and no preimage or concatenation encoding is defined**

- claim: `output_sha256`, `token_ids_sha256` and `channels.exact.hash` are named without their byte inputs, §3.2 defines `exact` over token ids while §3.1 builds the battery hash from text hashes, and the App. B match rule does not say which field is compared.
- evidence: §3.2: "**exact** — greedy output token ids per item. The battery-level hash is *the checksum*." §3.1: `"exact": { "hash": "sha256 of concatenated output_sha256 in battery order" }` — `output_sha256` is the text hash, `token_ids_sha256` is a separate item field. Neither the text encoding (decoded text? which decode? UTF-8? U+FFFD for undecodable bytes?), the integer encoding for ids, nor whether "concatenated" means raw 32-byte digests or 64-char hex is stated. App. B exact: "1 − (matching items / items)" — matching on which field is unstated.
- consequence: The product's headline number ("a reproducible checksum for a model's behavior", §0) is not reproducible: two independent implementations of `styxx fingerprint` on identical outputs produce different `exact.hash` values, so `verify --diff` between a stranger's cert and the lab's reports drift that is an encoding artefact and App. D step 4 fails. Two tokenizations that detokenize to the same text (or one text with different ids) give `same` under one reading and `drift` under the other.
- amendment: exact channel = token ids. Define in App. A: `token_ids_sha256 = sha256(JCS(ids))` where `ids` is the JSON array of integer token ids; `output_text = tokenizer.decode(ids, skip_special_tokens=false, errors="replace")` and `output_sha256 = sha256(UTF-8(output_text))`; §3.1: `"exact": { "hash": "sha256 of concatenated raw 32-byte token_ids_sha256 digests in item_id order" }`; App. B: "matching = equal `token_ids_sha256`". (The `vector_sha256` fields on seqlp/topk are addressed in S3-02.)
- sources: crypto-envelope, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Checked: §3.2 defines exact as 'greedy output token ids per item', §3.1 defines `channels.exact.hash` as 'sha256 of concatenated output_sha256 in battery order' where `output_sha256` and `token_ids_sha256` are distinct item fields; A.3 says 'concatenate per-item hashes' without saying raw digest or hex; A.4 covers only floats; App. B's exact row never names the compared field. The author's best defense is that `recipe.harness.commit` pins one implementation so a stranger reproduces by re-running the same code, and A.1's `sha256:<hex>` rendering arguably fixes hex-vs-raw — but §1 principle 1 says 'same evidence, same id, anywhere', not 'same code', and the lab's own doctrine (leg 3: a second implementation agreeing on 1689/1689 vectors, conformance vectors) makes independent implementability the standard; a spec whose headline checksum has no defined preimage cannot have conformance vectors. The text-vs-ids contradiction is a genuine verdict fork (§3.1 literal → `same`, §3.2 → `drift` when tokenizations detokenize identically). The fix is three sentences, but the consequence — the product's checksum is implementation-dependent — is blocker-class.

### S4-01 · blocker · §4.4 step 1; §5.1; §5.2; §5.3; §6; App. B
**"Noise-floor pool (§5)" does not exist; canary floors are 0 by construction (selection reuses the nuisance sweep), one flip = drift, and ratio = distance/0 cannot be JCS-encoded**

- claim: §4.4 sends flip2>0 items to a 'noise-floor pool' that §5 never defines, and canary items are selected to have zero flips under exactly the nuisance factors (batch size, order, GPU) that the §5 floor then varies, so the exact/lens floor on a canary battery is 0 with high probability; the verify result's `ratio` then divides by zero and the cert cannot be RFC 8785-encoded.
- evidence: §4.4 step 1: "**Exclude** every item with `flip2(i) > 0`. Those are nuisance-unstable; they go into the noise-floor pool (§5), never the battery." §4.2 δ2 = "batch size ∈ {1, 8, 32}, permuted item order, second GPU if available"; §5.1 varies "batch size, item order, physical GPU / driver where available" — the same factors; §5.1: "For subject S and battery B: 1. Run the recipe R times" and "`floor_c` = max(D_c)"; §5 contains no word "pool". §5.3: "Floors are per (S, B, recipe). Change any of them and the floor is void — verify exits 2". App. B exact = "1 − (matching items / items), over canaries" (quantum 1/256 = 0.0039); "Noise floors are computed with the same functions on same-subject runs". §6: per-channel `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}`. §2.1: canonical bytes are RFC 8785 — JSON has no Infinity/NaN; SCOUT.md: the lab's own jcs is "scoped to ... finite doubles, no NaN/Inf". §1.3: "'The model changed' is only sayable when the distance exceeds what re-running the *same* model produces."
- consequence: Reading A (floor measured on the excluded items): B differs from the fingerprint's battery, so §5.3 makes every canary verify exit 2, and those items were selected for instability so drift is never detectable. Reading B (δ2 runs reused as the R floor runs): every selected item has zero flips across exactly those runs, D_exact = {0,...}, floor_exact = 0; the first stray batch-shape flip in deployment (0.0039 > 0) is `drift` (exit 1) after a confirmation run that also has a nonzero chance of a flip — the verdict is produced by selection, not measurement (winner's curse). Simulation of the discrete null (per-item flip prob p, N=256, R=5): p=0 gives floor exactly 0; p=0.002–0.03 gives 9–11% single-run false positives and 2.4–3.1% after confirmation. Separately, a result cert with `floor: 0` and `distance: 0.0039` has `ratio: Infinity`, fails §2.1 and cannot be appended. If the floor is on different items than the distance, App. B's 'same units' sentence is false.
- amendment: (1) Delete "they go into the noise-floor pool (§5)"; replace with "they are excluded and listed in `body.excluded` with their flip2". The floor MUST be computed on battery B itself. (2) Add to §5.1: "Floor runs are fresh runs, disjoint from every run used in §4 selection; `noise_floor.runs` ∩ `battery.body.selection_runs` = ∅ and `verify` exits 2 if the intersection is non-empty"; add `selection_runs: ["sha256:…"]` to the battery body. (3) Add §5.3 bullet: "For canary-v1 the floor is measured on items selected for flip2 = 0 and is a lower bound on nuisance variation; the cert records `noise_floor.selection_biased: true` and `verify` prints it." (4) Define `ratio` as null when floor = 0 (never a non-finite double). (5) OPERATOR-GATED floor quantum for discrete channels: option A — `floor_exact = max(max(D_c), q/N)` with q ≥ 2 flips so a single flip is never drift; option B — floor from a one-sided binomial upper bound (e.g. 95% Clopper-Pearson) on the per-item flip rate given (R choose 2)·N item-comparisons with k observed flips, stored as `floor_upper`; option C — accept floor 0 and rename the verdict for 1..q flips `changed-below-power` with no drift vocabulary. Also OPERATOR-GATED whether R ≥ 8 is required for canary floors. Record the chosen rule in the fingerprint cert.
- sources: noise-floor-statistics, canary-selection, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Partly overstated, core survives. Grep confirms 'pool' appears only at §4.4, §4.5, §11 and App. C — §5 defines no noise-floor pool, so §4.4's cross-reference is dangling (editorial). The 'floor is 0 by construction / winner's curse' framing is weaker than claimed: §5.1 says 'For subject S and battery B: Run the recipe R times' and step 4 logs every run as its own fingerprint cert, so floor runs are fresh, not the δ2 sweep; a fresh-run floor of 0 on items selected for stability is a measurement, and §1.3 is literally satisfied. But two things stand: (1) floor_exact = 0 is the ordinary outcome for greedy batch-1 on one GPU regardless of selection, and with the 1/N quantum any single stray flip exceeds it, so §5.2's drift verdict on discrete channels rests on a floor with no width — the confirmation run helps but is not a calibrated rule; (2) §6 emits `ratio` = distance/floor in the result cert, which is non-finite when floor = 0 and distance > 0; §2.1 mandates RFC 8785 and the repo's own `_es_number_to_string` (styxx/attestation.py) raises ValueError on inf — the verify result cert cannot be issued or appended in the common case, failing App. D. An unissuable cert in the ordinary path keeps this at blocker even though the fix (ratio null; OPERATOR-GATED floor quantum) is small. The finding's simulation percentages were not re-run.

### S4-02 · blocker · §4.4 step 5; §5.2; §6; §1 principle 3; App. C
**`identity` from a single anchor flip is an unbacked swap accusation with no measured null and no confirmation run, and it overrides every channel verdict**

- claim: Anchors are 'zero flips' in a sweep of 3–10 variants, which bounds their per-item flip probability only at ~26–63%, yet one flip among 64 anchors is asserted from a single run to mean 'tokenizer or weights swap', overrides everything, exits 1, and can be used by a hostile issuer to convert a drift finding into a versioning event.
- evidence: §1.3: "No drift claim without a measured null." §4.4 step 5: "Add **K = 64 anchors**: the highest-margin items with zero flips under every δ. Anchors should never change. An anchor flip means a tokenizer or weights swap, not drift — `verify` reports it as a separate verdict (`identity`)." §5.2: "any anchor item changed | `identity` (reported separately, always)" and "Overall verdict: `drift` if any channel is `drift`; `identity` overrides everything"; the drift row requires "first run **and** confirmation run", the identity row has no confirmation. §4.2: δ2 = 3 (or 4) variants, δ1 has 2, δ3 has 3 samples. §5.3: a tokenizer change alters `tokenizer_sha256` so "the floor is void — `verify` exits 2"; §2.3 excludes `tokenizer_sha256` from comparability; §5.2 has no row comparing subject hashes for weights subjects. Memory `external1-gate-failed-in-wild.md`: an accusation class with 0.23 precision was DISABLED.
- consequence: Zero flips in n variants gives a 95% upper bound on per-item flip probability of 1−0.05^(1/n): 63% for n=3, 31% for n=8, 26% for n=10. If the true cross-environment flip rate of a high-margin 64-token greedy item is 0.5%, P(≥1 of 64 anchors flips) ≈ 27% per verify run; at 1% it is 47%; at 0.1% still 6%. For the quick battery (N=64, K=64) half the battery is zero-tolerance. Each event produces an exit-1 `identity` that the compliance view (§12) renders as a weights/tokenizer swap on an unchanged model — a false accusation of the class the lab already disabled once. Attack: model drifted; issuer changes one added-token entry in the tokenizer (subject stays comparable), re-issues; verify reports overall `identity`, the channel table is subordinate, and the docket text reads 'identity: tokenizer swap' — a versioning event, not a behavioural change. A hostile challenger on an alias gets a logged `identity` on a competitor from one nondeterministic flip. Exit code 1 collapses drift and identity, so CI cannot distinguish. The rule also contradicts §5.3 (a real tokenizer change ⇒ exit 2, not 1). 'Anchor flip means a swap' is a causal claim with no receipt: a safety fine-tune can flip a high-margin refusal item.
- amendment: (1) `identity (hash: <which>)` is emitted only when a subject field (`weights_sha256`, `tokenizer_sha256`, `config_sha256`) differs between ref and new — add that row to §5.2, reported always, independent of anchors. (2) Anchor flips become a separately reported count `anchors_changed: k/64` with verdict `anchor_change` (no cause asserted); delete "not drift" in §4.4.5 and write "reported in addition to the channel verdicts". (3) `anchor_change` requires the same confirmation run as `drift`; a single-run flip is `anchor_change (unconfirmed)`. (4) Give anchors their own floor from the R fresh runs: `floor_anchor` = max over run pairs of the number of anchors that differ, recorded in `noise_floor.per_channel.anchor`; escalation to exit status only when k exceeds `floor_anchor` on both runs and k ≥ K_id (OPERATOR-GATED, e.g. 8 of 64, chosen so the measured single-anchor cross-environment rate gives P(false identity) < 1%). (5) Record |δ2| explicitly in the battery cert and require |δ| ≥ 10 so `flip = 0` bounds the item's flip probability below 26% at 95%. (6) §5.2: "Overall verdict is the SET of per-channel verdicts plus `identity` when present; `identity` never replaces a channel verdict"; text output prints `identity + drift(exact, seqlp)`. (7) Fix the override order: subject-hash mismatch on a comparability field ⇒ exit 2 with a named reason. §6: exit 1 = drift; add exit code 4 = identity (with or without drift).
- sources: noise-floor-statistics, canary-selection, adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually exact: §4.4.5 'An anchor flip means a tokenizer or weights swap, not drift'; §5.2 identity row has no confirmation run while the drift row requires one; 'identity overrides everything'; §6 folds identity into exit 1. The author's strongest defense is that anchors are the highest-margin items with zero flips under δ1 precision changes (far harsher than nuisance), so an anchor flip is physically strong evidence, and that §5.3 already routes declared subject changes to exit 2 — meaning `identity` is aimed at silent alias swaps, its intended use. That defense does not rescue the text: a causal 'means a swap' sentence with no receipt, no measured null (anchors get none even though the §5.1 R-runs would yield one for free), no confirmation, and an override that erases the per-channel table contradicts §1.3 and the lab's standing doctrine after EXTERNAL-1 (`external1-gate-failed-in-wild.md`: an accusation class at 0.23 precision was DISABLED). §12 would render it under 'change tracking' against a possibly unchanged model. Single-run, null-free accusation with the strongest override is a wrong-verdict path; blocker stands. Amendment items (2)-(4),(6),(7) are the right shape; K_id and |δ| ≥ 10 are OPERATOR-GATED.

### S4-06 · blocker · §4.1; §4.2 δ2; §5.1; §9; App. D steps 4–5
**Canaries maximize numeric sensitivity, the floor spans one GPU, and App. D asks strangers on other hardware to file challenges: every honest canary cert becomes `disputed`**

- claim: Canary items are by construction those whose greedy output flips under small numeric perturbation; a floor measured on the issuer's single GPU does not cover a challenger's different kernels/driver, so reproduction on other hardware exceeds the floor on the `exact` channel and two such reproductions mark the cert disputed.
- evidence: §4.2: "δ1 | precision ... | sensitivity to representational change"; "second GPU if available" (the lab has one: glimmer REPORT.md:18). §5.1: "physical GPU / driver where available". §9: "Disputed status ...: a cert with ≥ 2 unresolved challenges above the target's own floor from independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)." App. D step 5: "If the result exceeds the cert's noise floor on a confirmation run, log a challenge."
- consequence: The acceptance test of App. D, run by two strangers with RTX 4090 / A100 / different CUDA against a floor built on an RTX 4070 Laptop, produces two above-floor challenges on the canary fingerprint and the client computes `disputed` on a cert whose model has not changed. The mechanism that is supposed to give certs weight (§9 "Successful reproductions are how a cert earns weight") instead discredits exactly the most sensitive certs.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED. Options: (a) require the floor to include at least two distinct hardware/driver configurations before a canary cert may carry a public drift claim, and tag single-hardware floors `floor_scope: single-hardware`, with challenges from other hardware classified by clients as `out-of-scope reproduction`, not disputes; (b) define challenge validity against a challenger-side floor: a challenge is valid only if the challenger also submits its own R-run floor on its hardware and d(new, ref) exceeds max(target floor, challenger floor); (c) make the `exact` canary distance first-token or prefix-k only to lower numeric sensitivity, accepting reduced drift power. Whichever is chosen, §8.6 must list cross-hardware numerics as a known limit.
- sources: canary-selection
- verifiers: STANDS: The structural mismatch is in the text: §5.1.1 varies 'physical GPU / driver where available', §9 requires disputes to come from 'distinct hardware', and App. D step 5 tells a stranger to challenge on any above-floor confirmation run, while the only issuer the spec sequences (§14, this box) has one GPU (glimmer REPORT.md setup table, nvidia-smi today), so every lab floor is single-hardware and every valid dispute is cross-hardware by construction. Canary selection maximizes 1−σ(margin) and flip under representational change (§4.3, App. C), so the battery is enriched for items whose greedy path flips under bf16 kernel/driver differences; the author's partial cover, that δ2 batch variation and the R runs already inject numeric noise, is a weaker perturbation than a different CUDA arch and is unmeasured. The author's strongest reading, that §2.2 puts hardware in `subject` and §5.3 voids the floor when S changes so a stranger on other hardware gets exit 2 rather than exit 1, does not rescue the acceptance test: under that reading App. D step 5 can never be reached from other hardware, §9's distinct-hardware dispute rule is unsatisfiable, and 'successful reproductions are how a cert earns weight' is dead for weights subjects; under the other reading two honest strangers mark an unchanged model `disputed`. Either way the App. D acceptance test fails on the spec's most sensitive cert and the fix is a design decision (operator-gated), not wording. Blocker stands; confidence medium because the cross-hardware flip rate on selected canaries has no receipt yet, only a mechanism.

### S7-02 · blocker · §7.2; §7.4
**§7.2 promotion example quotes a depth ΔAUC = 0.041 [0.018, 0.063] that no receipt holds and the committed keystone negative contradicts**

- claim: The example promotion claim string asserts a positive, preregistered depth effect over semantic entropy on TriviaQA/PopQA-rare, Gemma-2-2B; no receipt in the repo holds that number, and the only committed measurement of exactly that hypothesis is a certified CLOSED_NEGATIVE with ΔAUC = 0.0026 [-0.0044, 0.0188].
- evidence: Spec §7.2: "\"claim\": \"Mean SAE-attributed layer adds ΔAUC = 0.041 [0.018, 0.063] over semantic entropy for predicting answer correctness on TriviaQA (ID) and PopQA-rare (OOD), Gemma-2-2B, preregistered.\"". Repo: papers/depth-truth/FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth_2026_07_03.md:4 "Verdict: `CLOSED_NEGATIVE_NO_TRUTH_SIGNAL`"; :32 "H2 additivity (SE) | ΔAUC = **0.0026** | [-0.0044, 0.0188] | ... **NO** (LRT p=1.0, DeLong p=0.708)"; :35 "H3 OOD retention | ΔAUC_ood = **-0.0517** | [-0.1069, -0.0116] ... anti-signal". OATH_CONTRACT.md:100-103 "quoting a number is treated as claiming it". Doctrine (SCOUT.md:27; memory styxx-charter-epistemic-boundaries.md): "Every number cites a receipt; a claim without a receipt is not a claim."
- consequence: The spec, once committed and hashed as entry #0, becomes a lab document asserting a positive depth effect with a fabricated CI on the very cell the lab published as null; under the lab's own OATH verifier the line contains measurement vocabulary ("adds ΔAUC") and would be accused UNGROUNDED. Anyone quoting the spec (or the compliance view copying the example) republishes a false result under the lab's name, and the depth-truth TICKET ("If future copy is written that claims depth flags hallucination, this ticket's finding is the receipt that it must not") is violated by the spec itself.
- amendment: Replace the example claim with a non-numeric placeholder (`"claim": "<EXAMPLE ONLY — not a claim; every numeral in it must resolve to a leaf in the refs'd result cert>"`) or with the real receipted sentence, e.g. "Mean SAE-attributed layer adds no AUROC over semantic entropy for answer correctness on TriviaQA/PopQA-rare, Gemma-2-2B (ΔAUC 0.0026 [-0.0044, 0.0188]); see sha256:<FINDING certificate> — a null; not promotable" citing papers/depth-truth/FINDING_...certificate.json. Add to §7.2: "A promotion `claim` string is valid only if every numeral in it equals a value at a JSON-pointer in the refs'd result cert body; `styxx instrument promote` refuses otherwise. Every number in this spec is illustrative unless it carries a receipt path; the depth example is the committed negative in papers/depth-truth/."
- sources: codebase-reconciliation, doctrine-and-commitments, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Best author's case: §7.2 is an illustrative body with `…` and `<prereg>` placeholders and §7.4 lists depth as lab-tier, so no promotion is being asserted. That does not rescue it: the sentence is not a neutral placeholder but names the exact PREREG_v2 H2 cell (Gemma-2-2B, over SE, TriviaQA ID / PopQA-rare OOD) and asserts a positive CI; a repo-wide grep finds no receipt for 0.041 [0.018, 0.063] (the only 0.041 hits are unrelated knowsay/calib-poison numbers), while papers/depth-truth/FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth_2026_07_03.md line 32 holds H2 ΔAUC 0.0026 [-0.0044, 0.0188] NO, verdict CLOSED_NEGATIVE_NO_TRUTH_SIGNAL, with an OATH-HELD certificate. OATH_CONTRACT.md §5 'quoting a number is treated as claiming it', the DISCHARGED TICKET's 'this ticket's finding is the receipt that it must not', and the spec's own §3 header (binding once hashed as entry #0) make this a fabricated positive on a certified-negative cell inside a document the lab would commit. Fix is one line; severity is about committability, and under the lab's written doctrine it cannot be committed as-is.

### S7-03 · blocker · §7.2; §7.4; §14 (Weeks 1–4, Weeks 8–12); §8.6
**§7.4/§14 schedule 'keystone v2 prereg' as charon entry #2 and a future 'public negative' for an experiment the repo already ran and closed negative**

- claim: The spec's depth row and sequencing describe as future work the exact hypothesis, model, datasets and prereg (PREREG_v2) that were ratified, run and closed CLOSED_NEGATIVE in July 2026; issuing that prereg cert now violates §7.2's own definition ("issued before any confirmatory data exists"), timestamps a preregistration after its result, and re-opens depth without citing the negative.
- evidence: Spec §7.4: "| depth (mean attributed layer) | lab | keystone v2 prereg cert → result → promotion or a public negative |"; §14: "First entries, in order: #0 this spec's hash; #1 monochord prereg v3; #2 keystone v2 prereg."; §14 Weeks 8–12: "Depth: promotion cert if the keystone result supports it; otherwise a public negative result cert and the README changes that follow from it. Both outcomes ship."; §7.2: "prereg — issued before any confirmatory data exists."; §8.6: "Defends against: ... post-hoc hypothesis changes". Repo: papers/depth-truth/PREREG_v2.md:1-5 "# PREREG v2 — the keystone: does depth predict truth? ... FROZEN on commit — ratified by flobi 2026-07-01"; FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth_2026_07_03.md:3-4 "Verdict: `CLOSED_NEGATIVE_NO_TRUTH_SIGNAL`" (H1 AUROC 0.5468 [0.4738, 0.6183], H2 ΔAUC 0.0026 [-0.0044, 0.0188], H3 ΔAUC_ood -0.0517); :7-8 "Pre-registered, frozen, ratified by flobi 2026-07-01 (`PREREG_v2.md`, supersedes v1). The main run completed 2026-07-03"; :80-81 "A richer depth aggregation ... could carry signal the single-token head cannot — that is a *new* prereg, not a rescue of this one."; papers/depth-truth/TICKET_readme_truth_in_advertising_2026_07_03.md:1 "`DISCHARGED (no correction needed)`" (the README follow-up already happened).
- consequence: Entry #2 of the new log would be a prereg cert whose timestamp post-dates the confirmatory data by two months; a stranger who finds papers/depth-truth sees the lab preregistering a hypothesis after its own negative result — the post-hoc-hypothesis defect §8.6 says charon defends against — and concludes the lab backdates preregistrations. Weeks 1–12 spend the only GPU on a hypothesis already falsified under frozen bars, and the eventual 'public negative' would double-issue a result that already has an OATH certificate.
- amendment: Replace the §7.4 depth row with: "depth (mean attributed layer) | lab | CLOSED_NEGATIVE on gemma-2-2b / short-form QA (papers/depth-truth/FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth_2026_07_03.md, sha256 of its certificate); first log entries for depth are a `result` cert of kind `confirmatory` wrapping the committed FINDING and its certificate, refs = sha256 of PREREG_v2.md bytes. Any NEW depth prereg is v3, refs that certificate, states which aggregation changed, and must name a different (model_family, task_family) or a different endpoint." §14: delete "#2 keystone v2 prereg"; entry #2 is either "result cert over the committed keystone negative" or a v3 prereg written fresh. §14 GPU line: OPERATOR-GATED — (a) run a v3 depth prereg with a changed aggregation, or (b) reassign the GPU to canary δ1 sweeps and the plan-of-record measurement.
- sources: codebase-reconciliation, doctrine-and-commitments, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: I checked whether 'keystone v2' could mean a fresh v8-era prereg: papers/PLAN_the_next_level_2026_09_02.md (plan of record) contains zero mentions of keystone, depth or monochord; no 'keystone v2' document exists outside papers/depth-truth; PREREG_v2.md is titled 'the keystone: does depth predict truth?', FROZEN and ratified 2026-07-01, and the FINDING closed it 2026-07-03 with a certificate while the README ticket is DISCHARGED. The spec's Weeks 8-12 line ('public negative result cert and the README changes that follow from it') re-describes the completed July sequence as future work, and §14 would log that prereg as entry #2 with a timestamp two months after its confirmatory data — violating §7.2's own definition ('issued before any confirmatory data exists') and manufacturing the post-hoc-hypothesis defect §8.6 says charon defends against. The FINDING's own line 80-81 says a richer aggregation is 'a *new* prereg, not a rescue', which the spec could have invoked as v3 but did not, and nowhere cites the negative. Blocker stands; the amendment (a `result` cert wrapping the committed negative, any new depth work as v3 with a changed aggregation) is correct and OPERATOR-GATED on the GPU reassignment.

### S8-01 · blocker · §8.1; §8.2; §2; App. D step 2
**Leaf entry encoding is undefined: 'full cert bytes (JCS) + cert id' has no byte layout, and the id is already inside the cert**

- claim: The spec never says what byte string is hashed as the leaf; 'entry = full cert bytes (JCS) + cert id' admits at least three readings (JCS(cert) alone since `id` is an envelope field; JCS bytes concatenated with the id string; a wrapper object), and §8.2's 'one JCS cert per line' suggests the line IS the entry, so two implementers produce different leaf hashes and an inclusion proof from one does not verify in the other.
- evidence: §8.1: "**Entry** = full cert bytes (JCS) + cert id." and "**Leaf hash** = `sha256(0x00 || entry_bytes)`" — `entry_bytes` is never defined. §8.2: "one JCS cert per line". §2 envelope already contains `"id": "sha256:<64 hex>"` inside the cert; §2.1: "`id` = `sha256(JCS(cert with "id" and "sig" removed))`". Nothing says what happens when the appended id and the recomputed id disagree at a mirror (only at append: §2.1 "rejected at log append and at `verify`").
- consequence: Appendix D step 2 ('verify the inclusion proof') is unverifiable by a stranger: a second implementation (the browser verifier the lab already maintains for sworn) cannot agree on leaf hashes with the Python one, and the 8.0 acceptance test ('pass on a fresh mirror clone by someone who isn't flobi') has no defined answer.
- amendment: Replace §8.1 first two bullets with: "Entry bytes = the UTF-8 bytes of the JCS serialization of the complete cert including `id` and `sig`, exactly the bytes stored on one ndjson line, excluding the line terminator. Leaf hash = sha256(0x00 || entry_bytes). The cert id is NOT appended; it is derived from the entry at verification time and a mirror MUST reject (status TAMPER) any entry whose embedded `id` does not recompute per §2.1."
- sources: transparency-log, crypto-envelope, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: §8.1 reads 'Entry = full cert bytes (JCS) + cert id' and hashes `entry_bytes` never defined; §8.2 says the stored line is 'one JCS cert per line'; §2 already embeds `id` in the cert. The text gives two literal readings — JCS(cert) alone, or JCS(cert) with the id appended (with what separator, and hex or `sha256:` form?) — and the repo's only in-tree precedent, styxx/transparency.py, uses a third scheme (string tag `styxx-tlog-leaf:` over a hex digest), while the untracked styxx/v8/merkle.py takes opaque bytes and INTERFACES says hashing an entry 'is the caller's job', so nothing in code resolves it either. Author's best reading ('+ cert id' is descriptive, the line is the entry) is plausible but a stranger cannot know it, and this is the root of every inclusion and consistency proof — App. D step 2 and the §14 tag criterion ('four log verify-* commands pass on a fresh mirror clone') have no defined answer across two implementations, which the lab's own doctrine (JS verifier at 1689/1689 vector parity) treats as the standard. Stands at blocker; the fix is two sentences but the spec is what gets frozen as entry #0.

### S8-06 · blocker · §8.1; §14; §2
**'#0 this spec's hash' is undefined: no cert type can hold a document, and the byte string differs Windows/Linux**

- claim: Charon entries are 'full cert bytes (JCS) + cert id' of one of eight types; a markdown spec is none of them, and 'its hash' over a CRLF working tree (this box) differs from the LF git blob (Linux CI), the exact E15 defect charon v0.1 already paid for.
- evidence: §8.1: "**Entry** = full cert bytes (JCS) + cert id." §2: "\"type\": \"fingerprint | battery | eval | prereg | result | promotion | action | challenge\"". §14: "#0 this spec's hash". `papers/charon/SPEC_charon_v01_2026_09_02.md:226-227`: "E15 — every digest Charon computes over WORKING-TREE bytes is content identity modulo newlines. A raw hash of a document or a receipt read from a checkout differs between a Windows". SCOUT.md: "Repo is CRLF on Windows ... a raw hash of working-tree bytes differs Windows vs Linux".
- consequence: Entry #0 cannot be appended at all as written (schema validation rejects it), or is appended as an ad-hoc object whose hash a Linux mirror recomputes differently — the first entry of the transparency log fails Appendix D step 1.
- amendment: Define in §8.1: "A document entry is a `result` cert with `body.kind = "document"` and `body = {path, commit, git_blob_sha256, eol: "lf"}`; the hash is the git blob (LF) never the checkout." Add `document` to the `result.kind` enum in §7.2.
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: §8.1 line 238 defines an entry as 'full cert bytes (JCS) + cert id', §2 line 32 enumerates eight types, §7.2 line 213 fixes result.kind to {confirmatory, pilot, robustness, verify, response}, and §8.3 has CI schema-validate every append — a markdown spec's hash fits none of them. The only author-side candidate is an action cert with action_kind 'commit' (§10), but action certs require a model-fingerprint ref, live in the hourly sub-log, and are a weeks 8-12 deliverable, so it cannot be entry #0 in week 1. On bytes: the spec never says blob vs checkout; .gitattributes shows the repo is CRLF-sensitive with byte-pinning only for listed sworn files, and papers/charon/SPEC_charon_v01 E15 records that the lab already paid for a working-tree hash differing Windows vs Linux. As written the log's first entry and the spec's own binding clause are unimplementable per the spec's schema, so an implementer must invent an ad-hoc object — exactly the failure class the lab has receipts for. Blocker stands; the fix is a short document-kind definition.

### S9-01 · blocker · §9; §2.3
**Challenge validity never checks subject match — a q4 run can dispute a bf16 cert**

- claim: Recipe match is defined over battery/decoding/template/system-prompt only; subject (weights hash, precision, provider alias) is not in the comparability set, so a challenger's fingerprint of a different model or precision is a valid challenge as written.
- evidence: §2.3: "Two certs are **comparable** iff `battery`, `decoding`, `chat_template_sha256`, and `system_prompt_sha256` match exactly." §9: "A challenge whose fingerprint cert fails the recipe-match check is `invalid` … No recipe match, no challenge." The challenge body has `"recipe_match": true` and `"environment": { "hardware": "…", "runtime": "…" }` — no subject field. §2.2 puts `precision` in `subject`, not `recipe`.
- consequence: Steps: (1) target = fathom's bf16 gemma-2-2b fingerprint; (2) challenger fingerprints q4_k_m of the same repo with the identical recipe (recipe_match = true); (3) `exact` distance is far above the bf16 floor; (4) repeat from a second key → target is Disputed. Conversely, a hostile issuer 'reproduces' its own cert from a second key on a different model with a coincidentally low distance. Both statuses are wrong.
- amendment: §9 rule 1 becomes: "A challenge is valid iff the challenger's fingerprint is comparable (§2.3) AND has the same subject identity: for weights subjects `hf_repo`, `revision`, `weights_sha256`, `config_sha256`, `tokenizer_sha256`, `precision` equal; for alias subjects `provider` and `alias` equal. `hardware`, `runtime`, `region`, `observed_at` may differ and are what a challenge is for." Clients compute and display the subject-match result beside `recipe_match`.
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: §2.3 line 80 defines comparability over battery, decoding, chat_template_sha256, system_prompt_sha256 only; §2.2 line 55 places precision (and weights/config/tokenizer hashes) in subject; §9 line 278 makes recipe match the sole validity test and the challenge body (lines 270-275) carries no subject field, while Disputed (line 280) is client-computed from those challenges. Strongest author defenses: 'dispute a cert by reproducing it', Appendix D step 3 ('obtain the subject'), and `verify --ref <cert>` having no --subject flag imply same-subject intent; but `styxx log challenge <target> <own>` (line 305) accepts any fingerprint the challenger produced with any --subject, and anchors do not rescue it because §4.4(5) selects anchors as items with zero flips under every δ including the q4_k_m sweep, so a q4 challenger passes the identity check by construction. §5.3 ('floors are per (S, B, recipe)') shows the spec itself knows the target floor is meaningless for a different S, which makes §9 an internal contradiction that yields a wrong Disputed status and a spoofable reproduction. Blocker stands; the one-rule amendment is correct.

### S0-01 · major · §0 header; §0 non-goals; §3 header line; §7.2; §8; §14
**'charon entry #0' and 'Charon v0' overwrite a shipped, sworn, attacked charon v0.1 (243-line ferry log) with a different object under the same name and no migration**

- evidence: Spec line 3: "becomes binding when committed and its hash is logged as charon entry #0"; §0 "Charon v0 is a git repo"; §8 "Follows RFC 6962 ... with two additions"; §8.3 "Append = PR; CI validates schema, recomputes id, checks signature, appends, publishes STH."; §7.2 "that is charon's first real use"; §14 "Charon v0: git-backed log, Merkle tree, STH ... Timestamped preregistration is the first real use of the log." Repo: styxx/charon.py:1-2 "the ferry log: every verdict this lab issued, re-derived from bytes, chained"; styxx/charon.py:99 LOG_SCHEMA = "styxx.charon/log/v1"; styxx/charon.py:409-420 `"""Dispatch on the artifact's suffix. Anything else is refused by name."""` (.sworn.json / .capsule.html / .certificate.json only); styxx/charon.py:533 `bad.append("seq is not dense (expected %d)" % i)`; papers/charon/charon.log.jsonl = 244 lines (header + 243 entries), line 2 "seq": 1, head 1647a26abadb35d0 (`python -m styxx.charon status`); papers/charon/RESULT_charon_v01_ships_2026_09_02.md:6-7 "**This document is itself sworn**", :22-24 "Its head is `1647a26abadb…` ... It carries 243 lines" (26 HELD spans); papers/charon/SPEC_charon_v01_2026_09_02.md ERRATA: "the v0 log written before the pass stays in history at `a5cf9ec`" (so "v0" is already taken); ATTACKS_charon_v01_battery:106-108 records the name collision was to be 'recorded rather than owed' and kills "a line cannot be removed ... without the chain saying so". Memory charon-v01-ferry-log.md: "Charon reproduces, never adjudicates", statuses SAME_LINE / MOVED_VERIFIER / SKEW / DRIFT / UNRESOLVED / HEAD_MISMATCH / TAMPER, "the chain binds order and content **to the HEAD**"; a-receipt-is-history-too.md: "Never regenerate a committed receipt in place."
- consequence: Two incompatible artifacts and two `charon` modules under one name inside one package; a reader holding the sworn RESULT (head 1647a26…, 243 lines) and a 'charon' whose entry #0 is a spec hash has irreconcilable objects; under v0.1's own rule a line inserted before seq 1 is TAMPER; the seven v0.1 statuses and the re-derived-from-bytes rule (not merely signature-checked) silently disappear; `styxx log` semantics are undefined for the old log; every memory/doc sentence about 'charon' becomes ambiguous; and the spec's binding event depends on a log that does not exist until the code the spec governs is written.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED: (a) rename the v8 log (any name not `charon`, e.g. 'the ledger' or 'charon v2 — a Merkle log, not the ferry log') and add §8.0: "styxx.charon v0.1 (`styxx/charon.py`, `papers/charon/charon.log.jsonl`, head 1647a26…) is history and is not rebuilt, renumbered or reinterpreted by this spec; its 243 lines stay under their own schema; its head is pinned as a sworn span and becomes the FIRST entry of the new log; new-log `result` kind `verify` entries MAY be ingested into the ferry log as a fourth artifact kind in a later charon version"; or (b) number the v8 log charon v1.0 with an explicit migration section: the v0.1 log is frozen at head 1647a26…, its file/HEAD is pinned by an early v1 entry, the seven v0.1 statuses map to named v8 verdicts, v0.1 lines are appended as `attachment` certs with `role: charon_v01_line`, append keeps re-derivation (§8.3 must say 'CI re-derives body from recipe where the cert type allows it', not only 'checks signature'), and "Charon v0 is a git repo" is reworded because v0 names a superseded log at a5cf9ec. In both options: bind this spec today by its own commit and a v0.1 line of kind `sworn` over the spec's sidecar, not by a future entry #0 (the spec's hash is entry #1 or the header, never #0); delete both occurrences of "first real use" — the lab has timestamped preregistrations by commit since PREREG_v2 (`6af88ed`) and the EXTERNAL-1 sealed key digest.
- sources: codebase-reconciliation, doctrine-and-commitments, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Checked: styxx/charon.py:2 is 'the ferry log ... re-derived from bytes, chained', derive() refuses anything but .sworn.json/.capsule.html/.certificate.json, check_chain demands dense seq from 1, `python -m styxx.charon status --log papers/charon/charon.log.jsonl` prints '243 lines, head 1647a26abadb35d0, chain ok', RESULT_charon_v01_ships is sworn over that head, and SPEC ERRATA line 169 says the v0 log stays in history at a5cf9ec. The strongest author-side reading is that 'Charon v0' is the spec's own tier label (§8.2 'Storage v0 (weeks 1-4)', §8.3 'v0: fathom's key only / v1: open submission'), that §8.2 lays out a NEW directory (charon/entries/*.ndjson) rather than the v0.1 file, and that the Merkle code already exists as styxx/transparency.py ('RFC 6962 for attestations') so the spec is consolidating two modules under one name, not corrupting the v0.1 log; on that reading the 'insert before seq 1 is TAMPER' and 'unimplementable in the shipped charon' consequences do not follow, and the ATTACKS name-collision paragraph is about external projects, not this one. What survives: the spec never mentions v0.1, reuses a version label the ERRATA already assigns to a superseded log, gives no migration for the seven v0.1 statuses or the re-derivation rule, and says twice that preregistration is 'the first real use of the log' against 243 shipped lines and commit-timestamped prereg history (PREREG_v2 at 6af88ed). That is a contradiction with committed history plus two false sentences; no verdict or number in the spec comes out wrong and Appendix D does not fail, so major, agreeing with the first verifier.

### S0-03 · major · §0; §7.2; §12; Appendix D
**Sworn v0.2 — the lab's current claim-carrying format on 38 documents — is never mentioned; "every claim is a cert" leaves the promotion `claim` string as receipt-less prose, and the spec itself would be UNSWORN**

- claim: The spec does not state whether sworn spans are superseded, wrapped, or parallel; its `claim` string carries numbers with no leaf pointer, so Appendix D gives a stranger no step that checks the sentence against the result cert; and the spec (0 sworn spans) is to be committed as the binding document in a papers/ tree where every SPEC/RESULT/PLAN is sworn with a sidecar and receipt.
- evidence: Spec §0: "every claim is a cert, and every cert is reproducible by a stranger from its own recipe. Anything that can't be reproduced from the cert is not a claim; it's a note."; §7.2: "The `claim` string is scope-limited and is the **only** sentence the compliance view may quote"; §12: "Every sentence in the view either quotes a promotion `claim` string verbatim or ...". Word "sworn" occurs 0 times in the spec; `grep -c "<sworn"` on the draft = 0. Repo: `git ls-files '*.sworn.json'` = 38 (PLAN_the_next_level, RESULT_charon_v01_ships, every RESULT_* since 2026-09-01); styxx/sworn.py:25-27 "THE INVARIANT. *The author chooses what to swear; the author cannot choose what the receipt says.* ... Everything outside a span is NARRATIVE by definition"; :121 `VERDICTS = ("HELD", "FAILED", "UNRESOLVED", "MALFORMED", "WITHHELD")`; memory sworn-v02-and-the-plan-2026-09-02.md: "Sworn spans are the lab's CURRENT claim-carrying format".
- consequence: The compliance view quotes a sentence no verifier binds to a number; a promotion cert can carry a claim string whose CI does not match its own result body and pass every check in Appendix D; the lab's plan of record (sworn, four legs, leg 2 waiting on signature) is displaced without being named.
- amendment: Add §2.4 "Relation to sworn": "A `result` or `promotion` body's `claim` is a sworn document fragment: every numeral in it is a `<sworn r="sha256:<result cert id>#/body/<pointer>" k="numeric">` span resolved by styxx.sworn against the refs'd cert; `styxx instrument promote` refuses a claim with an unsworn numeral. Sworn v0.2 is not superseded; a cert is a receipt kind sworn spans may name (`r="cert:sha256:…#/pointer"`)." Add to line 3: "this spec is committed as a sworn document (sidecar + receipt) and its `.sworn.json` is what the log hashes"; add Appendix D step 6: "resolve every sworn span in the claim string against the refs'd cert".
- sources: codebase-reconciliation
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed: 'sworn' occurs zero times in the spec; git ls-files '*.sworn.json' returns 38; styxx/sworn.py:25-27 carries the invariant and VERDICTS as quoted. One citation is misattributed — the sentence 'Sworn spans are the lab's CURRENT claim-carrying format' is SCOUT.md line 12, not the memory file, though the memory file does record sworn v0.2 and PLAN_the_next_level as the plan of record. The material gap is real: §7.2 makes the promotion claim string the only quotable sentence and §12 quotes it verbatim, but nothing in §2, §7.2 or Appendix D binds the numerals in that string to the refs'd result body; a promotion whose claim contradicts its own result cert passes every check listed. That is an exploitable hole in the spec's own 'every claim is a cert' rule. Major.

(`grep -rn stacc` hits only `CHANGELOG.md:959` "The not-stacc fix".)

PREREG_v2 §6') and 'stacc' is recorded (CHANGELOG.md:959 'The not-stacc fix', tests/test_unmeasured_not_adversarial.py), so those two terms are resolvable by grep. But 'monochord', 'gold fix' and 'monday/tuesday' return nothing anywhere in the worktree, and the spec defines none of them. §4/§14 make fixed-v1 depend on 'styxx-bench after the gold fix', so an implementer cannot construct the public battery, and §14 names log entries #1/#2 by undefined documents. Stays major; the amendment should cite PREREG_v2 and the not-stacc changelog entry rather than remove those two terms.

### S1-01 · major · §1.1; §2; §2.1
**`created` and `issuer` sit inside the id preimage, so "same evidence, same id, anywhere" (§1.1) is false and contradicts every existing lab content address**

- claim: §2.1 removes only `id` and `sig` before hashing, leaving `created` (and `issuer`) in the canonical bytes; §1.1 promises "Same evidence, same id, anywhere", which is false whenever identical subject+recipe+body is issued at two times or by two issuers. Every content-addressed artifact the lab already ships keeps timestamps outside the digest.
- evidence: §1.1: "**Content-addressed.** A cert's id is the sha256 of its canonical bytes. Same evidence, same id, anywhere." §2.1: "`id` = `sha256(JCS(cert with \"id\" and \"sig\" removed))`". §2 envelope: `"created": "2026-09-07T18:00:00Z"` and `issuer` remain in the digested object. Repo: styxx/attestation.py:194-195 (`_canonical_payload` drops `generated_at`, comment: "Two attest() runs on an identical substrate therefore produce the same digest"); styxx/attestation.py:294 `core = {k: v for k, v in artifact.items() if k not in ("generated_at", "digest")}`; styxx/charon.py:61 "Timestamps are outside every digest"; styxx/charon.py:94 `_OUTSIDE_DIGEST = ("entry_id", "timestamp", "note")`; sworn verdict receipt digests its core without `timestamp` (papers/charon/RESULT_charon_v01_ships_2026_09_02.sworn-receipt.json keys `digest`, `timestamp` separate; styxx/sworn.py R9).
- consequence: A reproduction (§9: "A challenge that lands *below* the target's floor is a reproduction"; "Successful reproductions are how a cert earns weight") of the same subject+recipe+body by a second lab, or the same lab a second later, can never share the target's id, so nothing in the log links equal evidence except free-text `refs`; dedup of an identical fingerprint re-signed later is impossible and the log fills with distinct ids for one evidence set; App. D step 1 recomputes an id that encodes who and when rather than what.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED: (a) keep §2.1 and rewrite §1.1 to "Same bytes, same id" (drop the evidence claim); or (b) define two addresses: `evidence_id = sha256(JCS({styxx, type, subject, recipe, body}))` and `id = sha256(JCS(cert minus id, sig))`; `refs` and `selected_against` point at `id`, reproduction/dedup compare `evidence_id`; or (c) mirror the lab rule verbatim — §2.1: "`id` = sha256(JCS(cert with `id`, `sig` and `created` removed)); `created` is disclosed as unsealed metadata; the log's STH timestamp is the only proof of time" (§7.2 already calls the log timestamp "the proof of order") — and optionally also drop `issuer` from the preimage, signing the full envelope; if `issuer` stays in the digest under (c), cross-issuer ids remain distinct and §1.1 must be reworded to "Same bytes, same id" as well.
- sources: crypto-envelope, codebase-reconciliation, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Checked: §2.1 removes only `id` and `sig`, so `created` and `issuer` are in the preimage; §1.1 says 'Same evidence, same id, anywhere'; styxx/attestation.py:189-195 and :294 drop `generated_at` from the digest with the comment that identical substrates must produce the same digest, and styxx/charon.py:61 says 'Timestamps are outside every digest'. Author's side: including the timestamp in the leaf is exactly what RFC 6962 does, §5.1.4 needs byte-identical noise-floor runs to remain distinct certs (dropping `created` would collapse them, as the reviewer's own caution notes), and §9 links reproductions by `refs` ids rather than free text, so the dedup consequence is overstated and the mechanism may be deliberate. What survives is that §1.1 is false as written and the spec silently departs from the lab's standing digest rule without saying so; no verdict, number, or Appendix D step is wrong. Major, agreeing with the first verifier; fix is rewording §1.1 to 'same bytes, same id' or adding an evidence_id.

### S1-02 · major · §1.5; §8.1; §8.2; §8.4; §15.5; §14
**§8 re-promises the sentence the charon battery killed: append-only is only checkable against a head pinned outside the log**

- claim: Consistency proofs prove extension only relative to an STH the reader obtained elsewhere; the spec stores STHs in the same git repo, signs them with the log operator's own key, defers anchoring until a mirror exists, and places the first non-operator mirror at the 8.0 tag — so for the whole build the 'append-only' claim has exactly the defect the v0.1 red team found.
- evidence: Spec §8.1: "**Consistency proof** — proves STH(n) is a prefix-extension of STH(m), m < n. This is what makes "append-only" checkable rather than promised."; §8.2 "sth/2026-09-07T18.json  # signed tree heads" inside the same repo; §8.3 "v0: fathom's key only"; §15.5 "**Anchoring.** Skip until a mirror exists."; §14 "8.0 tagged when the four `log verify-*` commands pass on a fresh mirror clone by someone who isn't flobi." ATTACKS_charon_v01_battery:16-24: "**the chain binds order and content to the head** ... `--expect-head` is the whole defence and it depends on a reader having been given a head by some other channel"; :73-76 "A forger who rebuilds the whole chain produces an honest-looking log. Only a head pinned outside the log catches it. ... Every prefix of a log is a valid log." Memory charon-v01-ferry-log: "do not let any of them come back".
- consequence: The operator (or anyone with the log key) rewrites entries/*.ndjson, re-signs every STH, force-pushes; every inclusion and consistency proof still verifies; a stranger following Appendix D step 2 sees a clean log. The spec states 'append-only, publicly auditable' (§1.5) as a property when it is, in v0, a promise.
- amendment: §8.1 add: "A consistency proof establishes append-only only between two STHs the reader obtained from independent channels. With one operator key and no external pin, the log establishes internal consistency and nothing more, and `styxx log verify-*` prints that sentence." Require, before the word 'append-only' appears in any output: an STH pinned outside the repo (a sworn span in another committed document, a second-party mirror record, or an anchor). §15.5 → OPERATOR-GATED: anchor from the first STH (cost is a fee) versus wait for a mirror; note that until either exists v0 is 'a signed list'.
- sources: doctrine-and-commitments
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §8.1 says the consistency proof "is what makes 'append-only' checkable rather than promised", §8.2 keeps STHs in the same repo, §8.3 has one operator key, §15.5 defers anchoring, and §14 tags 8.0 on a fresh mirror clone with no prior STH. ATTACKS_charon_v01_battery lines 16-24 and 73-76 say exactly what is cited ("Only a head pinned outside the log catches it"; "Every prefix of a log is a valid log"), and the charon memory says "do not let any of them come back". §8.4 (mirrors publishing conflicting STHs) and §8.6 (operator misbehavior only "mitigated") partially acknowledge the limit, which is why this is major rather than blocker, but neither states that a consistency proof establishes append-only only between STHs obtained by independent channels, and §1.5 asserts the property outright. An implementer would ship a `verify-consistency` that prints append-only for two STHs pulled from the same repo — the retired sentence, re-promised. The ed25519 STH signature does raise the bar above v0.1's unsigned chain (the forger needs the log key), but the operator is precisely the party §8.6 admits is not prevented.

### S10-03 · major · §10; §11
**`cogn_audit_on_send` is a library pre-send hook over text drafts running four cognometric instruments; `agent emit` action certs carry none of them**

- claim: The function audits (and optionally revises) an agent's outbound draft with sycophancy/deception/overconfidence/refusal and writes a trajectory JSONL that styxx.credits and the register-corpus extractor consume; the §10 action cert instead refs a model fingerprint and shows `readings.lab.depth/lens` (white-box, weights-subject channels), so `agent emit` is not a successor and the four instruments it does run are absent from the §7.4 registry entirely.

Repo: styxx/middleware.py:119-131 `def cogn_audit_on_send(prompt: str, draft: str, *, llm_revise: Optional[ReviseFn] = None, max_revise: int = 3, ... log_path ...) -> Tuple[str, AuditTrajectory]`; :3-9 "cognometric self-audit for agent send-paths ... audits each draft via ``styxx.preflight``, optionally calls the host's revise function"; styxx/cognometrics.py:59 `COGN_INSTRUMENTS = ["sycophancy", "deception", "overconfidence", "refusal"]`; styxx/credits.py:27 "The trajectory JSONL that ``styxx.cogn_audit_on_send(log_path=...)`` already"; styxx/__init__.py:533 public export.
- consequence: darkflobi's actual send-path audit (the F10 reflex loop) has no place in v8; the 'glass-box darkflobi' demo would emit certs about SAE depth of a model it calls over an API alias, where white-box channels are by §3.2 unavailable.
- amendment: §10: "Action certs are additive to `cogn_audit_on_send`, which is retained. `readings` MUST list only instruments in the registry applicable to the subject kind; for `alias` subjects white-box readings (`depth`, `lens`, `resid`) are absent, never zero. The four cognometric instruments enter the registry as lab tier with `code_sha256` from styxx/cognometrics.py." Change the §11 row to "retained; `agent emit` wraps its AuditTrajectory as `body.readings.lab.cognometrics`".
- sources: codebase-reconciliation
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate and repo-confirmed: styxx/middleware.py:1-10 and :119-131 define `cogn_audit_on_send(prompt, draft, *, llm_revise, max_revise, ..., log_path)` as the F10 reflex-loop pre-send audit over text drafts; styxx/cognometrics.py:59 lists the four instruments; styxx/credits.py:27 consumes its trajectory JSONL; styxx/__init__.py:533 exports it publicly. §11 lists it under "Removed, with one-minor-version shims that print the replacement" mapped to `agent emit`, whose §10 body carries model-fingerprint readings (`depth`, `lens`) and no draft audit at all, and §7.4's registry neither registers nor retires the four cognometric instruments (glimmer is explicitly retired; these are silently dropped). An implementer following §11 would shim away a public API and break styxx.credits and the register-corpus extractor with no successor for the function — a real contradiction, hence major. The alias/white-box point is a secondary inference about darkflobi's deployment rather than spec text, but §3.2 does make `depth`/`lens` unavailable for alias subjects, so the example is misleading for the stated reference deployment.

The finding's memory citations (x-posting, apex-agent-extraction) do not state the model; the correct source is the uptime memory, but the fact stands. Nothing in §2 or §10 defines the genesis action's refs; §14 mandates schema validation for all eight types, so the first-action shape is a real gap, though an implementer would likely allow a one-element refs.

### S11-01 · major · §11; §7.1; §8
**v8 verbs `fingerprint`, `attest`, `log` collide with live 7.x subcommands of different meaning; the removal table covers 4 of 47 top-level commands**

- claim: `styxx fingerprint` today prints a cognitive-identity vector aggregated from the audit log (and `fingerprint compare <session_a> <session_b>`); `styxx attest <file>` mints a Verifiable Cognometric Attestation checked by `verify-attestation`; `styxx log` is the audit-log namespace owning `tail/stats/timeline/session/clear/rotate/migrate-provenance`, including a destructive `clear`.
