The spec assigns each name a different object (model fingerprint cert, compliance view, append-only transparency log `append | prove | mirror | sth`) with no rule for which namespace wins, none of these is in the shim table, and a 'one-minor-version shim that prints the replacement' cannot occupy a name that is simultaneously live with new semantics; 43 of 47 top-level commands have no stated disposition.
- evidence: Spec §11: "styxx fingerprint --subject <spec> --battery ...", "styxx attest --subject <spec> --view research|compliance", "styxx log          append <cert> | prove <id> | verify-inclusion <id> <sth> | verify-consistency <sth1> <sth2> | sth | mirror | challenge <target> <own>", "Removed, with one-minor-version shims that print the replacement" (table of 4 rows). Repo: styxx/cli.py:3203-3206 `p_fp = sub.add_parser("fingerprint", help="print or compare cognitive identity fingerprints")`, :3218-3222 `fp_sub.add_parser("compare", ...)` positional `session_a`, `session_b`; :902-909 `def cmd_attest(args): """styxx attest <file> — emit a Verifiable Cognometric Attestation ..."""`; :3231 `p_log = sub.add_parser("log", help="audit log operations")`; :3259 `log_sub.add_parser("clear", help="delete the audit log (0.1.0a4)")`; :3262 `rotate`; :3265 `migrate-provenance`; enumeration of `sub.add_parser` = 47 top-level names (init, audit-claims, …, feed) plus 9 nested = 56. Also styxx/attack/fingerprint.py:3 "cross-instrument cognometric signatures" and benchmarks/cognometry_fingerprint_atlas_v0.json reuse the word for an N-vector of instrument scores.
- consequence: A user running the documented `styxx log clear` on a machine holding a charon mirror deletes the wrong thing, or the 'append-only' log gains a destructive namespace sibling; `styxx fingerprint` with no flags silently means a different artifact to 7.x users and 7.x docs; users of `styxx log tail` and `styxx fingerprint compare` break with no shim; the 7.x→8.0 table omits seven existing `log` subcommands; 'CLI surface (v8)' is not a surface.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED: either (a) rename the v8 verbs so 7.x names keep their meaning until removed — `styxx cert fingerprint`, `styxx cert verify`, `styxx charon ...` for the transparency log (matching `styxx/charon.py` and the papers), `styxx view compliance`; 7.x `fingerprint` → `cogn fingerprint` (or removed, shim); `log tail|stats|…` → `audit …` — or (b) put v8 under a new console script (`styxx8`) for one minor version. In either case add Appendix E: all 47 top-level 7.x subcommands (and every existing `log` subcommand) with one of {kept, shim→X, removed-no-replacement}.
- sources: codebase-reconciliation, internal-consistency, feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Verified in styxx/cli.py: fingerprint (3203-3229) prints/compares audit-log cognitive-identity vectors; attest (902+) mints a Verifiable Cognometric Attestation; log (3231-3267) is the audit-log namespace with tail/stats/timeline/session/clear/rotate/migrate-provenance. Count is 47 top-level + 9 nested = 56 parsers. §11 reassigns all three names to different objects (model fingerprint cert, compliance/research view, RFC 6962 transparency log) and the only disposition mechanism offered is a shim that prints the replacement, which cannot coexist with a live same-named command carrying new semantics; no passage in the spec gives a disposition for the other 43 top-level commands or the seven log subcommands. The consequence that a 7.x 'log clear' deletes a charon mirror is speculative (the v8 log has no clear verb), but the silent semantic swap and unshimmed breakage are a real contradiction of the 'Removed, with shims' claim.

### S11-02 · major · §11; §14 weeks 1–4
**`ci-baseline`/`ci-test` are cognometric audit-log regression checks; `fingerprint`/`verify` do not replace them**

- claim: Today `ci-test` runs `sla.check_health` over the last N audit-log entries (gate pass rate, confidence, warn rate) and `ci-baseline` snapshots pass rate / mean confidence / vulnerability counts from `load_audit`; neither runs a model, a battery or a noise floor, so the row asserts an equivalence that does not hold, deletes the package's only cognometric CI surface, and leaves the exported `styxx.regression_test` Python API undocumented.
- evidence: Spec §11: "| `ci-baseline` / `ci-test` | `fingerprint` / `verify` + exit codes |". Repo: styxx/cli.py:247-262 `def cmd_ci_test(args): ... from .sla import check_health; report = check_health(min_pass_rate=..., min_confidence=..., max_warn_rate=..., window=...)`; styxx/cli.py:358-375 `def cmd_ci_baseline(args): from .ci import Baseline; from .analytics import load_audit; entries = load_audit(last_n=50) ... gates = [e.get("gate") or "pending" ...]`; styxx/ci.py:3 "styxx.ci — cognitive regression testing for CI/CD pipelines" and :20-26 "Fails the build if: pass rate drops below threshold / new critical vulnerabilities appear / confidence regresses vs baseline"; styxx/__init__.py:378 exports `regression_test, create_baseline`; documented at docs/REFERENCE.md:504-505; PyPI 3,164 downloads last month (pypistats).
- consequence: A 7.x user with `styxx ci-test --min-pass 0.8` in a pipeline gets a shim telling them to run `verify`/`fingerprint`, which needs a fingerprint cert of a model whose weights they may not have; their cognometric regression check silently ends.
- amendment: Split the row: keep `ci-baseline`/`ci-test` in 8.0 unchanged (no shim), mark deprecated in help text, and state the Python API `styxx.regression_test` is untouched until 8.1 — or move the cognometric regression check to the `instrument` registry as a lab-tier reading over the audit log. Do not claim `fingerprint`/`verify` replace them.
- sources: codebase-reconciliation, feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: styxx/cli.py cmd_ci_test (247-262) runs sla.check_health over the last N audit entries and cmd_ci_baseline (358-375) snapshots load_audit(last_n=50); styxx/ci.py header describes cognitive regression testing over gate pass rate, confidence and vulnerabilities. Neither runs a battery, a model, or a noise floor, so the §11 row equating them with fingerprint/verify + exit codes asserts an equivalence that does not hold; nothing else in the spec (§6 exit codes, §7) covers audit-log regression checks. styxx/__init__.py:378 exports regression_test/create_baseline and docs/REFERENCE.md:504-505 documents the CLI, so real users lose their only cognometric CI gate with a shim pointing at an unrelated tool. The PyPI download figure was not verified but is not load-bearing.

### S12-01 · major · §12 constraints; §13 items 5-6
**Docket summary rule 'every sentence ends with the cert id it came from' cannot be satisfied for aggregating sentences, and the summary and instrument readings have no cert type to be logged as**

- claim: Predecessor-diff and quant-delta sentences necessarily draw on two fingerprint certs plus a verify result (and two batteries → two results), so no single trailing id is 'the cert it came from'; §13 says all contents are certs in charon but `summary` and 'instrument readings' match none of the eight types or the five result kinds.
- evidence: §13.6: "every sentence ends with the cert id it came from. If a sentence can't cite a cert, it doesn't ship."; §13: "Contents, all as certs in charon"; §13.3: "`verify --diff` bf16 vs q4_k_m on both batteries"; §2 types: "fingerprint | battery | eval | prereg | result | promotion | action | challenge"; §7.2 result kinds: "confirmatory, pilot, robustness, verify, response"; §12: "Every sentence in the view either quotes a promotion `claim` string verbatim or is a neutral description of a cert".
- consequence: Either the generator emits sentences that end with one id while depending on three (a stranger checking the cited cert finds numbers that are not in it), or every comparative sentence is dropped and the docket summary contains no diff at all; the summary itself is unloggable as written so the docket is not 'all certs'.
- amendment: §13.6: "every sentence ends with the ids of every cert whose fields it uses, in `refs` order; a sentence that states a number uses only fields of a single `result` cert (the verify result), and ends with that id; if no logged result cert holds the number, the sentence does not ship." Add `result.kind = summary` (body: `sentences: [{text, refs}]`, `template_sha256`) so item 6 is a logged cert, and add `result.kind = reading` for item 5 (body: instrument id, version, code_sha256, tier as computed, value, and the promotion id or `null`). Replace "no adjectives" with an enforceable rule: sentences are generated only from a fixed template set whose sha256 is in the summary cert.
- sources: stranger-reproducibility
- verifiers: STANDS: Half of this finding fails: a predecessor-diff or quant-delta sentence does have a single owning cert, because §6 says `verify --diff` 'Emits a `result` cert, `body.kind = "verify"`, with per-channel {distance, floor, ratio, verdict}' that refs both fingerprints, so a sentence stating those numbers ends with that verify-result id and a stranger opening it finds the numbers (two batteries simply yield two result certs and two sentences). The other half stands: §13 opens with 'Contents, all as certs in charon' and lists items 5 (instrument readings) and 6 (summary), yet §2 fixes 'One schema, eight types', §7.2 fixes `kind ∈ {confirmatory, pilot, robustness, verify, response}`, §11 `instrument run <id>` names no output cert, and §14 week 1 builds schema validation for exactly eight types — so the summary and non-agent instrument readings cannot be logged without the implementer inventing a type or kind, which is a verdict-vocabulary/schema decision the lab treats as operator-gated. That is a contradiction inside the spec, so major stands, but the amendment's first sentence (multi-id suffixes) is unnecessary; only the new `result` kinds are needed.

### S13-01 · major · §13; §14 weeks 4–8; §2.2
**Generalization dockets on gemma-2-9b / llama-3.1-8b are infeasible on this box: 8 GB VRAM, 2.7 GB free disk, ~50x over the 48-hour budget**

- claim: The only GPU is an RTX 4070 Laptop (8,188 MiB); bf16 gemma-2-9b (~18.5 GB) and llama-3.1-8b (~16 GB) cannot be resident, neither model is in the HF cache, and the disk has 2.7 GB free — so §13.1 'bf16, R = 8' on those models is impossible, and CPU-offloaded generation (~1 tok/s over laptop PCIe) puts the fixed-v1 noise floor alone at 512 items x 8 runs x 64 tokens ≈ 262k tokens ≈ 70 h, before canary selection.
- evidence: `nvidia-smi`: "NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4070 Laptop GPU, 8188 MiB"; `df -h`: "930G 928G 2.7G 100% /c"; HF cache lists `models--google--gemma-2-2b` but no gemma-2-9b or Llama-3.1-8B. §13: "1. **fingerprint** on fixed-v1 (bf16, R = 8 noise floor)" and "The generalization runs in the 90-day plan (gemma-2-9b, llama-3.1-8b, one more family) *are* dockets 1–3." §2.2 example: "\"hardware\": { \"gpu\": \"RTX 4090\" }" — the lab does not own one. `experiments/glimmer_dayzero_2026_08_10/REPORT.md:18`: "RTX 4070 Laptop 8GB + 31.5GB RAM, 18/── layers offloaded, ~2.5 tok/s".
- consequence: Weeks 4–8 'Dockets 1–3 on the generalization models' cannot complete; the 48-hour trigger is missed by weeks; the box (documented OOM-prone, 'never two heavy runs at once' in `styxx-calibration-leg-2026-09-05.md`) will crash mid-floor and the R=8 null is never produced.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED: (a) restrict v8.0 dockets to models that fit 8 GB in bf16 (gemma-2-2b, Llama-3.2-3B, Qwen2.5-3B — all cached) and state the ceiling in §13; (b) rent a cloud GPU for the 9B/8B dockets and record its `hardware` in the subject (then the noise floor is that machine's, not this box's); (c) define the reference precision for >4B models as q8_0 under llama.cpp and drop bf16 from §13.1. In every case add 'free ≥60 GB disk' as a week-1 task.
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Checked: nvidia-smi reports 'NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4070 Laptop GPU, 8188 MiB'; df -h /c shows 2.5G free at 100%; the HF cache holds gemma-2-2b, Llama-3.2-3B, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct and Mistral-7B but no gemma-2-9b or Llama-3.1-8B; glimmer REPORT.md:18 documents ~2.5 tok/s with CPU offload on this box; memory styxx-calibration-leg-2026-09-05.md says the box has been OOM and 'never two heavy runs at once'; grep finds no 90-day plan document naming those models in the repo. Author's side is real: §2.2 puts `hardware` in the subject and its own example is an RTX 4090 the lab does not own, so the spec never says the dockets run on this laptop; §14 explicitly separates 'GPU runs keystone v2' from CPU work, and the 'available on this machine' commitment in the plan of record (line 69) is about sworn leg 2 panels, not a universal rule. What survives is that §13/§14 commit to bf16 R=8 dockets on 9B/8B models within 48 hours with no hardware, budget, or disk line, citing a 90-day plan that is not in the repo; that is an unbacked plan commitment, not a defect in the verdict machinery, and Appendix D does not fail. Major, agreeing with the first verifier; the 2.5 GB free disk is a genuine week-1 blocker for any model download and should be named in §14 regardless of which option is chosen.

### S13-02 · major · §13.3; §5.3; §6
**Quant delta (docket item 3) is a cross-subject `--diff` with no governing floor: exits 2 by §5.3, or the verdict depends on an unstated choice of floor**

- claim: bf16 and q4_k_m are different subjects (§2.2 `precision` is a field of S) with different per-(S, B, recipe) floors; §5.3 voids the floor when the subject changes and §5.2 returns `inconclusive` without a floor, and §6 `verify --diff` prints a single per-channel floor with no rule for which cert's floor (or which combination) decides `same`/`drift` — so the docket's quant-delta step can never print a reproducible verdict.
- evidence: §13: "3. **quant delta** — `verify --diff` bf16 vs q4_k_m on both batteries." §5.3: "Floors are per (S, B, recipe). Change any of them and the floor is void — `verify` exits 2, not 0." §2.2: `precision` is a field of the subject S. §5.2: "no noise floor on record for (S, B, recipe) | `inconclusive` — verify says why, exits 2". §6: "styxx verify --diff <cert A> <cert B> # compare two existing certs; no execution" with per-channel `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}` — one floor.
- consequence: The routine docket step, and the entire 'the Glimmer runbook, now a routine step' claim, produce exit 2 and no number; or two implementers pick A's floor and B's floor respectively and print different verdicts and ratios for the same pair of certs, so the docket's headline quant-delta number is not reproducible.
- amendment: Add to §6: "`--diff A B` uses A's floor (the `--ref`/left cert's (S,B,recipe) floor), prints `floor_owner: A`, and prints B's floor beside it. If A has no floor for its own (S, B, recipe), exit 2. Cross-subject diffs are labelled `cross-subject` in the result cert and are never `same`; the verdict vocabulary for them is `within-floor(A)` / `beyond-floor(A)`." (Vocabulary change is operator-gated per lab doctrine.)
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing, canary-selection
- verifiers: STANDS: The author's best defense holds partway: §5.3 literally resolves the cross-subject case (precision is in S per §2.2, so A's floor is void and the verdict is `inconclusive`, exit 2, deterministically — no implementer chooses A's or B's floor), and the per-channel distance remains reproducible via Appendix B and §3.3, with only exit 3 suppressing distances (§6). What survives is that §13.3 ("routine step") and §15.4 ("whether 256 is enough to separate q4 from bf16") plainly intend the bf16 floor to govern the quant delta, and nothing in §5–§6 licenses that, so docket items 3 and 4 are exit-2 by construction. A stated-intent-vs-rule contradiction with a one-sentence fix, sharing its root cause with S2-01: major, not blocker.

### S13-03 · major · §13.6; §7.1; §12
**Docket summary constraints are unenforceable and permit misleading-by-omission and lab readings rendered as measurements**

- claim: 'No adjectives' is not a machine-checkable rule, 'ends with the cert id it came from' does not require that every docket cert be summarised, and nothing forces lab-tier readings in the summary to carry the banner, so a summary can cite only the flattering certs and print a lab reading as a fact.
- evidence: §13.6: "Constraint: no adjectives, and every sentence ends with the cert id it came from. If a sentence can't cite a cert, it doesn't ship." §13.5: "instrument readings — every registered instrument, in the tier the log assigns." §7.1 fixes the lab banner for `attest` output only: "writes certified readings into the certified section and everything else into a `lab` section under a fixed banner".
- consequence: Summary for docket gemma-2-9b: "The model did not drift on 256 canaries. sha256:A" (true; the battery was selected two hours earlier against this same model, so `same` is tautological). "Depth reading 13.7 on TriviaQA. sha256:B" (a lab-tier action/result cert; no adjective; reads as a measurement). The q4 delta result showing `drift(exact)` on sha256:C is simply not mentioned. Every constraint in §13.6 is satisfied.
- amendment: §13.6: replace the constraints with: "The summary is generated from a fixed template table (one template per (cert type, verdict/kind) pair, versioned and hashed into the docket); no free text. Completeness: one sentence per cert in the docket, in log order; a docket cert without a sentence fails generation. Lab-tier readings use the template that begins with the §7.1 banner text. Canary `same` sentences carry the template suffix `(battery selected against this subject at log index N)`." Drop 'no adjectives'.
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: §13.6's only constraints are 'no adjectives' and 'every sentence ends with the cert id it came from. If a sentence can't cite a cert, it doesn't ship' — nothing in §13 requires that every docket cert be summarised, so a summary that omits a `drift(exact)` verify result while citing only `same` results satisfies the spec as written. The banner leak is also real in the text: §7.1's banner and 'There is no flag to move a reading between sections' bind `styxx attest` output, §0's third non-goal binds 'a certified section', and §1.6 binds 'compliance output' — the §13.6 human summary is none of these, so a lab-tier depth reading with a cert id and no adjectives is a spec-conforming sentence that reads as a measurement, which contradicts the lab's doctrine that no instrument reading stands without a promotion cert. The tautology example is overdrawn (item 2 logs a canary fingerprint, not a `same` verdict, and §4.5 `selected_against` already records the provenance), and the template amendment overlaps S12-01/S12-02, but the omission and banner holes are exploitable in the public-facing deliverable, so major stands rather than blocker: no cert or verdict is wrong, the summary document is.

### S13-04 · major · §13; §14; §4.2; §5.1
**GPU budget for one gemma-2-2b docket is 25–40 serial GPU-hours with no slack for the mandatory confirmation run, on a GPU the spec assigns to 'keystone v2'**

- claim: Counting the spec's own steps on the actual GPU, a single 2B docket consumes most of the 48 hours even if nothing fails, and §14 simultaneously reserves the GPU for a document that does not exist.
- evidence: Arithmetic from spec parameters: §4.5 example `"pool_size": 12000`; §4.2 δ1 (2 quant variants), δ2 (batch ∈ {1,8,32} + permuted order ⇒ ≥3), δ3 (n=3), plus reference ⇒ ≥9 generations/item ⇒ ≥108,000 generations of 64 tokens. gemma-2-2b bf16 ≈ 5.2 GB over 256 GB/s ⇒ ≥20 ms/token decode; batch-1 legs (reference + δ2 batch 1 = 24,000 gens x ~2.5 s) ≈ 17 h; δ1 under llama.cpp (2 x 12,000 x ~1.5 s) ≈ 10 h; batched legs ≈ 3 h ⇒ ~30 h selection. fixed-v1 floor R=8: 4,096 gens ≈ 1.5–3 h; canary floor 320 x 8 ≈ 1 h; q4 runs on both batteries ≈ 0.5 h (+ ~2 h if q4 needs its own floor). §14: "GPU runs keystone v2; everything below is CPU work until noted" and "canary-v1 selection (GPU: the δ1 precision sweep — schedule between keystone batches)". SCOUT.md: "NOT FOUND ... 'keystone v2' (as a document)". `papers/depth-truth/PREREG_v2.md` is titled "the keystone" and already ran to `CLOSED_NEGATIVE_NO_TRUTH_SIGNAL` (`FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth_2026_07_03.md:4`).
- consequence: The docket deadline is missed on the first drift confirmation or a single OOM; 'schedule between keystone batches' is unschedulable because keystone v2's GPU demand is undefined; the spec's 'Science and marketing on the same GPU hours' is false (keystone is depth on gemma-2-2b; dockets 1–3 are other models).
- amendment: Add to §13 a budget table per docket size (pool, N, R, expected GPU-hours on the declared hardware) and make `pool_size` a docket parameter with a default that fits: e.g. pool 2,000, N=256, K=64, R=5 for the 48-hour docket (≈6 GPU-h selection + 1.5 h floors). In §14 replace 'GPU runs keystone v2' with a named document and its hour budget, or delete the sentence.
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §4.5 shows `pool_size: 12000`, §4.4 sets docket N=1024 + K=64, §5.1 sets R=8 for dockets, §5.2 mandates a confirmation run, §13 sets a 48-hour deadline, and §14 says 'GPU runs keystone v2' and 'schedule between keystone batches' while giving no hour budget anywhere. The actual GPU is an RTX 4070 Laptop 8GB (experiments/glimmer_dayzero_2026_08_10/REPORT.md:18, voice_lora TRAIN_PLAN.md:12), not the RTX 4090 in the §2.2 example, so the 256 GB/s assumption is right and the order-of-magnitude estimate stands; a 9B docket in bf16 does not fit at all. 'keystone v2' exists only as papers/depth-truth/PREREG_v2.md ('the keystone'), already CLOSED_NEGATIVE, so the GPU reservation is for an undefined workload. The pool_size figure is an example rather than a mandate, so the exact hours are arguable, but the absence of any budget against a hard deadline on known hardware is a real defect, not a stylistic one.

### S14-01 · major · Title block; §14 Weeks 1–4; §2
**Binding condition is circular (entry #0 needs the log §14 schedules to build), names no defined cert type, and leaves 'its hash' unspecified on a CRLF repo**

- claim: The spec becomes binding only when its hash is logged as charon entry #0, but the log accepts only signed certs of eight types none of which carries a document hash, the log does not exist until the code this spec governs is written (violating freeze-before-code), and which bytes are hashed (working tree vs git blob, CRLF vs LF) is unstated.
- evidence: Header: "status: DRAFT v0.1 · 2026-09-07 · becomes binding when committed and its hash is logged as charon entry #0". §14 Weeks 1–4: "Charon v0: git-backed log, Merkle tree, STH, inclusion + consistency proofs, `mirror`. First entries, in order: #0 this spec's hash; #1 monochord prereg v3; #2 keystone v2 prereg." §2: "One schema, eight types." §8.3: "CI validates schema". SCOUT.md:27: "Specs are frozen in their own commit BEFORE code." SCOUT.md:23: "a raw hash of working-tree bytes differs Windows vs Linux (charon v0.1 E15 lesson)"; memory charon-v01-ferry-log.md E15; memory sworn-v02-and-the-plan-2026-09-02.md: "swear `path:` hashes to the blob, never to a hash the harness took of the checkout."
- consequence: By its own rule the spec is never binding: implementation of §2–§8 begins against an unfrozen draft, any edit during weeks 1–4 is a silent rewrite rather than an erratum, and entry #0 is either a hand-made non-conforming line every conforming verifier rejects (Appendix D step 1 fails on the founding entry) or a Windows CRLF digest a Linux stranger cannot reproduce.
- amendment: Header → "becomes binding at the commit that adds it; frozen text is the git blob at that commit; later changes are dated ERRATA sections appended below, never edits (the charon v0.1 SPEC convention)." Add a `.sworn.json` sidecar canoned with `--commit <sha>` so the spec's identity is the blob hash the sworn verifier already checks. Remove the binding dependency on entry #0. If entry #0 is retained as a log record, make it a conforming cert: a `prereg` cert with `kind: spec-freeze` (add `kind` to the prereg body enum, or add a `doc` type) carrying the git blob id and referencing that commit — never a working-tree hash.
- sources: transparency-log, doctrine-and-commitments
- verifiers: STANDS: Checked the header ("becomes binding when committed and its hash is logged as charon entry #0"), §14 Weeks 1–4 ("#0 this spec's hash" in a log built in those same weeks), §2's eight types, and the repo convention in papers/sworn and papers/charon SPEC_* headers ("frozen before any code"); .gitattributes pins only sworn/centroid paths, so a working-tree hash of this file is CRLF-dependent per memory charon-v01 E15. The author can argue the header is a status line and that an `action` cert with `action_kind: commit` (§10) could carry the sha conformingly, but the conjunction as written leaves the spec unbound during the weeks its own code is built, contrary to lab doctrine. It does not make §2–§13 unimplementable and the fix is a header edit before the freeze commit, so it drops from blocker to major.

### S14-02 · major · §14 (Weeks 8-12); App. D
**The 8.0 ship gate tests App. D steps 1-2 only; steps 3-5 (reproduce on foreign hardware, log a challenge) are never exercised before the tag**

- claim: App. D calls itself "the acceptance test for 8.0" but the only stated 8.0 gate is that the four `log verify-*` commands pass on a fresh mirror, which exercises only id/signature and inclusion/consistency proofs; no receipt is required showing a non-flobi machine ran `verify --ref` on a public cert and landed within the floor.
- evidence: §14: "8.0 tagged when the four `log verify-*` commands pass on a fresh mirror clone by someone who isn't flobi."; App. D steps 3-5: "obtain the subject", "Run `styxx verify --ref <cert>`", "log a challenge"; memory `differential-needs-calibration.md`: "an agreement number is worthless without measuring its own detection power"; SCOUT lab doctrine: "Every number cites a receipt".
- consequence: 8.0 can be tagged with every public fingerprint cert failing its own acceptance test (e.g. the cross-hardware exact-mismatch rate on fixed-v1 is unknown; nobody has measured it), and the spec's "the spec has failed" clause would trigger the day after the tag.
- amendment: Add to §14 Weeks 8-12: "8.0 is tagged only when a `result` cert, `kind = verify`, issued under a key that is not fathom's, on hardware whose (gpu, driver) differs from every entry in the target's `noise_floor.runs`, is in the log for at least one fixed-v1 fingerprint cert, with per-channel distances published whether or not they fall within the floor. The cross-hardware `exact` mismatch rate from that run is the first receipt cited by §5."
- sources: stranger-reproducibility
- verifiers: STANDS: §14 Weeks 8–12 states the sole tag condition as the four `log verify-*` commands passing on a fresh mirror clone by a non-flobi; Appendix D calls its five steps "the acceptance test for 8.0" and steps 3–5 are cross-hardware reproduction. The author's defense — dockets 1–3 in weeks 4–8 exercise `verify --ref` — fails because those runs are on the lab's single GPU (§5.1 "where available", §15.1), so nothing schedules a stranger's `verify --ref` before the tag, contrary to memory differential-needs-calibration.md. A contradiction between §14 and App. D rather than a wrong verdict; agrees with the first verifier's downgrade to major.

### S14-03 · major · §14; §0; §13
**Silently supersedes the sworn plan of record (four legs, operator-signed measurement, claim ledger)**

- claim: The spec presents a 12-week sequencing that never mentions sworn, the leg-2 measurement awaiting the operator's signature, the leg-4 provenance prereg, or the claim ledger, and refers to a '90-day plan' that is not a committed document — replacing PLAN_the_next_level without naming it or stating that it is superseded.
- evidence: Spec §14: "Maps onto the 90-day plan. GPU runs keystone v2"; §13: "The generalization runs in the 90-day plan (gemma-2-9b, llama-3.1-8b, one more family) *are* dockets 1–3." The word 'sworn' does not occur in the spec (SCOUT.md:12: "The v8 spec never mentions sworn"). papers/PLAN_the_next_level_2026_09_02.md:7-8 "Successor to `closed-model-frontier/PLAN_prior_art_and_the_next_move_2026_08_31.md`, which it does not edit."; :67-85 "Leg 2 — the measurement ... **What blocks it is one signature on the bars.**"; :99 "Leg 4 — the science move, preregistered and cheap."; :111-131 "The claim ledger". Memory sworn-v02-and-the-plan: "The plan of record is now `papers/PLAN_the_next_level_2026_09_02.md`". No file named '90-day plan' exists in the worktree (grep).
- consequence: Two plans of record coexist; the GPU and the operator's attention move to fingerprinting and Merkle logs while the measurement that every licensed sentence in the ledger waits on stays unsigned and unrun, and the ledger's prohibitions are not inherited by the new document.
- amendment: Add a section 'Relation to PLAN_the_next_level_2026_09_02' stating one of — OPERATOR-GATED: (a) v8 is scheduled after legs 2 and 4 of the plan of record, which remain first and are not edited; (b) v8 supersedes the plan of record, named and dated, with the reasons; (c) v8 runs in parallel with a stated GPU/attention split. In every case: inherit the claim ledger verbatim by reference, and either commit the '90-day plan' as a document with a path or delete every reference to it.
- sources: doctrine-and-commitments
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed "sworn" is absent from the spec, that no '90-day plan' document exists in the worktree (grep hits are docs/token and an unrelated T+90 roadmap), and that papers/PLAN_the_next_level_2026_09_02.md :7-8, :67-85 ("What blocks it is one signature on the bars"), :99 and :111-131 read as cited, with memory sworn-v02-and-the-plan naming it the plan of record. The author can argue coexistence: v8 is an engineering spec for a different surface and leg 2 runs on CPU/CLI and blocks on a signature, not GPU hours, so the GPU-contention consequence is overstated. What survives is an unbacked reference plus no stated relation to the plan of record and no inheritance of its claim ledger — an operator-gated relation statement, technical content unaffected: major.

### S14-04 · major · §14 weeks 8–12; §8.2 README; §11
**'The four `log verify-*` commands' (8.0 acceptance criterion) do not exist: §11 defines two**

- claim: The 8.0 tag condition references four `verify-*` log commands and the README 'four verification commands', but the CLI surface lists only `verify-inclusion` and `verify-consistency`, so the acceptance criterion is unsatisfiable as written.
- evidence: §14: "8.0 tagged when the four `log verify-*` commands pass on a fresh mirror clone by someone who isn't flobi." §8.2: "README.md # the four verification commands, nothing else". §11: "styxx log append <cert> | prove <id> | verify-inclusion <id> <sth> | verify-consistency <sth1> <sth2> | sth | mirror | challenge <target> <own>".
- consequence: The stranger tasked with the acceptance test cannot find commands three and four; the tag condition is either met vacuously or never.
- amendment: Enumerate the four in §11 and §8.2 identically, e.g. `verify-sth <sth>` (signature + key), `verify-inclusion <index> <sth>`, `verify-consistency <sth_m> <sth_n>`, `verify-entry <index>` (id recompute + cert signature); move `challenge` out of `log` into its own top-level command since it issues a cert.
- sources: transparency-log
- verifiers: STANDS: §11 lists exactly two `verify-*` subcommands under `log` (verify-inclusion, verify-consistency); §14 gates the 8.0 tag on 'the four `log verify-*` commands' and §8.2 says the README holds 'the four verification commands, nothing else'. No other passage enumerates the four. Because the count is the tag gate and the README is constrained to exactly that set, an implementer cannot resolve it without inventing two commands; it is an internal contradiction in the release criterion, not a stylistic slip.

### S14-06 · major · §14; §6; §11
**No definition of done, test strategy, or error-handling contract per week — the sequencing cannot be checked by anyone but the author**

- claim: §14 lists artifacts per four-week block with no acceptance test, no test-count/coverage bar, no Linux-CI requirement, and no statement of what `fingerprint`/`verify`/`log` do on partial failure (OOM mid-run, a missing SAE, a truncated ndjson, an STH whose key file is absent).
- evidence: §14 weeks 1–4 through 8–12 contain only noun lists (e.g. "Charon v0: git-backed log, Merkle tree, STH, inclusion + consistency proofs, `mirror`."). §6 defines exit codes for four verdict states only; no exit code for I/O or signature failure. Memory `styxx-calibration-leg-2026-09-05.md`: "The box has been OOM since ~2am; every 'torch crash' was that ... CI on Linux is the authority." Lab tradition: every leg ships a SPEC → ATTACKS → RESULT triple (`papers/charon/`, `papers/closed-model-frontier/*receipt_binding*`).
- consequence: A week can be declared done with nothing verifiable; an OOM halfway through R=8 leaves 5 run certs in the log with no rule for whether the canonical fingerprint may reference an incomplete floor (§5.1 step 4), producing a floor from fewer pairs than the spec's minimum.
- amendment: Add to each §14 block: the sworn RESULT that closes it, the test files, and the CI condition ('green on py3.9–3.12 Linux'). Add §6.1 'Failure exits': 4 = invalid cert (id/sig), 5 = I/O or environment failure (model not loadable, OOM, missing SAE) — no result cert is emitted on 4/5. Add to §5.1: "a floor cert is only issued when all R runs completed; partial run sets are stored as `fingerprint` certs with `noise_floor: null`."
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate in its core: §14 blocks are noun lists, §6 defines exit codes 0-3 for verdict states only, and nothing in the spec says what `fingerprint`/`verify`/`log append` do on I/O, signature, or environment failure; §5.1 step 4 lets the canonical fingerprint reference 'the others' with no all-R-completed rule. The material hole is the CI contract: an uncaught crash in `verify` exits 1, which §6 defines as `drift`, so an OOM (memory styxx-calibration-leg-2026-09-05.md line 56 confirms the box's OOM history) produces a wrong verdict in any pipeline keyed on the table. The finding overstates 'no definition of done' — §14's last bullet (four `log verify-*` commands on a fresh mirror by someone who isn't flobi) and App. D give an overall acceptance test, and the test-count/Linux-CI asks are process preferences, not spec defects; the SPEC→ATTACKS→RESULT tradition is confirmed by papers/charon/ and papers/closed-model-frontier/ATTACKS_receipt_binding_battery_2026_09_05.md. Major for the failure-exit gap and the partial-floor ambiguity, not for the per-week ceremony.

### S14-07 · major · §14 weeks 1–4; §7.4
**Entry #2 'keystone v2 prereg' has no document and the only 'keystone' prereg already closed negative; entry #1 'monochord prereg v3' has no v1 or v2 in the repo**

- claim: The first three log entries include two preregistrations that do not exist as files; one of them names an experiment whose v2 ran and was terminated with `CLOSED_NEGATIVE_NO_TRUTH_SIGNAL`, and the spec's §7.2 example promotion claim is that experiment's rejected hypothesis.
- evidence: §14: "#1 monochord prereg v3; #2 keystone v2 prereg." §7.4: "depth ... keystone v2 prereg cert → result → promotion or a public negative"; "monochord (H1–H3 spectral) | lab | prereg v3". SCOUT.md: "NOT FOUND ... 'monochord', 'keystone v2' (as a document)". `papers/depth-truth/PREREG_v2.md:1-5`: "PREREG v2 — the keystone ... FROZEN on commit — ratified by flobi 2026-07-01"; `FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth_2026_07_03.md:4`: "Verdict: `CLOSED_NEGATIVE_NO_TRUTH_SIGNAL`"; `:32` "ΔAUC = 0.0026 [-0.0044, 0.0188]" vs §7.2 "ΔAUC = 0.041 [0.018, 0.063]".
- consequence: Weeks 1–4 cannot log entries #1/#2; the GPU schedule anchored on 'keystone v2' has no consumer; a reader of §7.2 takes a refuted number for a worked example of what a promotion looks like.
- amendment: Name the files: `papers/depth-truth/PREREG_v3.md` and `papers/monochord/PREREG_v3.md` with authors and week; if they are not written by week 2, entries #1/#2 are dropped from §14 and the log's first real use is the fixed-v1 battery cert. Replace the §7.2 example claim with a placeholder that cannot be mistaken for a measurement ("<claim text; must match a result cert's endpoint verbatim>") and cite the CLOSED_NEGATIVE finding in §7.4.
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Partly misstated, substantively correct. 'monochord' has zero hits anywhere in the repo, so §14 entry #1 and §7.4's 'prereg v3' name a document with no v1/v2/v3. The keystone v2 prereg DOES exist as a document (papers/depth-truth/PREREG_v2.md, 'FROZEN on commit — ratified by flobi 2026-07-01'), so the 'no document' wording is wrong — but the actual problem is worse: it already ran and closed (FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth_2026_07_03.md: `CLOSED_NEGATIVE_NO_TRUTH_SIGNAL`, H2 ΔAUC = 0.0026 [-0.0044, 0.0188]), so logging it as entry #2 'timestamped preregistration' proves nothing about order, §14's 'GPU runs keystone v2' has no new experiment behind it, and §7.2's example promotion carries ΔAUC = 0.041 [0.018, 0.063] — a number the lab's own receipt refuted, presented without a receipt, which violates the 'every number cites a receipt' doctrine in the spec's only worked example of a promotion. Nothing elsewhere in the spec marks §7.2's claim as hypothetical or cites the negative finding.

### S2-03 · major · §2.1; §8.1; §8.4; §10; App. D step 2
**STH signing input is undefined; cert sigs and STH sigs share raw-32-byte form with no domain separation**

- claim: The spec never says which bytes the STH `sig` covers, and the cert `sig` is an ed25519 signature over a bare 32-byte digest with no context prefix, so a signature made for one purpose is bit-identical in form to a signature made for another.
- evidence: §2.1: "`sig` = ed25519 signature over the raw 32-byte digest." §8.1: "**Signed tree head (STH)** = `{ log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp, sig }`, ed25519 with the log key." — no signing-input definition anywhere; RFC 6962 §3.5 signs version||type||timestamp||tree_size||root_hash, which the spec claims to follow ("Domain separation exactly as RFC 6962") but does not reproduce. §15.1 leaves open whether the log key and the issuer key are the same ("The Alienware should hold a delegated signing key"); §8.3 v0 is "fathom's key only". Contrast the lab's own practice: styxx/charon.py:457-459 `header_digest` — "Domain-separated so a header can never masquerade as an entry".
- consequence: A stranger implementing App. D step 2 ("Fetch the STH; verify the inclusion proof") cannot verify an STH because the signed bytes are unspecified; two implementations will disagree and `log verify-inclusion` is unimplementable as written. If the STH sig is implemented as a raw signature over `root_hash` (the natural reading of §2.1's pattern) and one key signs both certs and STHs, any signed cert id X can be replayed as a valid STH `{root_hash: X, tree_size: anything, timestamp: anything}` — §8.4's misbehaviour proof ("two STHs with the same tree_size and different root_hash") can then be forged against the operator with the operator's own signature, and mirrors will publish it.
- amendment: Replace §2.1 sig line with: `sig` = ed25519 over the ASCII bytes `"styxx-cert-v8\x00"` || raw 32-byte id digest. Replace §8.1 STH line with: STH = `{log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp, sig}` where `sig` = ed25519 over `"styxx-sth-v8\x00"` || JCS({log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp}) and `log_id` = `sha256:` of the raw 32-byte log public key. Sub-log STHs (§10) use `"styxx-sublog-sth-v8\x00"`. Add to §2.1: the log key MUST be distinct from every issuer key.
- sources: crypto-envelope
- verifiers: STANDS: Checked §8.1: the STH is `{ log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp, sig }`, 'ed25519 with the log key', and no sentence anywhere names the signed bytes; the 'Domain separation exactly as RFC 6962' clause is in the leaf/node-hash bullet, and the STH shape does not match RFC 6962 §3.5's TreeHeadSignature (no version/signature_type, ed25519 instead of the RFC's algorithms), so 'follow the RFC' is not a usable fallback. Two independent implementations (JCS-over-object-minus-sig vs raw-over-root_hash vs RFC struct) will disagree, and §14's own acceptance gate ('four `log verify-*` commands pass on a fresh mirror clone by someone who isn't flobi') is then satisfiable only by reading the reference code, which the lab doctrine (spec frozen and logged as entry #0 before code) forbids. The forgery half is contingent: §8.1 'the log key', §8.2 `keys/log.pub`, and §2.1 `issuer.key` already distinguish the log key, and §15.1 recommends a separate delegated key, so cert-id-as-STH replay requires an operator choice the spec steers away from; charon.py:457 `header_digest` shows the lab already practices domain separation, so the missing context prefix is a regression but not an exploit as written. Major, agreeing with the first verifier: a one-paragraph missing definition that must land before commit, no wrong verdict or number.

### S2-05 · major · §2.2; §4; §4.5; §5.1; §8.6; §15.3
**Alias provider routes recognised battery prompts to a pinned model; verify says `same` for a swapped model**

- claim: Every battery prompt (fixed-v1 and canary-v1) is published in the clear in the log, so a stateful API provider can recognise them by hash and serve the reference model only for those requests while serving a different model to everyone else; `verify --ref` then returns `same` and nothing in the spec states this limitation.
- evidence: §4.5: "Prompts are stored in the clear unless the battery is marked `redacted`." and the battery body carries `"prompt_sha256": "…", "prompt_text": "…"` per item. §4: "fixed-v1 — public". §5.1.1: "for alias subjects, time-of-day and region within the shortest window that yields R runs" (same prompts every run). §8.6: "Does not address: providers who make reproduction impossible." — the attack makes reproduction trivially succeed, which is not the listed case. §15.3: "canaries — selected per model, after release — can't be trained against in advance" — but the canary cert is logged before any verify runs, so the provider learns them at first publication. §12 shows `result (verify)` under "change tracking / version control".
- consequence: Steps: (1) provider computes sha256 of each incoming prompt and matches against the published battery items; (2) on match, route to a frozen snapshot of the fingerprinted model (or to a response cache keyed by prompt+decoding, which also fixes seqlp/topk since the provider returns the logprobs); (3) serve a new/cheaper model to all other traffic. Every `styxx verify --ref` on the alias reads `same`, the confirmation run reads `same`, and the compliance view lists an unbroken `same` history for a model that was swapped. The verdict is false and no reader can tell.
- amendment: Add to §4.6 and §8.6, verbatim: "For alias subjects, a `same` verdict is evidence only against a provider that does not condition on the request. A provider that recognises published prompts and serves them from a pinned model or a cache defeats every black-box channel; the spec has no defence and does not claim one." Label alias verify results in §6 output and §12 as `same (published-prompt)`. OPERATOR-GATED: whether to add an alias-only `probe` mechanism (a small set of never-published paraphrase items generated per verify run, reported as a separate advisory channel with no floor) — trade-off: it violates principle 2 (not reproducible by a stranger) and so must be a note, not a claim.
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §4 makes fixed-v1 public, §4.5 stores `prompt_text` in the clear by default, §5.1.1 reuses the same items every run, and a provider that hashes incoming prompts and routes matches to a pinned snapshot or cache makes every alias `verify --ref` read `same` while §12 lists that history under change tracking. Author's side is real: §2.2 says an alias fingerprint can never be as strong and is labeled black-box everywhere, §8.6 disclaims providers who defeat reproduction, §4.6 already concedes canary-targeted training, and the spec never claims to catch a dishonest provider — the `same` verdict is literally true (distance under floor on those prompts), so no number or App. D step is wrong. It survives below blocker because it is an undisclosed attack in a threat model that partially addresses adversarial providers (yearly rotation, post-release canaries) and therefore misleads by omission; the fix is a limitation sentence in §4.6/§8.6 and a label on alias verdicts, and any probe mechanism is operator-gated and would be a note under §0, not a claim.

### S2-09 · major · §2
**"Keep the OATH envelope" is false — the v8 envelope shares no field with OATH certificates, and "OATH v2" reads as superseding 213 committed receipts**

- claim: The shipped OATH certificate is a flat numeric-claim verdict record with no id, issuer, signature, refs, type or self-digest; the v8 envelope (styxx/type/id/created/issuer/subject/recipe/body/refs/sig) is a new format, so the sentence describes something that does not exist and hides a migration, and labelling it 'OATH v2' reads the 213 committed 'styxx OATH v0' certificates and their rule versions v0.3…v0.13 as predecessors of an unrelated signed envelope.
- evidence: §2: "## 2. Cert envelope (OATH v2) — Keep the OATH envelope; type the payloads. One schema, eight types." Repo: styxx/certify.py:1341-1376 `cert = {"oath": "styxx OATH v0 (numeric-claim certificate)", "prereg": ..., "document": ..., "document_sha256": ..., "receipts_sha256": ..., "receipt_binding": ..., "verifier_sha256": ..., "counts": ..., "epistemics_summary": ..., "uncovered": ..., "verdict": verdict, "ungrounded": ..., "abstained": ..., "ledger": ledger}`; certify.py:932 `"schema": "styxx-oath/epistemics-summary/v1"`; real instances papers/depth-truth/FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth_2026_07_03.certificate.json and papers/agent-conscience/BLOCKED_source_independence_2026_07_24.certificate.json have exactly those keys. OATH_CONTRACT.md:1-2 defines OATH as "how to write a document that carries its own receipts", rules cite "(v0.3 count-binding…)", "(v0.7.)", "v0.8", "(v0.11)"; 213 tracked `*.certificate.json`. Memory styxx-leg1-receipt-binding: "213 certificates, 631 citations"; a-receipt-is-history-too: "certificates are already treated as immutable — re-issues are new commits". The only existing self-addressed envelopes are styxx.attestation (`digest.value`/`digest.portable`) and the capsule `binding` block (styxx/capsule.py:775-778, alg "sha256-jcs").
- consequence: An implementer looking for the envelope to keep finds none and either bolts v8 fields onto the OATH v0 dict (breaking `corpus_audit`, the capsule `sha256-jcs` binding and the 213 committed certificates) or ignores the sentence — the spec does not say which; a reader concludes the OATH v0 certificates and the corpus audit that pins them are deprecated, the charon v0.1 lines over them (213 of 243) lose their referent, and the OATH verifier's own version line becomes ambiguous.
- amendment: Retitle §2 "Cert envelope (styxx/8.0, new)" and replace the first sentence with: "This envelope is new. It does not replace the OATH numeric-claim certificate (styxx OATH v0, `*.certificate.json`, styxx/certify.py), the sworn sidecar/receipt, the attestation artifact or the capsule; those are untouched history, none is re-issued under this envelope, and `corpus_audit`/charon v0.1 continue to verify them under their own schemas. An `eval`/`result` cert MAY carry one of those artifacts' digests in `body` and reference it via `refs: ["sha256:<digest of the artifact file>"]` through an `attachment` cert. The only element inherited from existing artifacts is the canonicalizer, `styxx.attestation.jcs`."
- sources: codebase-reconciliation, doctrine-and-commitments, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §2 reads 'Cert envelope (OATH v2) — Keep the OATH envelope; type the payloads', and certify.py:1341–1376 shows the shipped OATH certificate is a flat dict (`oath`, `prereg`, `document`, `document_sha256`, `receipts_sha256`, `receipt_binding`, `verifier_sha256`, `counts`, `verdict`, `ledger`…) with no id, issuer, sig, refs or type; OATH_CONTRACT.md defines OATH as a document-carries-its-receipts contract; charon.log.jsonl tallies 213 `oath-certificate` lines. No other passage explains what is 'kept' or how OATH v0 artifacts relate to the new envelope. Under the lab's doctrine (every claim cites a receipt; committed certificates are history) an unbacked continuity claim that relabels an adoptable public contract as superseded is an unbacked claim and a naming contradiction, which is major; not a blocker, because a competent implementer would ignore the sentence rather than re-issue history, so no verdict or App. D step is wrong.

### S2-10 · major · §2.3; §3.1; §5.1 step 4; §11
**Seed is both a fixed recipe field and the varying quantity that picks the canonical run; `body.runs` is undefined**

- claim: If the R noise runs use different seeds they have different `decoding` and are not comparable with each other by §2.3, so the floor is computed across comparisons the spec forbids; if they use the same seed, 'lowest run seed' selects nothing; seed is not in the nuisance list and at temperature 0 greedy decoding ignores it; and `runs` in the body is never defined.
- evidence: §2.3: `"decoding": { "temperature": 0, "top_p": 1.0, "max_new_tokens": 64, "stop": ["\n"], "seed": 7 }` and "Two certs are **comparable** iff `battery`, `decoding`, `chat_template_sha256`, and `system_prompt_sha256` match exactly." §5.1 step 1: "varying only nuisance factors: batch size, item order, physical GPU / driver". §5.1 step 4: "The canonical fingerprint (lowest run seed) carries `noise_floor.runs` referencing the others." §3.1: `"runs": 1`; §11: `[--runs 5]`.
- consequence: An implementer who varies the seed per run produces noise-floor certs whose pairwise `--diff` exits 3 (recipe mismatch), so the floor is built from comparisons `verify` itself would refuse; one who keeps seed 7 has no rule for which run is canonical, and the choice changes which cert carries the floor and therefore which id App. D step 1 recomputes.
- amendment: Seed is fixed across the R runs (temperature 0 makes it moot). Remove 'lowest run seed'; add `body.run_index` (0..R−1) and the recorded nuisance settings `body.nuisance: { batch_size, item_order_sha256, gpu, driver }` to every run cert; canonical = run_index 0 (tie-break: lowest cert id lexicographic). Define `body.runs` as "R, the number of runs in this floor set; 1 for a single un-floored run"; non-canonical run certs carry `noise_floor: null`. The noise floor MUST be computed only among certs whose recipes are byte-identical (verify checks this and exits 3 otherwise).
- sources: noise-floor-statistics, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually exact: §2.3 puts `"seed": 7` inside `decoding` and makes `decoding` part of the exact-match comparability rule ("Otherwise `verify` exits 3"); §5.1 step 1 lists nuisance factors (batch size, item order, GPU/driver, time/region) without seed; §5.1 step 4 says "canonical fingerprint (lowest run seed)", which only makes sense if seeds vary; §3.1 shows `"runs": 1` and §11 `[--runs 5]` with no definition. No other passage resolves it. Under the reading step 4 invites, the R run certs are pairwise non-comparable by the rule §2.3 calls the single most important honesty rule, so the floor is built from comparisons `verify --diff` would refuse; under the fixed-seed reading the canonical-cert rule selects nothing. That is a contradiction in the noise-floor protocol (principle 3's mechanism), so major stands; the amendment's run_index/nuisance fields are one reasonable fix.

### S2-13 · major · §2 envelope; §8.1 STH `log_id`; §8.2 `keys/log.pub`; §10 sub-log; App. D step 2
**The cert does not say which log it is in or which log key signs it; App. D step 2 cannot start 'from the cert alone'**

- claim: The acceptance test says every step must be possible from the cert alone, but the envelope has no `log`/`log_id` field, no inclusion-proof pointer and no log-key reference, while STHs carry a `log_id` and the log key lives at `keys/log.pub` in a repository the cert does not name; action certs additionally need a sub-log root that nothing in the cert names.
- evidence: §2 envelope fields: styxx, type, id, created, issuer, subject, recipe, body, refs, sig; §8.1: "STH = { log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp, sig }"; §8.2 tree: `keys/log.pub # log key`; §10: "Any individual action is then provable with two inclusion proofs: action → sub-log root, sub-log root → charon" — the action cert body has no sub-log id. App. D: "2. Fetch the STH; verify the inclusion proof." "If any step is impossible from the cert alone, the cert is defective and the spec has failed."
- consequence: Every cert fails the acceptance test by construction on day one: a stranger handed a cert file must already know the GitHub Pages URL and, for action certs, the darkflobi sub-log location.
- amendment: Fix the canonical log location and `log_id` in this spec (§8.2, and therefore in entry #0) and state in App. D that "the public log" means that URL; rewrite the App. D preamble: "Inputs: the cert, the log location (a URL or clone) and the log public key, published together with the cert." Add an optional envelope field `"log": { "log_id": "...", "url": "..." }` (decide and state whether it is inside the id preimage); for action certs add `body.sublog_id` and require the hourly roll cert to carry the sub-log's URL.
- sources: stranger-reproducibility, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed: the §2 envelope has exactly styxx/type/id/created/issuer/subject/recipe/body/refs/sig — no log id, log URL, or log-key reference; §8.1 STHs carry a `log_id` the spec never assigns; §8.2 names GitHub Pages but no URL; §10's action certs need a sub-log root that no field names; App. D says "Fetch the STH; verify the inclusion proof" and "If any step is impossible from the cert alone, the cert is defective and the spec has failed." Nothing elsewhere fixes the log location (README has "the four verification commands, nothing else"). As written, step 2 is unsatisfiable from the cert alone for every cert, and for action certs the sub-log is unlocatable even with the main log known. This is the spec's own acceptance test contradicting its envelope; major is right (it is a definitional defect, not a wrong verdict).

### S2-14 · major · §2 `issuer`; §2.1; §8.2 `keys/log.pub`; §8.3; §8.4; §9; §15.1; App. D steps 1-2
**Issuer identity is a bare key with a free-text name and no trust root, rotation or revocation; 'independent issuers' is Sybil-trivial**

- claim: `issuer.key` is embedded in the cert it signs and `keys/log.pub` lives in the repo whose STHs it signs, so a stranger can only confirm that someone with some key signed it; `issuer.name` is unauthenticated; there is no registry of which keys CI accepts, no mechanism to rotate, delegate or revoke a key, and §9 defines issuer independence as key distinctness with self-reported hardware.
- evidence: §2: `"issuer": { "name": "fathom lab", "key": "ed25519:<base64url pubkey>" }`; §2.1: "Verify = recompute id, check sig against `issuer.key`."; §8.3: "v0: fathom's key only" (no location for that allowlist in §8.2's tree); §15.1: "The Alienware should hold a delegated signing key that can be rotated" — no cert type or file expresses delegation or rotation; §9: "**Disputed** status (client-computed): a cert with ≥ 2 unresolved challenges above the target's own floor from independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)." — hardware is self-reported in `body.environment`; memory `charon-v01-ferry-log.md`: "The chain binds order and content **to the HEAD**. `--expect-head` is the whole defence and the head must be pinned outside the log."
- consequence: Step 1 accepts forged certs with `issuer.name: "fathom lab"` under a fresh key and the compliance view displays them as fathom's; step 2 cannot detect a log-key rotation that re-signs a rewritten tree with a new `log.pub` — exactly the v0.1 attack the lab already recorded; one party with two keypairs marks any cert Disputed; if the lab key leaks or the Alienware key is retired, no cert can say so and every past and future signature by it stays 'valid' forever; CI for v0 has nowhere specified to look up the accepted issuer key.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED. Minimum: state that issuer identity IS the key (`name` is a display hint, never matched); add `charon/keys/issuers.json` (the v0 allowlist CI reads) and have step 1 check that `issuer.key` appears in the log's issuer roster entry (a roster cert signed by the log key, id fixed in this spec) or report `unknown issuer`; pin the log public key's fingerprint in this spec's text (entry #0) and in the README and require `styxx log mirror --expect-log-key <fp>`; define rotation/revocation as log entries — a `result` cert with `body.kind = "key"` signed by the old key naming the new key (rotation) or itself (revocation), reffing the old key's last STH and effective from the STH that includes it; validators treat certs whose issuer key is revoked and whose inclusion STH postdates the revocation as invalid. Options for Disputed: (a) replace "independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)" with "two challenges from distinct issuer keys; independence of issuers and hardware is self-reported and displayed as such"; (b) count only challenger keys that have an included fingerprint cert of their own older than the target; (c) require out-of-band key registration (GitHub account or DNS TXT) before a key's challenges count.
- sources: crypto-envelope, stranger-reproducibility
- verifiers: STANDS: All citations check out: `issuer` is a free-text name plus an embedded key; §2.1 verifies only against that embedded key; §8.3's "fathom's key only" allowlist has no home in §8.2's tree; §15.1 wants a rotatable delegated key but no cert type or file expresses rotation/revocation; §9 defines issuer independence by distinct keys and hardware that is self-reported in `body.environment`; memory `charon-v01-ferry-log.md` does say the chain binds to the HEAD and the head must be pinned outside the log. The spec puts the log key inside the repository it signs and pins its fingerprint nowhere, which is the same class of defect the lab already recorded for charon v0.1; Disputed status is Sybil-trivial by its literal definition; revoked/leaked keys stay valid forever. §15.1 acknowledging custody as open does not resolve any of this. Major stands; the amendment is correctly marked OPERATOR-GATED.

### S2-15 · major · §2 `refs`; §2.3 `battery`; §3.1 `noise_floor.runs`; §4.5; §7.2 `evidence`; §8.1; §8.3
**Cert ids embedded in recipe/body are outside `refs`, nothing makes append conditional on refs resolving, and a redacted battery can be referenced by a logged fingerprint**

- claim: `recipe.battery`, `body.noise_floor.runs`, `body.selected_against` and promotion `evidence` are cert ids that live outside `refs` with no rule on which list is authoritative, no rule makes log append conditional on those ids already being logged or of the right type, and §4.5 allows a redacted battery to be referenced by a logged fingerprint — contradicting §8.1's promise that the recipe stays complete.
- evidence: §2: `"refs": ["sha256:<id of any cert this depends on or responds to>"]`; §2.3: `"battery": "sha256:<battery cert id>"` — not in `refs`; §3.1: `"noise_floor": { "runs": ["sha256:…", "…"] ...}`; §4.5: `"selected_against": "sha256:<reference fingerprint cert id>"` and "A redacted battery cannot be used for a public claim — only a black-box \"internal\" fingerprint."; §7.2: `"evidence": ["sha256:<prereg>", ...]`; §8.1: "Redacted certs store hashes of outputs, never the outputs; the recipe stays complete."; §8.3 v0 CI: "validates schema, recomputes id, checks signature, appends" — no ref resolution; §5.1.4: "Every run is logged as its own fingerprint cert."
- consequence: A fingerprint whose `recipe.battery` names a cert that is not in the log (or is a `fingerprint`, not a `battery`, or is redacted) is appended and `verify --diff` reports comparability on a string match alone with no battery ever fetched, so a stranger cannot obtain the prompts and step 3 fails on a cert the log accepted; a noise floor can cite run certs that do not exist, satisfying §5.2 row 1 without any runs; validity checks (§7.1 'valid promotion') read one list and a forger fills the other; a stranger handed one of the R−1 non-canonical run certs has no `noise_floor` and exits 2 with no indication which sibling is canonical.
- amendment: Add to §2: "Every `sha256:` cert id appearing anywhere in `subject`, `recipe`, or `body` MUST also appear in `refs` with a role (`recipe.battery`, every `noise_floor.runs` id, `selected_against`, promotion evidence); validators check `refs` only — body fields are labels for position; a validator rejects a cert whose embedded ids are not a subset of `refs`." §8.3: "CI rejects an append whose `refs` do not all resolve to already-logged entries of the required type (dependency order is enforced; exception: `challenge`, whose `own` ref may be appended in the same PR), and rejects any fingerprint whose battery cert has `redacted: true`." §4.5: replace "internal fingerprint" with "a fingerprint over a redacted battery must not be submitted to charon". §3.1: add `"canonical": true|false`; non-canonical run certs issued after the canonical one carry `"noise_floor": { "canonical": "<id of canonical cert>" }`, otherwise state that the stranger locates the canonical cert via `styxx log prove`.
- sources: crypto-envelope, stranger-reproducibility, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: `recipe.battery`, `noise_floor.runs`, `selected_against` and promotion `evidence` are cert ids outside `refs`; §8.3 v0 CI "validates schema, recomputes id, checks signature, appends" with no ref resolution; §4.5 allows a redacted battery under an "internal" fingerprint and §5.1.4 logs every run as its own fingerprint cert. Part of the finding is by design — principle 5 and §8.3 say the log stores and does not endorse, so non-resolving refs at append time are not a hole per se, and validators are already told to check refs for challenges. What is material: a logged fingerprint over a redacted battery fails App. D step 3 for a stranger, and the R−1 non-canonical run certs carry no floor and no pointer to the canonical sibling, so a stranger handed one exits 2 at step 4 — both are certs the spec allows into the log that fail its own acceptance test, and §12 would still project them into the documentation category. Major is warranted on those two points; the refs-superset rule is a minor clarification.

### S2-16 · major · §2; §7.1; §7.2; §9; §10
**`refs` are untyped and positional; nothing obliges a validator to check the referenced certs' types**

- claim: Every cross-cert relationship is expressed as a bare `sha256:` id in an ordered list with meaning assigned by position or prose, and no rule says the validator must resolve each ref and check its `type`/`body.kind`, or that a result and the promotion citing it name the same prereg.
- evidence: §2: `"refs": ["sha256:<id of any cert this depends on or responds to>"]`. §7.2: "**result** — refs its prereg" / "**promotion** — refs prereg + result". §9: `"refs": ["sha256:<target cert>", "sha256:<challenger's own fingerprint cert>"]`. §7.1: "'certified' if a valid promotion cert covering that triple is in charon" — "valid" is defined in §2.1 only as recompute-or-signature. Promotion also carries a second, duplicate list `"evidence": ["sha256:<prereg>", "sha256:<result>", "sha256:<robustness result>"]` that can disagree with `refs`.
- consequence: A promotion whose `refs` point at a `pilot` result, at a `result` whose `refs` do not point at the same prereg, at the reveal cert but not the sealed prereg, or at a `challenge` cert, passes §2.1 validation and turns an instrument 'certified' in §7.1. A challenge with its two refs swapped targets the challenger's own cert. A result whose prereg was issued after its raw-data hashes exists in the log with no rule against it. Since `refs` order is inside the digest, positional meaning is frozen into ids, so a future reordering rule is a format break.
- amendment: Change `refs` to a list of `{"role": "<prereg|result|robustness|target|own|parent|fingerprint|sealed|battery|run|selected_against>", "id": "sha256:..."}` objects, delete promotion `evidence` (redundant with roled refs), and add to §2.1: "A validator MUST resolve every ref, check that its `type` (and `body.kind` where the role requires one) matches the role, and check that a `result` and the `promotion` that cites it name the same prereg id; failure is rejection at append and at `verify`."
- sources: crypto-envelope
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually exact: §2 defines refs as a bare ordered list of ids, §2.1 defines validity only as recompute-id-plus-signature, §7.1 grants 'certified' to any 'valid promotion cert covering that triple', and §7.2/§9/§10 assign meaning to refs purely by position or prose; the promotion body carries a second untyped `evidence` list that nothing binds to `refs`. No passage requires a validator to resolve a ref, check its type or `body.kind`, require the cited result to be `confirmatory`, or require result and promotion to name the same prereg. A promotion citing a `pilot` result (or a result bound to a different prereg) therefore passes every stated check and moves readings into the certified section, which is exactly what non-goal 3 forbids. The 'positional order frozen into ids' consequence is real but secondary; the certified-tier hole alone is major.

### S2-17 · major · §2.2; §2.3; App. A.1; App. A.2
**`chat_template_sha256`/`system_prompt_sha256` have no stated preimage, and A.2 is ambiguous on tokenizer files and omits `tokenizer_config.json`/`generation_config.json`, which change greedy output without a recipe mismatch**

- claim: Two of the four comparability keys are hashes of unspecified bytes; no weights-hash convention exists in the repo today so A.2 is new, and as written two implementers hash different files for gemma-2 (ships both tokenizer.json and tokenizer.model) and neither hashes `tokenizer_config.json` (where HF chat templates live) or `generation_config.json`, whose eos/stop and default sampling fields transformers applies at generate time.
- evidence: §2.3: `"chat_template_sha256": "...", "system_prompt_sha256": "..."` with no preimage; App. A.2: "Hash `config.json` and `tokenizer.json` (or `tokenizer.model`) separately." — no other files; §2.3 comparability excludes subject fields; §3.2 `exact` "greedy output token ids per item". Repo: papers/depth-truth/INSTRUMENT_FREEZE.json `"model": {"hf_id": "google/gemma-2-2b", "revision": "main"}` (no digest); `grep -rn safetensors --include=*.py .` → no hashing code anywhere; experiments/glimmer REPORT pins a GGUF by filename only.
- consequence: Two harnesses hash the Jinja string vs the file vs the rendered prompt and exit 3 against each other on identical models; two fingerprints of identical weights disagree on `tokenizer_sha256` and are reported as different subjects; a provider revision that edits only generation_config.json (new eos_token_id list) flips `exact` to `drift` while every hashed field matches.
- amendment: A.1: "`chat_template_sha256` = sha256(UTF-8 of the Jinja template string as loaded, no trailing newline); `system_prompt_sha256` = sha256(UTF-8 of the system prompt string; sha256('') when none)." A.2: "Hash, in fixed order, every file in {config.json, generation_config.json, tokenizer.json, tokenizer.model, tokenizer_config.json, special_tokens_map.json, chat_template.jinja} that exists, as (filename, digest) pairs into one `tokenizer_config_sha256` (listed under `subject.aux_sha256`); record which existed. `weights_sha256` covers *.safetensors and *.gguf; any *.bin present is a refusal (subject not certifiable)." Add `subject.weights_sha256`, `config_sha256`, `tokenizer_config_sha256` to the exit-3 comparability set for `weights` subjects (a different subject is never `drift`, it is `identity`).
- sources: codebase-reconciliation, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed: §2.3 lists `chat_template_sha256`/`system_prompt_sha256` with no preimage anywhere in the spec, and comparability (exit 3) hinges on their exact equality; A.2 hashes only config.json and 'tokenizer.json (or tokenizer.model)'. The HF cache on this box shows gemma-2-2b-it ships both tokenizer.json and tokenizer.model plus tokenizer_config.json (where the chat template lives), generation_config.json and special_tokens_map.json, so two honest implementers can legitimately hash different files. Repo checks agree with the evidence: no weights-hashing code exists (`grep safetensors --include=*.py` finds only loader flags), INSTRUMENT_FREEZE.json pins `revision: main` with no digest, and the glimmer REPORT pins a GGUF by filename. Two harnesses disagreeing on a hash preimage yields exit 3 on identical models, a wrong CI verdict; the generation_config-flips-to-drift scenario is plausible but the preimage gap alone carries the finding.

### S2-18 · major · §2.3; §7.1; §7.2; Appendix D
**The recipe's `sae` block cannot describe the depth instrument as frozen, and depth's code lives outside this repository — recipe-completeness fails for the only instrument the spec names**

- claim: INSTRUMENT_FREEZE pins depth to circuit_tracer over `gemma-scope-2b-pt-transcoders` across all layers with the metric function in `clawd/research` at fc6f2c3; the spec's recipe offers a single-layer residual SAE (`layer: 12, width: 16k`) and requires `instrument.code_sha256` of bytes that are not in styxx.
- evidence: §2.3: `"sae": { "repo": "google/gemma-scope-2b-pt-res", "layer": 12, "width": "16k", "revision": "..." }` and §1.2 "A cert contains everything needed to re-run it"; §7.2 promotion body `"instrument": "depth", "version": "1.2.0", "code_sha256": "…"`. Repo: papers/depth-truth/INSTRUMENT_FREEZE.json `"depth_metric": {"symbol": "get_mean_depth", "file": "research/experiment_12_power.py", "git_repo": "clawd/research", "git_sha": "fc6f2c3", "definition": "mean of circuit_tracer.attribute(... max_feature_nodes=500 ...).active_features[:, 0]"}`, `"sae": {"hf_repo": "google/gemma-scope-2b-pt-transcoders", "loader": "circuit_tracer 'gemma' preset ...", "circuit_tracer_git_sha": "6d64f60", "layers": "preset default (all layers)"}`; papers/depth-truth/harness/signals.py:15 "The `depth` signal (§4) is NOT computed here: it comes from `get_mean_depth`"; TICKET_readme_truth_in_advertising: "`research/` (the `get_mean_depth` origin) ... **not in this repo**".
- consequence: Appendix D step 4 (`styxx verify --ref`) is impossible from a depth-bearing cert alone; no depth version '1.2.0' exists to hash; §7.1's computed tier would be evaluated over a code_sha256 nobody can recompute.
- amendment: §2.3: make `sae` an open object with required `loader`, `repo`, `revision`, `layers` (list or "all"), `attribution` (tool name + git sha + params) and forbid single-layer defaults; §7.1: "an instrument is registrable only if its code is in this repository or vendored under instruments/<id>/ with its own sha256; depth is registered only after `get_mean_depth` and its circuit_tracer pin are vendored".
- sources: codebase-reconciliation
- verifiers: STANDS: INSTRUMENT_FREEZE.json says exactly what is cited: depth = `get_mean_depth` in `research/experiment_12_power.py` at clawd/research@fc6f2c3, SAE = `google/gemma-scope-2b-pt-transcoders` via circuit_tracer@6d64f60 over all layers; harness/signals.py line 15 and TICKET_readme_truth_in_advertising both say the depth code is not in this repo. The spec's only recipe shape for `sae` is a single residual SAE (`gemma-scope-2b-pt-res`, `layer: 12`, `width: 16k`) with no loader/attribution/layers-list fields, and §7.2 freezes a `code_sha256` for bytes a stranger cannot obtain. §14 schedules the keystone v2 prereg as charon entry #2 in weeks 1-4 and a depth promotion in weeks 8-12, so this is on the v8.0 path, not hypothetical; a depth-bearing cert would fail §1.2 recipe-completeness and Appendix D step 4. Major rather than blocker because the fingerprint layer (the acceptance-test object) does not depend on it and no depth cert exists yet.

### S2-21 · major · §2.2; §4.4; §7.1; §7.2
**Tier lookup needs `model_family` and a `task_family`, neither of which exists on any subject or in a shared vocabulary**

- claim: `tier` is 'computed from the log' on a triple whose second element is not in the subject and whose third element uses a different vocabulary from the battery families.
- evidence: §7.1: "tier(instrument, model_family, task_family)"; "never set by hand". §2.2 weights subject has `hf_repo`, `revision`, hashes — no `model_family`; alias subject has `provider`, `alias`. §7.2 scope: `"model_family": "gemma-2", "task_family": "short-answer factual QA"`. §4.4 families: "recall / short reasoning / instruction-following / format / refusal boundary".
- consequence: `styxx attest` cannot compute a tier without a hand-set mapping from `google/gemma-2-2b` to `gemma-2` and from `recall` to `short-answer factual QA` — exactly the 'set by hand' the spec forbids.
- amendment: Add required `subject.model_family` (string) to both subject kinds; define a single enum `task_family ∈ {recall, short-reasoning, instruction-following, format, refusal-boundary}` used by §4.4, §7.2 `scope`, and §7.1; tier match = exact string equality on both.
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed against the text: §7.1 computes tier over (instrument, model_family, task_family) and forbids setting it by hand; neither §2.2 subject shape has a `model_family`; §7.2's scope uses 'gemma-2' and 'short-answer factual QA' while §4.4/§4.5 use recall / short reasoning / instruction-following / format / refusal boundary; no shared enum exists in the spec or the repo (grep for model_family/task_family finds nothing). `styxx attest --subject <spec>` therefore cannot decide whether a promotion covers the subject and the reading's task without an invented mapping, and two implementations can disagree, one placing a reading in the certified section with no covering promotion, which non-goal 3 and principle 4 forbid. Not resolved elsewhere; major.

### S2-22 · major · §2.3; §3.1; §3.2; App. B
**topk is 'the first 8 answer positions' but `stop`/`max_new_tokens` can end an answer earlier; App. B then averages over positions that do not exist**

- claim: With `stop: ["\n"]` many answers are shorter than 8 tokens, the body example hardcodes positions 0..7, and the topk distance has no rule for unequal position counts.
- evidence: §2.3: `"max_new_tokens": 64, "stop": ["\n"]`; §3.2: "top-5 log-probs at the first 8 answer positions per item"; §3.1: `"… positions 0..7"`; App. B: "mean over items and positions of the L1 distance between top-k vectors".
- consequence: A one-token answer on one side and a nine-token answer on the other has no defined distance; implementations will silently pad, truncate or skip, each giving a different number.
- amendment: §3.2: "top-5 log-probs at positions 0..min(7, len−1) where len is the generated length before stop". App. B topk: "positions present on both sides are compared; a position present on one side only contributes the maximum per-position distance (5 · 20 nats); the count of such positions is reported as `topk.length_mismatch`."
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §2.3 fixes `"max_new_tokens": 64, "stop": ["\n"]`, §3.2 says 'top-5 log-probs at the first 8 answer positions per item', §3.1 hardcodes '… positions 0..7', and App. B's topk row only defines union-of-vocab alignment for tokens ('a token absent from one side is assigned lp = −20') — nothing in §3, §5, §6 or App. B says what happens when the two sides have different position counts. §6's exit-2 rule covers 'a channel absent on one side', not positions missing within a channel. For an alias subject a stopped answer has no further logprobs, so padding/truncating/skipping are all live implementer choices that change the topk distance and therefore the per-channel verdict. Material and unresolved elsewhere; major stands.

### S2-23 · major · §2.1; §8; App. D; §14
**No second implementation, conformance vectors, or JS verifier for certs and proofs — the lab's own tradition for every claim-carrying format is absent**

- claim: The spec's acceptance test asks a stranger to recompute ids, signatures, and Merkle proofs, but plans only the Python implementation; the lab's own sworn format shipped 1689 conformance vectors, a mutation-coverage measurement, and a browser verifier before it called itself verifiable, and the spec never mentions sworn at all.
- evidence: App. D: "1. Recompute the id from the canonical bytes; check the signature. 2. Fetch the STH; verify the inclusion proof." §14 lists no vectors, no second verifier, no test strategy. Repo: `conformance/sworn/README.md`: "the precondition for any second verifier; no claim"; `styxx/_data/sworn_verify.js` (53,025 bytes); memory `styxx-leg1-receipt-binding-2026-09-05.md`: "a 2nd implementation agreeing on 1689/1689 vectors"; memory `differential-needs-calibration.md`: "an agreement number is worthless without measuring the generator's detection power". SCOUT.md: "The v8 spec never mentions sworn."
- consequence: A stranger's only way to run App. D is `pip install styxx` — the implementation under test verifies itself; a JCS or Merkle bug is invisible until an outsider writes a verifier, and the 8.0 gate ('someone who isn't flobi') then measures the bug, not the log.
- amendment: Add to §14 weeks 4–8: "`conformance/charon/`: vectors for JCS id, signature, leaf/node hash, inclusion and consistency proofs (positive and refusal cases), generated by the recorder pattern of `conformance/sworn/gen_vectors.py`, with a mutation catalogue committed before the run; `styxx/_data/charon_verify.js` replays them in the browser." State in §2 that the eight-type envelope and the sworn document format coexist and how a sworn RESULT references a cert id.
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: All cited facts check out: `conformance/sworn/README.md` line 4 reads 'the precondition for any second verifier; no claim', `styxx/_data/sworn_verify.js` is 53,025 bytes, `conformance/sworn/gen_vectors.py` and `mutation_catalogue.json` exist, memory `styxx-leg1-receipt-binding` records '1689/1689 in-scope conformance vectors, 0 disagreements', and `differential-needs-calibration` states the agreement-number rule. The spec never mentions sworn; §14 lists no vectors, second verifier, or test strategy, and the 8.0 gate ('four `log verify-*` commands pass on a fresh mirror clone by someone who isn't flobi') runs the same implementation against itself. Under §1 principle 1 ('same id, anywhere') and App. D, a JCS or Merkle bug in the sole implementation is invisible to the acceptance test.
