Also §7.2 defines a `result` cert while SCOUT confirms every RESULT in `papers/` is currently a sworn document — an unaddressed coexistence. Major stands (planning gap plus an unbacked verifiability claim), not blocker since no number is wrong on its face.

### S2-24 · major · §2; §7.1; §7.3
**No schema versioning or migration rule between 8.0 and 8.1 although 8.1 changes promotion semantics**

- claim: The envelope has a `styxx: "8.0"` string but no rule for an 8.1 verifier reading 8.0 certs, an 8.0 verifier meeting an 8.1 cert, or whether `tier()` computed under 8.1 (discrimination-threshold gate) still honours 8.0 promotion certs that passed only the disclosure rule.
- evidence: §2: "\"styxx\": \"8.0\"". §7.3: "v8.0: a robustness result is **required** for promotion (disclosure). v8.1: promotion additionally requires discrimination under attack ≥ a threshold set in the prereg (gate)." §7.1: "tier ... computed from the log — never set by hand". Lab doctrine (memory `styxx-calibration-leg-2026-09-05.md`): "a moved core is a FORMAT change"; "Verdict vocabulary changes are operator-gated".
- consequence: On the 8.1 release every `certified` reading either silently stays certified (the gate is theatre) or silently becomes `lab` (a committed promotion cert's meaning was rewritten in place — the exact failure `a-receipt-is-history-too.md` records).
- amendment: Add §2.4 'Versioning': "`styxx` is the schema version; verifiers accept `major.minor` ≤ their own and refuse higher with exit 3. A promotion cert's tier is computed under the rules of the schema version it names; 8.1 rules apply only to promotions issued as 8.1. Any change to a distance function, score formula, or tier rule is a new `styxx` version, never an in-place change."
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Spec says exactly what is quoted: §2 has only `"styxx": "8.0"`, §7.3 changes promotion from disclosure (8.0) to a gate (8.1), and §7.1 defines tier() as 'a valid promotion cert covering that triple is in charon' with no rule for which version's validity test applies. The spec does have the right pattern elsewhere — §4 'old versions stay valid for old certs' and App. C 'Changing them makes canary-v2; it does not silently alter v1' — but only for batteries and canary scoring, never for the schema or tier rule, which confirms the gap rather than closing it. Memory `styxx-calibration-leg-2026-09-05.md` lines 43–44 confirm 'adding a field there is a FORMAT change' / 'a moved core' and its description line says verdict rename is OPERATOR-GATED. Since the spec itself specifies 8.1 semantics, the failure is in scope: certified-section output for existing promotions is undefined at the 8.1 boundary. Major stands.

### S3-04 · major · §3.2; App. B
**topk distance is a nonlinear function of exact flips: positions after a divergent greedy token are conditioned on different prefixes, and the −20 fill makes rank-boundary swaps dominate**

- claim: Top-5 vectors at positions 1..7 are taken under each side's own greedy prefix, so after any prefix flip the compared distributions are over different contexts, and each token that leaves the top-5 contributes ≈2·(20−|lp|) ≈ 30-38 L1 regardless of how small the underlying probability change was; items shorter than 8 tokens have undefined positions.
- evidence: §3.2: "**topk** — top-5 log-probs at the first 8 answer positions per item." App. B: "mean over items and positions of the L1 distance between top-k vectors after union-of-vocab alignment; a token absent from one side is assigned lp = −20". §2.3 `"stop": ["\n"]` with `max_new_tokens: 64` — many short-answer items end in <8 tokens. §6: exit 2 when "a channel absent on one side".
- consequence: The channel's noise floor is set by rank-5/6 near-ties (common, since the fifth token typically has lp ≈ −3…−6 and a close competitor), each swap adding ~34/(8·256) ≈ 0.017 to the mean at N=256, while a genuine uniform shift of every top-5 log-prob by 0.05 nats adds only ≈ 0.03 — the same order — so the floor's max is dominated by discrete swap events and the channel's power against smooth distribution shift is unmeasured. A single top-1 flip at position 0 cascades into up to 7 positions of context-mismatched distance. Items with 3-token outputs leave positions 3..7 missing on one or both sides: either exit 2 for the whole channel or an undefined mean. The fill value −20 is arbitrary for weights subjects, where the true log-prob of the absent token is available.
- amendment: (1) For weights subjects, compute topk teacher-forced on the REFERENCE greedy prefix (store `prefix_token_ids` per item) so positions compare the same context; for alias subjects state that topk is conditioned on each side's own prefix and is therefore not comparable after a flip — mask positions after the first divergent token and report `positions_compared`. (2) Define positions as `min(8, len(output))` and the mean over compared positions only; channel is `absent` only if zero positions exist. (3) For weights subjects use the true lp of the absent token (available from the full distribution); keep −20 only for aliases and record it as `topk.fill_lp` in the cert. (4) Optionally bound the per-position distance (e.g. total-variation over the aligned probabilities) so one swap cannot dominate the mean.
- sources: noise-floor-statistics
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually confirmed: §3.2 "top-5 log-probs at the first 8 answer positions per item", §3.1 schema "positions 0..7", §2.3 `stop: ["\n"]`, App. B "mean over items and positions ... a token absent from one side is assigned lp = −20". Nothing in the spec defines the distance when an item's greedy output has fewer than 8 tokens or when the two sides' outputs differ in length (common for short-answer items and for every item that flips), so two implementations legitimately produce different topk distances for the same certs — an irreproducible number the stranger's verdict depends on, contrary to §1.1/§3.3. The prefix-conditioning cascade and the −20 rank-swap dominance are design weaknesses (the floor absorbs them mechanically but the channel's power is unmeasured, which doctrine `differential-needs-calibration.md` — "Sample size is not power" — treats as a claim without a receipt); the undefined short-output/length-mismatch handling is the concrete defect. Not a blocker because §6 still yields a verdict for the other channels.

### S3-05 · major · §3.2 exact; §4 fixed-v1; §4.4; §5.1; App. B
**Cross-hardware greedy nondeterminism on `exact` and fixed-v1 has no receipt, yet the stranger's verdict rests entirely on it**

- claim: Canary-v1 excludes items that flip under the issuer's nuisance sweep, but fixed-v1 is drawn from styxx-bench with no nuisance filtering, and the nuisance sweep itself covers one GPU on this lab's box, so the rate at which a stranger's driver/kernel path flips a greedy token on the public battery is an unmeasured number the whole `exact` verdict depends on.
- evidence: §4: "fixed-v1 — public, model-agnostic, ~512 items, drawn from styxx-bench after the gold fix" (no δ2 exclusion); §4.4.1 applies `flip2` exclusion to canary only; §4.2 δ2: "second GPU if available"; SCOUT: "One GPU (per memory)", "'gold fix' ... NOT FOUND anywhere in the repo"; memory `differential-needs-calibration.md`: "Sample size is not power."
- consequence: A stranger reproducing a fixed-v1 fingerprint can see `exact` distance of a few percent from kernel-level differences alone, above a floor that was measured at 0 because the issuer's runs shared one GPU; `drift` is reported for an unchanged model, and the docket's predecessor/quant-delta numbers (§13.3-4) carry the same unmeasured term.
- amendment: §4 fixed-v1: apply the same δ2 exclusion (`flip2(i) == 0`) to fixed-v1 candidates on at least two (gpu, driver) pairs, and record `pool_nuisance_span` in the battery cert; §5.1: a public fingerprint's `noise_floor` must record `hardware_span` (set of distinct gpu/driver pairs), and `verify` prints `floor measured on N hardware configurations` beside every verdict. §14 Weeks 1-4: add "receipt: cross-hardware exact-mismatch rate on fixed-v1 for gemma-2-2b (this box vs Linux CI or a rented GPU)" as the number cited by §5 before any `drift` verdict is issued publicly.
- sources: stranger-reproducibility
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed: §4.4's `flip2` exclusion is scoped to canary-v1 selection; §4 fixed-v1 is "drawn from styxx-bench after the gold fix" with no nuisance filtering (and SCOUT records "gold fix" NOT FOUND in the repo); §4.2 δ2 and §5.1 vary hardware only "if available"/"where available", and SCOUT records one GPU on the lab box. So the floor a stranger is judged against (§5.2, App. D step 5) is measured with zero hardware variation, while §1.3 permits a drift claim only above "what re-running the same model produces" — a stranger's driver/kernel path is part of that null and is unmeasured. §5.3 ("What the floor does not cover") does not list this, so the spec does not even disclose the limit. A `drift` on an unchanged model across hardware is a false claim and the §13.3-4 docket numbers carry the same unmeasured term; `differential-needs-calibration.md` ("Sample size is not power") is the doctrine it violates. §9 lets the issuer answer with a new floor after the fact, which mitigates but does not make the original verdict correct.

### S3-07 · major · §3.1; App. B
**resid per-item profile is optional but the resid distance is defined per item; L is undefined**

- claim: If `profile` is omitted the resid distance cannot be computed; the lens distance divides by `L`, which no cert field records.
- evidence: §3.1: `"resid":    { "profile": [[..L..] per item, optional], "mean": [..L..] }`; App. B resid: "mean over items of the Jensen–Shannon divergence between normalized per-layer residual-norm profiles"; App. B lens: "mean over items of |Δ converge_layer| / L". No `n_layers`/`L` field in §2.2 or §3.1.
- consequence: A cert with only `mean` is accepted by schema and then fails at `verify` with no rule saying what to do; two models with different layer counts compared on fixed-v1 have two different L.
- amendment: Make `profile` required whenever the resid channel is present (drop 'optional'); add `"n_layers": L` under `channels.resid` and `channels.lens`; App. B lens: "L = the reference cert's `n_layers`; if the two certs' `n_layers` differ, the lens channel is `inconclusive`."
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually confirmed: §3.1 marks `resid.profile` "optional" while App. B defines resid as "mean over items of the Jensen–Shannon divergence between normalized per-layer residual-norm profiles", which cannot be computed from `mean` alone — an internal contradiction (§3.3 permits exactly one distance function, so computing JSD over the means is not an allowed substitute). `L` in the lens distance appears nowhere in §2.2/§3.1; it is derivable from the subject's config for a single model, but §13.4 mandates `verify --diff` predecessor diffs on fixed-v1 (e.g. gemma-2-2b vs -9b, different layer counts), where the spec gives no rule for which L to use, so two implementations produce different lens numbers for a docket entry. The profile-omitted case would likely be handled by §1.2 refusal, but the L ambiguity yields an irreproducible number, which is the doctrine's definition of a claim without a receipt.

### S3-08 · major · §3.2; §10; §13
**Docket item 5 'instrument readings' has no cert type; `lens` is both a channel and a lab reading**

- claim: No defined type carries instrument readings except `action`, which is agent-bound; the §10 example lists `lens` (a §3.2 channel) beside `depth` (a §7.4 instrument).
- evidence: §13.5: "**instrument readings** — every registered instrument, in the tier the log assigns." §13: "Contents, all as certs in charon". §10: `"readings": { "certified": {}, "lab": { "depth": 13.7, "lens": 12 } }`. §3.2: "**lens** — logit-lens convergence layer ... Cheap proxy for computational depth." §7.1: instruments are plugins with `id`, `version`, `code_sha256`.
- consequence: Docket readings cannot be logged (no type validates), and `attest` cannot decide whether `lens` is a checksum channel (never 'lab') or an instrument (tier-gated).
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED: (a) add `result` `kind = reading` with body `{ instrument, version, code_sha256, tier, tier_basis: <promotion id | null>, readings }`; or (b) reuse `action` with `action_kind: docket`. Either way state: "`lens` and `resid` are channels, never instrument readings; remove `lens` from the §10 example."
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §13 item 5 requires instrument readings 'as certs in charon', but §7.2 defines only prereg/result/promotion, `result.kind` is closed to {confirmatory, pilot, robustness, verify, response}, and the only remaining candidate `eval` (§2 envelope) has no body defined anywhere in §3–§10, so schema validation 'for all eight types' (§14) cannot be written for a docket reading. The §10 example does list `lens` under `readings.lab` while §3.2 defines `lens` as a white-box fingerprint channel and §7.4's instrument table does not register it, so `attest` has no rule for which section it belongs in. Not a blocker: fingerprint verdicts and App. D are unaffected; it is a missing schema for a §13 deliverable plus a contradiction, i.e. major.

### S4-03 · major · §4.2–§4.5; §2.3; §1.2; App. C; App. D
**The selection chain has no logged starting point and no logged δ-runs: the pool is not a battery kind, the reference fingerprint cannot be issued, and `flip2` can launder any exclusion**

- claim: Canary selection runs on the pool, but a fingerprint cert's `recipe.battery` must be a battery cert id and `body.kind` admits only `canary-v1 | fixed-v1`, so the fingerprint that `selected_against` names has no issuable battery; and the battery cert asserts per-item `flip1/flip2/flip3/margin` without referencing the fingerprint certs of the δ-variant runs, so a stranger cannot recompute the selection and an issuer can exclude every revealing item as 'nuisance'.
- evidence: §4.5: `"kind": "canary-v1 | fixed-v1"`, `"selected_against": "sha256:<reference fingerprint cert id>  (canary only)"`, `"pool_sha256": "…", "pool_size": 12000`; items carry `"score": 0.71, "margin": 0.42, "flip1": 0.67, "flip2": 0.0, "flip3": 0.33` with `selected_against` as the only ref. §2.3: `"battery": "sha256:<battery cert id>"`; §1.2: "A cert contains everything needed to re-run it ... If it doesn't, `verify` refuses to compare and says so." §4.4.1: "**Exclude** every item with `flip2(i) > 0`. … This is the load-bearing step". §4.2 δ2: "second GPU if available". App. D: "If any step is impossible from the cert alone, the cert is defective and the spec has failed." Content addressing makes the obvious workaround (canary battery cites a fingerprint that cites the canary battery) impossible: a cert cannot contain its own dependent's id.
- consequence: Either `selected_against` points at a fingerprint over `fixed-v1` (not what the δ-sweep measured, so the battery misstates its provenance) or at a fingerprint whose `recipe.battery` names no cert (unverifiable under App. D step 3). Attack: (1) issuer has old weights W and planned W'; (2) the δ2 'second GPU' run is silently W'; (3) every item distinguishing W from W' gets flip2 > 0 and is excluded 'as nuisance'; (4) the battery cert logs with plausible scores; (5) ship W'; verify reads `same` by construction. No stranger can detect this because the δ-runs are not in the log and App. D step 4 cannot re-derive the battery from its cert.
- amendment: Add `kind: "pool-v1"` to the battery cert with `items` = the full pool (prompts in the clear unless redacted) and no `selected_against`; the reference fingerprint's `recipe.battery` names the pool cert; a canary battery's `selected_against` MUST resolve to a fingerprint whose `recipe.battery` is a `pool-v1` cert whose id equals the canary battery's own `pool` ref (rename `pool_sha256` to `pool: "sha256:<pool battery cert id>"`). §4.5: the battery cert `refs` MUST list the fingerprint cert id of every δ-variant run (each a logged fingerprint with its own `subject.precision`, `hardware`, decoding); `styxx battery select --from-certs <battery cert>` recomputes `items[*].{margin,flip1,flip2,flip3,score,role}` from those certs and the battery is `invalid` on any mismatch. Add to §4.4: "Every δ2 run must carry the same `weights_sha256` as the reference; a δ2 run whose subject hash differs voids the battery." Add to §4.6: "A canary battery bounds `same` claims to changes after the battery's log timestamp."
- sources: crypto-envelope, adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: The schema-validity half is refuted for the same reason as S2-07: `selected_against` names the fixed-v1 fingerprint of the reference subject (§13 step 1 then step 2), which is issuable, and since §4.2 says the sweep runs 'on the reference subject, reference precision', citing that subject's fingerprint does not misstate provenance — a canary battery can ship. What survives is recipe-completeness (§1.2, App. D 'from the cert alone'): the battery cert carries `pool_sha256` and per-item `flip1/flip2/flip3/margin` but neither the pool nor any reference to the δ-variant runs, and δ2's 'second GPU if available' is underdetermined, so a stranger cannot re-derive selection and the laundering attack is undetectable from the log. Author's defense: §8.6 explicitly places dishonest-issuer fabrication under 'mitigates, does not prevent' with reproduction as the remedy, and canary fingerprints themselves remain fully reproducible because items ship with prompts in the clear — so verification of the fingerprint is unharmed; only the battery's provenance is unauditable. That is a real §1.2 failure on one cert type, within the declared threat model, fixable by logging δ-runs as fingerprint certs and referencing them — major, not blocker.

### S4-04 · major · §4.2; §4.3; §4.5; §1.1; App. C
**|δ2|, the reference configuration and the δ3 seeds are undefined; eligibility (flip2 == 0) rests on 3–4 hardware-conditional Bernoulli trials and the selected battery is not reproducible**

- claim: δ2 is a prose list, not a variant set: the reference run's batch size and order are never stated, permuted order at batch 1 is a no-op, 'second GPU if available' makes |δ2| lab-dependent, δ3 is unseeded, and with k = 3 or 4 trials the pass event flip2 == 0 has probability (1−p)^k — an item with true nuisance flip rate 0.2 passes 51% (k=3) of the time.
- evidence: §4.2: "δ2 | nuisance: batch size ∈ {1, 8, 32}, permuted item order, second GPU if available"; "δ3 | temp 0.2, n=3 samples". App. C: "flip2(i) = |{ v ∈ δ2 : out_v(i) ≠ out_ref(i) }| / |δ2|" with |δ2| never given. §4.5 params record only `tau, w_flip, w_margin, n, k_anchors, max_family_share`. §4.4.1: "canaries must be stable under noise and sensitive to change". §15.4 concedes only N/K are guesses, not k. §1.1: "Same evidence, same id, anywhere." The lab box has one GPU (glimmer REPORT.md line 18: "RTX 4070 Laptop 8GB"), so "second GPU" is unavailable here and |δ2| = 3.
- consequence: Two implementers produce different eligible sets from the same pool and model (different |δ2|, different reference batch, different δ3 draws), so the battery cert id differs and §1.1 fails for the battery cert; nobody can check a battery cert from its own recipe (App. D). Statistically the selected battery is enriched for items whose true nuisance flip probability is well above zero: canaries are low-margin by construction so p_i is largest exactly on the battery; if fresh floor runs span the same nuisance space the floor rises to cover them and the sensitivity §4 promises is lost silently; nuisance axes the floor runs never span (hardware/driver) are not absorbed at all. Also infeasible as written: batch 32 × (prompt + 64 tokens) does not fit an 8 GB GPU for the 7B subjects in the HF cache.
- amendment: Define δ2 as an explicit list of configurations with the reference fixed: ref = (batch 1, canonical A.3 order, device 0); δ2 = [(8, canonical, d0), (32, canonical, d0), (8, permuted with `params.perm_seed`, d0), (8, canonical, d1 if present)]; GPU variation recorded under `params.delta2_extra` when used and its variants counted; record |δ2| and each variant's fingerprint cert id in `params`. δ3 = 3 samples with seeds `params.delta3_seeds = [s, s+1, s+2]`. Make eligibility a held-out test: eligibility from k_sel selection runs, but the `flip2` reported per item in the battery cert is recomputed from the R fresh floor runs (§5) so the published number is not the selected-on number. Add a documented fallback when batch 32 does not fit (record `max_batch_run`).
- sources: canary-selection, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Text checks out: §4.2 gives δ2 as a prose list with 'second GPU if available', App. C divides by |δ2| that is never fixed, §4.5 params omit it, the reference run's batch size is unstated, and δ3 has no seed beyond the recipe's greedy `seed: 7`; the lab has one GPU (glimmer REPORT.md setup table: 'RTX 4070 Laptop 8GB', nvidia-smi confirms 8188 MiB). The author's best defenses are real: A.3 fixes canonical item order (item_id ascending), the battery cert enumerates items in the clear so every fingerprint verdict and the App. D test are unaffected, the §5 floor is measured on the selected battery so items that slipped through k=3 trials with nonzero nuisance flip rate raise the floor rather than produce a wrong verdict (a power loss, not an error), and §15.4 already marks canary parameters as guesses to be frozen once. But §0 says every cert is reproducible by a stranger from its own recipe and the battery cert is a cert; as written two implementers on different labs produce different eligible sets and different battery-cert ids, and the per-item flip2 numbers in the cert cannot be rechecked. The batch-32 infeasibility is not a spec defect: the spec's named subject is gemma-2-2b (§2.2, §15.4), which fits at batch 32 on 8 GB, and the cached 7B models do not fit in bf16 at any batch size. Survives at major: an unreproducible selection recipe, no wrong verdict.

### S4-07 · major · §4; §7.4; §14 weeks 1–4; §15.3
**fixed-v1 '~512 items drawn from styxx-bench after the gold fix' cannot be built: styxx-bench has 84 task lines, a missing adversarial.jsonl, and no recorded 'gold fix', 'monday/tuesday' or 'strict-gate normalization'**

- claim: The week-1–4 deliverable 'fixed-v1 ... battery cert issued' has no source of 512 items (the named benchmark is one-sixth that size), and its stated precondition is documented nowhere in the repository.
- evidence: §4: "**fixed-v1** — public, model-agnostic, ~512 items, drawn from styxx-bench after the gold fix." §7.4: "| styxx-bench | — | becomes fixed-v1 after the monday/tuesday gold fix and strict-gate normalization |". §14: "fixed-v1 from styxx-bench after the gold fix; battery cert issued." §15.3: "Contamination is handled by yearly rotation". Repo: bench/suite.yaml:1-2 `schema_version: "1.0" name: styxx-bench`; `wc -l bench/tasks/*.jsonl` = 20 + 22 + 21 + 21 = 84 across creative/factual/reasoning/refusal; bench/suite.yaml and bench/README.md:54-59 list tasks/adversarial.jsonl ("21 prompts"), file absent; grep for "gold fix", "gold_fix", "strict-gate"/"strict_gate" → nothing in bench/ or papers/ (only papers/calib-poison-general/capability_battery.py:231, a weekday list, for monday/tuesday); SCOUT.md:18: "NOT FOUND anywhere ... 'gold fix'". bench/tasks is already public in the repo.
- consequence: Either fixed-v1 ships with 84 items (and the ≤25%-per-family stratification of §4.4 has 4 families of ~21) or weeks 1–4 silently author 400+ new items with no prereg of their construction — an unverifiable battery violating §1 principle 1 and 'every number cites a receipt'. The cross-model comparison layer and the day-zero docket cannot be built as written; a stranger cannot locate the defect the battery is said to be fixed after; every fingerprint referencing the battery cert is unspecifiable.
- amendment: Replace with: "fixed-v1 = the 84 committed styxx-bench items at blob <sha> plus items drawn by a committed script from named public datasets already cached (PopQA, ARC, MMLU, TruthfulQA ... with seed and exclusion list), target 512, with per-item provenance and a construction receipt in the battery cert body `pool`; styxx-bench may contribute at most its 84." Replace "after the gold fix" with a path to a committed defect record (open `bench/TICKET_gold_fix_<date>.md` naming the wrong labels before the spec cites it) or delete the phrase; same for "monday/tuesday" and "strict-gate normalization". Delete 12000/~512 until a pool exists.
- sources: codebase-reconciliation, doctrine-and-commitments, feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Repository confirms: bench/suite.yaml names styxx-bench, `wc -l bench/tasks/*.jsonl` = 20+22+21+21 = 84, tasks/adversarial.jsonl is listed in suite.yaml and README.md but has never existed in git history, and bench/ also lacks the runner.py/scorer.py its README describes; grep finds no 'gold fix', 'gold_fix' or 'strict-gate' anywhere. On the author's side: '~512' is explicitly approximate, §15.4 marks battery sizes as guesses, the reas-021 defect the phrase refers to is locatable (bench/tasks/reasoning.jsonl:21, gold 'Wednesday' for a Tuesday answer), and a 512-item pool is feasible from datasets already in the HF cache (PopQA, ai2_arc, mmlu, truthful_qa, trivia_qa), so the deliverable is buildable once the spec says how. But as written the source cannot supply the count, the precondition is recorded nowhere a stranger can find, and the §4.4 five-family stratification cannot be met from four 21-item families; that is an unbacked number and a repo contradiction under the 'every number cites a receipt' doctrine, not a wrong runtime verdict, since fingerprints are relative to whatever battery cert is actually issued. Major, with the reas-021 path as the defect record the spec should cite.

### S4-08 · major · §4.2 δ1; §2.2; §2.3; §13 step 3
**δ1 confounds precision with inference runtime: q8_0/q4_k_m are llama.cpp GGUF quant types while the reference runs under transformers**

- claim: q8_0 and q4_k_m are llama.cpp GGUF quantizations and transformers loads GGUF only by dequantizing, so the δ1 variants must run under a second runtime whose tokenizer, chat-template and batch-size semantics differ; flip1 then counts stack differences as 'representational change' while comparability (§2.3) ignores `runtime`.
- evidence: §4.2: "δ1 | precision: bf16 → q8_0 → q4_k_m (same weights)". §2.3: "runtime": { "framework": "transformers" }. §2.2 precision enum mixes "bf16 | fp16 | q8_0 | q4_k_m"; A.2: "A GGUF file is hashed whole and its quant type recorded in `precision`". Box: llama.cpp `llama-b10434` present; `gguf 0.19.0` installed; glimmer ran under llama-server (REPORT.md:18). Disk: 2.7 GB free — a gemma-2-2b q8_0 + q4_k_m export needs ~4.4 GB.
- consequence: flip1 = 0.5 or 1 for items whose only change is llama.cpp tokenizing the prompt differently from transformers; those items are ranked as boundary canaries and the docket's "quant delta" (§13.3) reports runtime differences as quantization effects. s(i) is then not 'sensitivity to representational change' as labelled; exports cannot be written to disk today.
- amendment: §4.2: run every δ1 variant in the reference runtime (transformers with bitsandbytes int8 / nf4, recording the exact quant config), or run the reference itself in llama.cpp at bf16/f16 GGUF so all δ1 variants share one stack; if two runtimes are kept, measure δ2 nuisance under the reference runtime AND under llama.cpp at bf16 so flip1 is read against a same-runtime baseline. Record `runtime` per variant in the battery cert `params`. Add 'free disk ≥ 60 GB' to week 1.
- sources: canary-selection, feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Spec text confirmed: §4.2 δ1 names q8_0/q4_k_m (GGUF quant types), §2.3 recipe runtime is transformers, and §2.3 comparability omits `runtime` while §13.3 diffs bf16 vs q4_k_m. The finding's 'must run under a second runtime' is factually wrong: transformers loads GGUF via `gguf_file=` by dequantizing, which preserves the quantization rounding of the weights inside the reference stack, and `gguf 0.19.0` plus `bitsandbytes 0.49.2` are installed, so a same-stack δ1 is feasible today (disk at 2.7 GB free is an environment fact, not a spec defect). What survives is that the spec is silent on which stack δ1 and the §13.3 quant delta run under, records no per-variant `runtime` in the battery cert, and the lab's own precedent (glimmer under llama-server) makes a two-runtime implementation the likely reading — in which case flip1 and the docket's published quant-delta number would carry tokenizer/kernel differences labelled as quantization effects. That is a reachable false published number, so major, but the amendment should be 'fix the runtime and record it', not the finding's stronger premise.

### S4-09 · major · §4; §4.2; §4.3; §4.6; §5.1; §11
**canary-v1 requires logprobs and re-quantizable weights, so it is impossible for alias subjects, yet §4, §5.1 and the CLI imply otherwise**

- claim: margin(i) needs top-2 log-probs and δ1/δ2 need weights to quantize and batch sizes to set, so alias subjects (the black-box drift case) can never have a canary battery, while the text offers canary-v1 for drift detection on any subject and §5.1 defines alias canary floors.
- evidence: §4: "**canary-v1** — model-specific, selected against the subject to be sensitive by construction ... Used for drift detection on that subject." §4.3: "`margin(i)` = lp(top1) − lp(top2), in nats." §4.2 δ1: "precision: bf16 → q8_0 → q4_k_m (same weights)". §5.1: "for alias subjects, time-of-day and region". §4.6: "the selection cost is one δ-sweep". §11: `styxx battery select --pool <file> --subject <spec>` accepts any `<spec>`.
- consequence: `styxx battery select --subject <alias>` either crashes or silently emits a battery with undefined scores; the subjects most in need of drift detection (silent provider swaps) have no battery except fixed-v1, which §4.1 says is insensitive, while the spec says canaries are for drift detection.
- amendment: §4: "canary-v1 requires `subject.kind = weights`. Alias subjects use fixed-v1 with their own floor; a canary battery selected on the open-weight twin of an alias (when one exists) may be used and is labelled `selected_against_kind: weights`." §11: `battery select` exits 3 on an alias subject.
- sources: canary-selection, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textual check: §4 says 'Two batteries per fingerprint' and §4.1 motivates canaries by 'a silent swap', which is the alias-only scenario (weights subjects are hashed per A.2, so a swap there is `identity`, not drift); §4.2 δ1 needs re-quantizable weights and §4.3 margin needs top-2 logprobs, neither available for an alias, and §11 `battery select` accepts any `<spec>`. One misreading: §5.1's 'for alias subjects, time-of-day and region' is the battery-agnostic noise-floor procedure, not a definition of 'alias canary floors'. Still, no passage says canary-v1 requires `kind = weights`; §2.2's 'the spec does not pretend otherwise' concerns fingerprint strength, not battery selection. The result is a capability claim (canaries for silent-swap detection) that the spec cannot deliver for the subjects where swaps occur — an unbacked claim, hence major; a careful implementer would gate `battery select` on weights subjects, which is why this is not a blocker.

### S4-10 · major · §4.2 δ3; §4.3; App. C
**flip3 (n = 3 samples at temp 0.2) double-counts the margin term, adds Monte-Carlo noise, and is not reproducible by a stranger**

- claim: The probability that a temp-0.2 sample leaves the greedy path is a deterministic function of the logits already in hand; estimating it with three GPU samples, unseeded in the spec, makes s(i) and therefore the selected battery non-reproducible while adding no information beyond margin.
- evidence: §4.2: "δ3 | margin: temp 0.2, n=3 samples | proximity to decision boundary". §4.3: "flip3(i) = fraction of δ3 samples ≠ reference" and "s(i) = 0.6 · mean(flip1(i), flip3(i)) + 0.4 · (1 − σ(margin(i) / τ))" — two terms both labelled boundary proximity. §2.3 seed 7 governs greedy decoding; no δ3 seed is specified anywhere; §0: "every cert is reproducible by a stranger from its own recipe."
- consequence: flip3 ∈ {0, 1/3, 2/3, 1} from three draws whose values depend on the torch CUDA RNG stream, which is not portable across devices or torch versions. A stranger re-running `battery select` gets a different flip3 vector, a different top-N, a different battery cert id, and cannot tell whether the issuer cherry-picked. The 0.6 weight on mean(flip1, flip3) is therefore 0.3 on a two-level quantity (flip1) and 0.3 on noise.
- amendment: Replace flip3 with a deterministic quantity from the reference greedy pass: `stay(i) = Σ_t log softmax(z_t / 0.2)[greedy_t]` (log-probability of the greedy path at T = 0.2), and use `flip3'(i) = 1 − exp(stay(i))`. If sampling is kept, fix `delta3.seed` in params, state the sampler (torch.multinomial on CPU from stored logits), and mark flip3 as `informative, not reproducible` in the cert.
- sources: canary-selection
- verifiers: STANDS: Spec text confirmed: §4.2 δ3 is 'temp 0.2, n=3 samples', §4.3/App. C define flip3 from those samples, and the only seed in the spec is the recipe's decoding seed 7 for greedy runs — nothing binds a δ3 sampler, device, or seed, and CUDA multinomial streams are not portable across devices or torch versions regardless. The finding is right that the quantity is closed-form from the greedy pass (P(leave path at T=0.2) = 1 − Π_t softmax(z_t/0.2)[greedy_t]), so three draws add Monte-Carlo noise with no new information. Material consequence: s(i), the top-N, and therefore the battery cert id are not reproducible by a stranger, violating §0's rule and §1 principle 2 for battery certs and making cherry-picking undetectable; the fingerprint verdict itself is unaffected because verify consumes the issued battery as data. Major, not blocker.

### S4-11 · major · §4.3; App. C; §4.5 params
**The margin term can never exceed 0.2, so the recorded weights 0.6 / 0.4 misdescribe the score (effective 0.75 / 0.25); σ is undefined**

- claim: margin(i) = lp(top1) − lp(top2) ≥ 0 always, so σ(margin/τ) ≥ 0.5 and 1 − σ(margin/τ) ∈ (0, 0.5]; multiplied by 0.4 the term's range is (0, 0.2], not [0, 0.4], and σ is never defined.
- evidence: §4.3: "margin(i) = lp(top1) − lp(top2), in nats." "s(i) = 0.6 · mean(flip1(i), flip3(i)) + 0.4 · (1 − σ(margin(i) / τ)), τ = 1.0 nat". §4.5: "params": { "tau": 1.0, "w_flip": 0.6, "w_margin": 0.4 }. App. C: "Weights 0.6 / 0.4 and τ are parameters of canary-v1 and are recorded in the battery cert."
- consequence: A reader of the battery cert believes margin carries 40% of the score; it carries at most 25%. An item with margin 0 scores 0.2 from this term, the same ceiling as any other item; the term cannot distinguish "on the boundary" from "0.7 nats away" by more than 0.07. Two implementers may define σ differently (logistic vs erf). Changing this later is, by App. C, canary-v2, so it must be fixed before entry #0.
- amendment: Define σ as the logistic function. Use `0.4 · exp(−margin(i)/τ)` (range (0, 0.4]) or `0.4 · 2·(1 − σ(margin(i)/τ))`; update App. C and the params block accordingly.
- sources: canary-selection, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: §4.3 and App. C define margin(i) = lp_top1 − lp_top2, which is ≥ 0 by construction, so for any sigmoid-shaped σ the term 1 − σ(margin/τ) lies in (0, 0.5] and 0.4·(…) tops out at 0.2; the spec's own params block records w_margin: 0.4 as if the term spanned [0, 0.4]. σ is never defined anywhere in the spec (searched §4.3, §4.5, App. C). Material: App. C freezes these parameters as canary-v1 and says changing them makes canary-v2, and two implementers choosing logistic vs erf produce different s(i) rankings and therefore different battery certs from the same pool, contradicting §1.1 (same evidence, same id). Not a verdict-flipping error, so major rather than blocker.

### S4-12 · major · §4.4 step 5; App. C; §4.2; §4.5; §12
**Two incompatible anchor definitions (§4.4 step 5 vs App. C), δ4 dropped from one of them, single-valued `role` with overlapping sets, and K = 64 hard-coded in §12 although App. C can yield fewer**

- claim: §4.4 filters zero-flip items then takes the 64 highest-margin (always 64 if available); App. C requires margin in the pool-wide top-64 AND zero flips, which can return fewer than 64 and ignores δ4, which §4.4's "every δ" includes.
- evidence: §4.4.5: "Add K = 64 anchors: the highest-margin items with zero flips under every δ." App. C: "anchor(i) = flip1 = flip2 = flip3 = 0 and margin(i) in the top-64 of the pool". §4.2 lists δ4 as "(optional) semantically-null template jitter". §12 example sentence: "fingerprint cert X, 256 canaries + 64 anchors". §4.5 `role: "canary | anchor"` is single-valued but App. C `battery = top-N eligible ... ∪ anchors` allows overlap.
- consequence: Two implementers produce different anchor sets from the same data, breaking §1.1 and the `identity` verdict's meaning; one can produce 41 anchors while the compliance view claims 64. An item in both sets has no single `role`, so App. B's "over canaries; anchors reported separately" double-counts or drops it. Whether anchors count toward the 25% family cap is unstated, and since top-margin items concentrate in recall, anchors will be nearly all one family.
- amendment: Single definition in App. C and §4.4.5: `zero(i) = flip1 = flip2 = flip3 = 0 and (δ4 not run or flip4 = 0)`; `anchors = the K items with the largest margin(i) among zero(i)`; `K_actual ≤ K` recorded as `k_anchors_actual`; record `delta4_run: true|false` in `params`; anchors are removed from the pool before canary ranking; anchors are exempt from the family cap and their family mix is recorded. Replace §12's literal "64 anchors" with the cert's `k_anchors_actual`.
- sources: canary-selection, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: §4.4 step 5 reads 'the highest-margin items with zero flips under every δ' (a top-K among the zero-flip set, δ4 included when run); App. C reads 'flip1 = flip2 = flip3 = 0 and margin(i) in the top-64 of the pool' (pool-wide top-64 intersected with zero-flip, δ4 absent). These are different set definitions and only the second can yield fewer than 64. App. C's 'top-N … ∪ anchors' with §4.5's single-valued role is also as stated, and nothing in the spec says whether anchors count toward max_family_share. The one weak sub-point is §12: '256 canaries + 64 anchors' is a quoted example of a neutral sentence, not a hard-coded constant, but that does not rescue the finding. Two conforming implementers produce different anchor sets, and the identity verdict (§5.2, overrides everything) depends on that set, so major stands.

### S4-13 · major · §4.4 step 3; §4.5; §4 fixed-v1; §11 `battery select --pool`
**Stratification by "family" has no source: the pool schema is undefined, styxx-bench carries `category` with different values, and the shortfall rule is missing**

- claim: The five spec families do not exist in any pool the repo has; styxx-bench items carry `category ∈ {factual, reasoning, refusal, creative}`, other batteries use yet another taxonomy, and the spec never says what happens when a stratum cannot fill.
- evidence: §4.4.3: "Stratify by task family (recall / short reasoning / instruction-following / format / refusal boundary): no family > 25% of the battery." §4.5: "pool_size": 12000 and "family": "recall". Repo: bench/suite.yaml lines 13–27 list categories factual/reasoning/refusal/creative/adversarial; bench/tasks/factual.jsonl line 1 fields: id, category, prompt, gold, gate, should_refuse — no `family`. experiments/glimmer_dayzero_2026_08_10/battery.jsonl uses tool_planning, factual_recall, refusal_boundary, calibration_bait, instruction_following, code_reasoning.
- consequence: `styxx battery select --pool <file>` cannot be implemented without inventing a family field and a mapping; two implementers map differently and get different batteries. With N = 256 and a 25% cap, at least four families need ≥ 64 eligible items each; low-margin items concentrate in reasoning/format, so a stratum runs short and the spec is silent on whether N shrinks or lower-s items backfill — the battery cert's `n` becomes unverifiable.
- amendment: Define the pool file schema: `{item_id, prompt, family}` with `family` an enum fixed in this spec, plus the mapping from bench `category` values; cite the pool by its cert id and actual size. Shortfall rule: fill remaining slots from the next-highest s(i) regardless of family and record `stratification_shortfall: {family: deficit}` in the battery cert.
- sources: canary-selection
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed in the repo: bench/suite.yaml categories are factual/reasoning/refusal/creative/adversarial and bench/tasks/factual.jsonl rows carry id/category/prompt/gold/gate/should_refuse with no family; experiments/glimmer_dayzero_2026_08_10/battery.jsonl uses tool_planning/factual_recall/refusal_boundary/calibration_bait/instruction_following/code_reasoning. The spec's five families (§4.4.3) match neither, `styxx battery select --pool <file>` (§11) never defines the pool file, and no passage states what happens when a stratum cannot fill under the 25% cap. Partial mitigation: §4.5 lists `family` per item, so a reasonable implementer would require the pool to carry it — but the mapping and the shortfall rule are still invented per implementer, so the battery cert's selection is not reproducible from its recipe (§1.2). Major, medium confidence because the pool-schema half is easily inferred; the shortfall half is not.

### S4-14 · major · §4; §4.5; App. B exact; §5.2; §13 steps 1 and 4
**fixed-v1 items have no role, but the `exact` distance is defined over canaries only, `identity` needs anchors, and the battery item schema requires selection fields**

- claim: For a fixed-v1 battery there are no items with role `canary` or `anchor`, so App. B's exact distance is over an empty set, the `identity` rule is undefined, and the per-item `score/margin/flip*` fields are model-specific quantities a model-agnostic battery cannot carry.
- evidence: App. B: "exact | 1 − (matching items / items), over canaries; anchors reported separately as identity". §4.5 items: "role": "canary | anchor", "score": 0.71, "margin": 0.42, "flip1": 0.67, "flip2": 0.0, "flip3": 0.33 — one schema for "kind": "canary-v1 | fixed-v1". §4: "fixed-v1 — public, model-agnostic". §13.1: "**fingerprint** on fixed-v1"; §13.4: "predecessor diff — verify --diff against the prior model in the family on fixed-v1 only".
- consequence: The first docket item and every cross-model claim run on a battery for which `verify` has no defined exact distance (0/0) and no identity rule; an implementer who treats all fixed-v1 items as canaries and one who treats them as anchors produce `drift` vs `identity` for the same bytes.
- amendment: §4.5: "For `kind: fixed-v1` every item has `role: item`; the battery has no anchors." Selection fields (`score, margin, flip1, flip2, flip3, selected_against`) are required iff `kind = canary-v1` and forbidden otherwise; schema-validate this. App. B exact: "over items with role ∈ {canary, item}; anchors (role: anchor) reported separately; a battery with no anchors emits no `identity`/`anchor_change` row."
- sources: canary-selection, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: §4.5 gives one body schema for kind canary-v1 | fixed-v1 with role 'canary | anchor' and model-specific score/margin/flip fields; `selected_against` is marked '(canary only)', which implies fixed-v1 items are not canaries, yet App. B's exact distance is 'over canaries; anchors reported separately as identity' and §5.2 makes identity override everything. No passage assigns fixed-v1 items a role. §13 steps 1, 3 and 4 all run on fixed-v1. An implementer who marks fixed-v1 items as anchors turns the §13.3 bf16-vs-q4 diff into `identity` (which §4.4.5 defines as 'tokenizer or weights swap, not drift') while one who marks them canaries reports `drift` — different verdicts from the same bytes. Major; arguably higher since a verdict is at stake, but the canary reading is the likelier default.

### S4-15 · major · §4.1; §4; §4.4; §15.4; §13 step 2
**§4.1's sensitivity claim has no receipt and no planned measurement; §15.4 tunes N on the data that will be claimed**

- claim: The spec asserts canary-v1 is "sensitive by construction" to quantization, fine-tuning and swaps but selects only on quantization sensitivity, never measures detection power against a fine-tune or a swap on a held-out split, and proposes to "adjust once, then freeze" N after looking at the runs — a claim without a receipt and a differential without calibration.
- evidence: §4.1: "A random battery mostly measures items the model answers with a wide margin; those don't move under quantization, fine-tuning, or a silent swap. Items that sit on decision boundaries do." §4: "selected against the subject to be sensitive by construction". §15.4: "the noise-floor runs will tell you whether 256 is enough to separate q4 from bf16 on gemma-2-2b. Adjust once, then freeze." No receipt anywhere for 'wide-margin items don't move under quantization'. Memory `differential-needs-calibration.md`: "an agreement number is worthless without measuring the generator's detection power ... publish the miss list"; SCOUT doctrine: "a claim without a receipt is not a claim." A swap is detected by any battery; quantization is an approximately isotropic perturbation while a fine-tune is a directed step — sensitivity to one does not imply the other.
- consequence: The docket (§13.2) and compliance view will describe a battery as "sensitive" on the strength of a paragraph. If a LoRA moves outputs on a domain whose items are high-margin, canary-v1 misses it while fixed-v1 might not, and the product's central promise is false with no experiment able to say so. If boundary items are also the nuisance-unstable ones (plausible: low margin ⇒ batch-order sensitivity), step 1 of §4.4 empties the pool and the claim inverts. Measuring q4-vs-bf16 separation on the same battery that was selected on q4 flips is circular.
- amendment: Reword §4.1 as "Hypothesis H-canary (unmeasured)". Add a required `prereg` cert (log entry before any canary fingerprint is published) for "canary-v1 sensitivity" with predicted holes committed before the first δ-sweep: pool split into selection half / held-out half; endpoints = detection (distance / floor ratio) on canary-v1 vs fixed-v1 vs a size-matched random battery for (a) q4_k_m, (b) a known fine-tune on this box — Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct vs the voice LoRA in papers/agent-conscience/voice_lora_2026_08_13/TRAIN_RECEIPT.json — and (c) gemma-2-2b vs gemma-2-2b-it; publish the result cert either way, including the miss list and the fraction of low-margin items lost to the flip2 exclusion. N and K may be adjusted only by a canary-v2 params change after that result.
- sources: canary-selection, doctrine-and-commitments
- verifiers: STANDS: §4.1 asserts as fact that wide-margin items 'don't move under quantization, fine-tuning, or a silent swap' and §4 calls canary-v1 'sensitive by construction'; the spec contains no receipt, no cert, and no planned measurement of detection power against a fine-tune or a held-out split, while selection uses only δ1 (quantization) and δ3. §15.4 proposes to pick N from the q4-vs-bf16 separation on the same battery selected on q4 flips, which is circular. The memory file differential-needs-calibration.md says exactly what the finding quotes (detection power must be measured, predicted holes committed first, miss list published), and SCOUT records 'a claim without a receipt is not a claim'. The cited voice LoRA directory (papers/agent-conscience/voice_lora_2026_08_13/TRAIN_RECEIPT.json) exists. Unbacked central claim plus a doctrine violation: major.

### S4-16 · major · §4.1; §4.2; §7.4; §13 step 3
**Glimmer is misstated three ways: it was a single-quant, temp-1.0 day-zero read with unfilled results, never an instrument, a precision sweep, or a quant-delta runbook, and the spec assigns it three different successors**

- claim: The spec retires "glimmer" as an instrument, turns it into δ1, and calls docket step 3 a re-run of the Glimmer runbook, but the only glimmer artifact is a DRAFT cognometric read of Muse-Glimmer-30B at one GGUF quant, temperature 1.0, scored by register instruments with a Gemini comparator, whose glimmer legs never completed and whose report documents its own runbook step as broken.
- evidence: §4.1: "Glimmer stops being a runbook and becomes the selection oracle." §7.4: "| glimmer | retired as instrument | becomes δ1 in canary-v1 (§4.2) |". §13.3: "quant delta — verify --diff bf16 vs q4_k_m on both batteries. This is the Glimmer runbook, now a routine step." Repo: experiments/glimmer_dayzero_2026_08_10/REPORT.md:1-2 "day-zero cognometric read (DRAFT — glimmer legs in flight)", :9-10 "UD-Q4_K_XL (15.9 GB), not the BF16 build", :18 "sampling | temp 1.0, top-p 0.95, top-k 64", :22 "the runbook's `styxx ci-test --window` step CANNOT see these entries", :24 "instruments | styxx 7.35.0 register audit ... sycophancy / deception / overconfidence / refusal", :52 "IN FLIGHT — do not quote until filled", :55-61 results table with placeholders "{GLIM_PROBES_PASS}", :76-81 "the runbook's verdict step ... silently scores NOTHING"; `ls runs/` = baseline-gemini-battery, baseline-gemini-probes only; run_fixtures.py:59 `TEMPERATURE = 1.0`; finalize_glimmer.py:135 "optional next legs: Q3_K_XL dose curve" (never run); grep for bf16-vs-q4 comparison in that REPORT → none.

- amendment: Strike the three sentences. §7.4 row: "glimmer (day-zero read, experiments/glimmer_dayzero_2026_08_10, DRAFT, one quantization, no bf16 arm) | never an instrument; superseded by the docket (§13) |". §13.3: "quant delta ... a new procedure; no prior run exists; the first run is a pilot whose wall-clock and VRAM are recorded in the battery cert `params`." §4.1: delete the Glimmer sentence. One sentence only describes glimmer's successor.
- sources: canary-selection, codebase-reconciliation, doctrine-and-commitments, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed in experiments/glimmer_dayzero_2026_08_10: REPORT.md is 'DRAFT — glimmer legs in flight', subject is Muse-Glimmer-30B UD-Q4_K_XL only ('not the BF16 build'), sampling temp 1.0 (run_fixtures.py:59 TEMPERATURE = 1.0), instruments are the 7.35.0 register audit with a Gemini comparator, runs/ holds only baseline-gemini-battery and baseline-gemini-probes, the results table is placeholders marked 'do not quote until filled', the runbook's verdict step is documented as scoring nothing, and finalize_glimmer.py:135 lists the Q3 dose curve as an unrun 'optional next leg'. No bf16-vs-q4 comparison exists anywhere in the repo. 'Glimmer' names the subject model, not an instrument or a precision sweep, so §4.1, §7.4 and §13.3 each describe prior work that did not happen; logging that as charon entry #0 is the 'we tested it with no recipe' case §8.6 says the log defends against. Major.

### S4-20 · major · §7.4; §14
**Unbacked references: monochord, 'keystone v2' as a document, 'the 90-day plan'**

- claim: The spec schedules log entries #1 and #2 for artifacts that do not exist in the repository or the workspace and maps its timeline onto a plan that is not committed.

'90-day plan': the only plan by that name is clawd/fathom-lab/strategy/master-plan-v1.1.md §5 (dated 2026-05-06, week-1 items are domain registration and emails), not the gemma-2-9b/llama-3.1-8b generalization plan §13/§14 map onto; the plan of record papers/PLAN_the_next_level_2026_09_02.md does not use the term. One correction: 'keystone v2' as a document DOES exist — papers/depth-truth/PREREG_v2.md is titled 'PREREG v2 — the keystone', and autofire_pilot.py references the 'keystone v2 pilot' — so that leg of the finding is refuted; worse for the spec, that prereg was already run and closed CLOSED_NEGATIVE on 2026-07-03, so scheduling it as future log entry #2 is a different defect (S4-15's territory). Two of three references remain unbacked, and entry #1 of the log is unlocatable: major stands.

### S4-21 · major · §4.5; §8.1; §12; §11; §6; §9
**Redacted batteries launder into public change-tracking: `redacted` does not propagate to verify results or the compliance view**

- claim: The spec says a redacted battery 'cannot be used for a public claim' but defines no enforcement: the verify `result` cert has no redaction field, the log stores redacted certs, and §12 shows all `result (verify)` and `fingerprint` certs under public categories, so an unchallengeable `same` history is displayed as change tracking.
- evidence: §4.5: "A redacted battery cannot be used for a public claim — only a black-box \"internal\" fingerprint. The default is clear." §8.1: "Redacted certs store hashes of outputs, never the outputs; the recipe stays complete." §12 table: "documentation of the model as deployed | fingerprint (canonical + noise floor), battery"; "change tracking / version control | result (verify), fingerprint history for the subject"; §12 constraints mention only lab-tier readings as excluded. §6 result body: "per-channel `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}` and the overall verdict" — no redaction marker. §9: a challenge needs recipe match, impossible without prompts, so a redacted cert can never be Disputed.
- consequence: Steps: (1) issue battery with `redacted: true`; (2) fingerprint + noise floor; (3) every month `verify --ref` → result `same`; (4) `attest --view compliance` renders 'change tracking: 12 verify results, all same' with cert ids. No outsider can reproduce or challenge any of it, the certs show 0 challenges (looks clean), and the neutral-description constraint is satisfied. The 'cannot be used for a public claim' sentence is a promise the code does not keep.
- amendment: §2: add `"public": true|false` to the envelope, computed at `log append` as false if the cert or any cert in its transitive `refs` is redacted; `verify` copies it into the result. §12: "Certs with `public: false` appear only under a `non-public evidence` heading carrying the lab banner and never under the four categories." §4.5: replace 'internal fingerprint' with the field name. §11: rename `--redact` on `fingerprint` to `--redact-outputs` and on `battery` to `--redact-prompts`; the two are different and both set `public: false`.
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §4.5 says a redacted battery 'cannot be used for a public claim' but the battery body JSON in §4.5 has no `redacted` field at all (only the fingerprint body §3.1 does), §6's result cert carries only per-channel {distance, floor, ratio, verdict}, §8.1 stores redacted certs, and §12 maps every `result (verify)` and fingerprint into 'change tracking' with constraints that exclude only lab-tier readings. Nothing in the spec propagates redaction through `refs`, so an implementer of `attest --view compliance` has no field to filter on and will render a redacted-battery `same` history as change-tracking evidence — exactly what §12 promises is 'checkable by the reader's own engineers' and what §9 makes unchallengeable (no prompts, no recipe match, no dispute). App. D's acceptance test would fail on such a cert (step 3/4 impossible), which confirms the contradiction rather than resolving it. Real laundering path, no other passage handles it: major.

### S5-01 · major · §5.1; §5.2; §5.3; §9
**Issuer controls its own noise floor: nuisance set, run selection, and challenge threshold are all issuer-chosen**

- claim: The floor is the max of pairwise distances over runs the issuer chooses, under nuisance factors the issuer chooses ('where available'), with no pre-commitment of R or of the nuisance set and no rule that every run performed must be logged; the same issuer-owned floor is the threshold for whether a challenge counts, and the issuer can reply to any challenge with 'a new noise floor'.
- evidence: §5.1.1: "varying only nuisance factors: batch size, item order, physical GPU / driver where available; for alias subjects, time-of-day and region within the shortest window that yields R runs." §5.1.3: "`floor_c` = max(D_c) if |D_c| < 30". §5.1.4: "Every run is logged as its own fingerprint cert. The canonical fingerprint (lowest run seed) carries `noise_floor.runs` referencing the others." §5.3: "A provider that changes the model *during* the R-run window contaminates the alias floor. The cert records the window; readers judge." §9: "≥ 2 unresolved challenges above the target's own floor"; "The original issuer may reply with a `result` cert, `kind = response` (a new noise floor, …)"; "A challenge that lands *below* the target's floor is a reproduction, not a dispute".
- consequence: Inflation: run R=5 with one run on a nondeterministic backend (different attention kernel, fp16 on a different GPU, or for an alias a window spanning a known provider update); max-of-10-pairs makes that single outlier the floor for every channel; every later `verify` reads `same`. Nothing forces the discarded low-variance runs into the log, so a stranger cannot see the selection. Deflation (to accuse a competitor): R=5 batch-1 single-GPU deterministic runs give `exact` floor 0; any provider nondeterminism reads as `drift` after two runs. Challenge capture: once a challenge lands above the floor, the issuer logs a `response` with a wider floor and clients that read the latest floor now display the challenge as "a reproduction". The verdict, the Disputed status, and the reproduction count are all wrong in the direction the issuer wants.
- amendment: §5.1: (a) the nuisance plan (R, the enumerated nuisance factors and their values) must be logged as a cert BEFORE the runs (a `prereg` with `kind = noise-plan`, or a field in the battery cert), and every run under that plan is a logged fingerprint whose `refs` name the plan; a floor whose run count is below the plan's R, or whose runs are not all in the log, is void (exit 2). (b) Add per-channel floor caps for weights subjects at temperature 0 (e.g. `exact` floor > 0.02 marks the fingerprint `low-power`; cap values OPERATOR-GATED). (c) §9: a challenge is evaluated against the floor cert in force at the target's log index; a later `response` floor never reclassifies an earlier challenge; clients also compute and display a challenger-side floor from the challenger's own logged runs (require the challenger's fingerprint to carry R ≥ 2). (d) §5.3: replace "readers judge" with the machine rule: a floor whose runs' `observed_at` span exceeds a window recorded in the plan is void.
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Mostly accurate: §5.1.1 leaves the nuisance set issuer-chosen 'where available', §5.1.3 takes max of pairwise distances at R=5 (one outlier run sets every channel's floor), R and the nuisance plan are never pre-committed, and §9 keys reproduction-vs-dispute to the target's floor and lets the issuer answer with 'a new noise floor' without defining what resolves a challenge. The finding overstates one point: §5.1.4 does require every run to be logged as its own fingerprint cert with hardware in `subject` and the full D_c distribution stored, so an inflated floor is visible to a reader even though `verify` prints `same`; and 'the target's own floor' most naturally means `body.noise_floor` in the content-addressed target cert, which a later `response` cannot rewrite, so the challenge-capture leg is likely resolved correctly by an implementer. §8.6 already scopes a dishonest issuer as mitigated-not-prevented. What remains is not only adversarial: an honest issuer with one nondeterministic backend among five runs inflates the floor unintentionally, and nothing enforces the logging rule without a committed R. That is a genuine hole in the falsifiability §1.3 and §5 promise with a cheap fix (pre-committed noise plan, void floors on missing runs), and no wrong verdict is forced by the text. Major.

### S5-02 · major · §5.2; §5.3; §6; §13; App. B
**`verify --diff` cannot run the mandatory confirmation and has no defined floor, so docket items 3-4 (quant delta, predecessor diff) can never say `drift`**

- claim: `drift` requires a confirmation run, `--diff` executes nothing, which cert's floor `--diff` uses is unspecified, and the two docket diffs cross subjects (precision/runtime differ in S) for which §5.3 says no floor exists — so the day-zero docket's quant delta and predecessor diff always exit 2 or produce a verdict the implementer invented.
- evidence: §5.2: "d(new, ref) > floor_c on first run **and** confirmation run | `drift`". §6: "styxx verify --diff <cert A> <cert B>            # compare two existing certs; no execution" and it "Emits a `result` cert ... with per-channel `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}` and the overall verdict." §13.3: "**quant delta** — `verify --diff` bf16 vs q4_k_m on both batteries." §13.4: "**predecessor diff** — `verify --diff` against the prior model in the family on fixed-v1 only". §5.3: "Floors are per (S, B, recipe). Change any of them and the floor is void — `verify` exits 2". §2.2: `precision` and `runtime` are subject fields. App. B: "Noise floors are computed with the same functions on same-subject runs".
- consequence: Docket ships with two items whose only possible verdicts are `same`, `identity` or `inconclusive`; the spec's own headline product number (quant delta) is unproducible under its own rules. For a same-subject `--diff` where d > floor there is no reachable verdict row (no confirmation possible), so the implementer must invent one. For bf16 vs q4_k_m, S differs in `precision` (and `runtime` if GGUF/llama.cpp), so the only spec-conformant output is exit 2, or a verdict against a floor (bf16's) that is not the null of the q4 pipeline (different kernels, different nondeterminism). Two implementations disagree on which cert's floor to use.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED. Option A: give `--diff` its own verdict vocabulary `{same, exceeds_floor, identity, inconclusive}` with `exceeds_floor` explicitly labelled unconfirmed (`drift` reserved for `--ref` with confirmation); `floor`/`ratio`/`verdict` are null unless A and B share S and A carries a floor. Option B: let `--diff` accept `--confirm <cert C>` (a second existing cert of the same new subject) to satisfy the confirmation rule. Option C for cross-subject diffs: report the distance against BOTH sides' floors (`exceeds_floor_A`, `exceeds_floor_B`) with the fixed sentence 'not a drift claim; subjects differ in <fields>'. In every case add to §5.2: "For a cross-subject diff the floor used is the floor of cert A (the reference); the result cert names it in `floor_source`"; rewrite §13 items 3-4 to say what they output; and add a §6 exit code (or reuse 2) for 'distances printed, no verdict'.
- sources: internal-consistency, noise-floor-statistics
- verifiers: STANDS: Author's side is stronger than the finding allows on the cross-subject half: §5.2 keys every row on d(new, ref) and `--ref` compares against the reference cert's floor, so the intended floor for docket item 3 is cert A's (bf16, R=8 per §13.1); reading §5.3 'change any of them and the floor is void' as forbidding a different S on the compared side proves too much, because it would make `verify --ref` exit 2 on any weights swap and kill the `identity` row, and principle 3 only demands the null of the *same* model being re-run, which bf16's floor supplies. The distance and `ratio` fields (§6) are therefore producible, so the headline number is not unproducible. What survives on the text: §6 says `--diff` executes nothing, so §5.2 rows 3-4 (`same (transient)`, `drift`) are unreachable and there is no defined verdict or exit code when d > floor; which of A/B is the reference is unstated (the 7.x `migrate` the spec says it replaces does not exist as a top-level command in styxx/cli.py, only `log migrate-provenance`, so no direction convention is inherited); and verdict vocabulary is operator-gated by doctrine. That is a definitional hole in a CI contract, fixable by a dated erratum before code — major, not a blocker to committing the spec.

### S5-03 · major · §5.1.3; §5.2; §6; App. D step 5
**Max-of-pairwise floor has an ~10% per-channel false-positive rate at R=5, and the spec states no error rate and applies no correction across five channels**

- claim: The 10 pairwise distances from 5 runs have ~5 effective degrees of freedom; under an exchangeable continuous null a fresh same-model run exceeds their max about 10% of the time per channel, and with 'drift if any channel drifts' and no multiple-comparison rule the family-wise false-drift rate across five channels approaches 1 − (10/11)^5 ≈ 0.38 unconfirmed (~13% after confirmation); nothing in the cert or the result states an α or error rate.
- evidence: §5.1: "R = 5 minimum (10 pairwise distances). R = 8 for dockets (28 pairs)." §5.1 step 3: "`floor_c` = max(D_c)". §5.2: "Overall verdict: `drift` if any channel is `drift`"; "d(new, ref) > floor_c on first run **and** confirmation run | `drift`". §5 purpose: "make 'the model changed' a falsifiable statement" — no α, no error rate anywhere in §5, §6 or the result cert fields `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}`. App. D step 5: "If the result exceeds the cert's noise floor on a confirmation run, log a challenge."
- consequence: Simulation (iid Gaussian per-item noise, L1 mean over 64 or 256 items, 20,000 trials): P(d(new, ref) > max D) = 10.4% at R=5 (ideal exchangeable value 1/11 = 9.1%; the dependence among the 10 pairs raises it), 4.0-4.2% at R=8; with the single confirmation run 2.4-2.8% (R=5) and 0.5% (R=8); under a t(2) heavy-tailed null 14% / 4.2% single and 4.3% / 1.4% confirmed. With 'drift if any channel drifts' and no correction, the confirmed family-wise false-drift rate at R=5 is up to 8.2% for the three black-box channels and 13.2% for all five (less if channels are correlated, but the spec never measures that). A stranger following App. D step 5 logs a challenge on an unchanged model a large fraction of the time; §9's `disputed` (two challenges) is reached by two honest strangers without any drift. A verifier reading exit 1 has no way to know the nominal rate; every day-zero docket (§13, fixed-v1 + canary, 5 channels each, 3 models) is then expected to contain false drift verdicts, and `ratio` is reported against a floor with no stated error.
- amendment: (1) Put the nominal size in the result cert: `null: { runs: R, pairs: m, alpha_single: 1/(m+1), alpha_confirmed: ... }` computed under exchangeability, and print it beside every verdict; alternatively use the empirical CDF with the exceedance probability printed (`p_null = fraction of D_c ≥ d`) and the drift rule `p_null < α` with α fixed in the cert (`noise_floor.alpha`). (2) OPERATOR-GATED on the decision rule: option A — raise R to 8 minimum everywhere (single 4%, confirmed 0.5%); option B — declare overall `drift` only when ≥ 2 channels drift, or apply Bonferroni over channels present, or designate a single primary channel (`exact`) with the rest reported; option C — keep the rule and rename the verdict `exceeds-floor` with the α printed. (3) Use all R reference runs, not one: define d(new, ref) = min over reference runs (or compare to the canonical AND report the min); comparing to a single run discards R−1 samples of the very null that was measured.
- sources: noise-floor-statistics, stranger-reproducibility
- verifiers: STANDS: Spec text is as quoted: `floor_c = max(D_c)` from 10 pairs at R=5, 'drift if any channel is drift', and no α or error rate appears in §5, §6, the result-cert fields `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}`, or App. D. The exchangeability argument (a fresh same-model distance exceeds the max of ~10 dependent pairwise distances roughly 1/11 of the time, ~2.5-3% after one confirmation, compounded over five uncorrected channels) is standard and the spec offers no counter-measurement; 'store the full distribution' in §5.1 step 3 preserves the data but the decision rule never uses it. §5 claims to make drift 'falsifiable' while stating no size, which under the charter ('every positioning number cites a receipt') is an unbacked claim, and App. D step 5 turns each false exceedance into a logged challenge. Not a blocker: the rule is loose, not internally contradictory; major is correct.

### S5-06 · major · §5; §6; §13; §15.4; App. B
**`same` (exit 0) ships with a measured null but no measured detection power — an agreement number without its aperture**

- claim: The spec defines the null (floor) but never a positive control, so 'same' has no measured meaning and CI passes on it; the one sentence that mentions sensitivity assigns the job to noise-floor runs, which cannot measure it — the exact 'agreement number without aperture' the lab's calibration doctrine forbids.
- evidence: §5.2: "| d(new, ref) ≤ floor_c | `same` |"; §6 "| 0 | same |"; §6 result cert fields are `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}`. §15.4: "Defaults above (64 / 256 / 1024, K = 64) are guesses; the noise-floor runs will tell you whether 256 is enough to separate q4 from bf16 on gemma-2-2b. Adjust once, then freeze." (a noise-floor run cannot tell you that; only a known-different pair can). Memory `differential-needs-calibration.md`: "A differential test that reports '150,000 cases, zero disagreements' has two readings ... the implementations agree, or the generator cannot reach where they differ. Always measure the second before publishing the first." ... "Sample size is not power." ... "(4) publish the miss list in full". SCOUT.md:28.
- consequence: A `same` verdict (exit 0) on a battery whose floor is 0.02 will be published as 'no drift' even if a LoRA fine-tune, a q8_0 swap or a swapped model moves the exact distance by 0.01; nobody can tell because no known-change was ever run through the same verify. §12 puts `result (verify)` certs under 'change tracking' / 'documentation of the model as deployed' for compliance readers — exactly the audience for whom an unpowered `same` is misleading: a false negative with no number attached to how likely it is. This is the withdrawn-coverage-estimate mistake in a new object.
- amendment: §5 add a step 5: "Sensitivity receipt. Before any `same` verdict for (S, B, recipe), a `result` cert of kind `sensitivity` (a.k.a. `power`) must be on record, ref'd by every fingerprint that carries a noise floor: for each of a fixed positive-control set on the same (B, recipe) — at minimum q8_0, q4_k_m, the predecessor revision, and where available one small LoRA / fine-tuned sibling (OPERATOR-GATED list) — the per-channel distance, the floor, detected/missed, and the list of channels that did NOT detect it (the miss list). Absent that cert, verify prints `inconclusive (no sensitivity on record)` and exits 2." `verify` prints `power: detects {q4_k_m, predecessor}; misses {q8_0}` beside every `same`, and the compliance view may only describe a `same` verdict together with that miss list. Amend §15.4 accordingly (a noise-floor run does not size the battery; a sensitivity run does). Publish the miss list in full.
- sources: noise-floor-statistics, doctrine-and-commitments
- verifiers: STANDS: Spec text as quoted: `same` exits 0 with a result cert carrying only `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}`, and §15.4 assigns battery sizing to 'the noise-floor runs', which measure the null, not separation. The doctrine file `differential-needs-calibration.md` says exactly what the finding cites ('Sample size is not power', 'publish the miss list in full') and §12 files `result (verify)` under change tracking for compliance readers. Partial mitigation exists that the finding under-weights: canary-v1 is selected against δ1 (quantization) so canary batteries are sensitive by construction, and docket item 3 (quant delta) is a de-facto positive control — but neither is required before a `same`, ref'd from it, or reported as a miss list, and fixed-v1 has no sensitivity measurement at all. An unpowered `same` under change tracking is the withdrawn-coverage-estimate shape the lab's doctrine forbids; major stands.

### S5-07 · major · §5.2; §6; §7.4; §11
**The logprob 'fix' already shipped as `unmeasured`; the spec re-promises it as future work, introduces `inconclusive` as a second word for the same state, and gates none of its verdict-vocabulary changes on the operator**

- claim: Absent logprobs already return `classification "unmeasured", gate "unmeasured", measured: false`, regression-pinned; the spec lists the fix as future, names an instrument ("logprob gate") that is the vitals `gate` field, uses `inconclusive` for the absent-evidence state, introduces same/drift/identity/inconclusive, drops the charon v0.1 status vocabulary, and changes the CI exit contract from 0/1 to 0–3 — none listed as operator decisions although lab doctrine makes every verdict-vocabulary change the operator's call.
- evidence: Spec §7.4: "| logprob gate | lab | fix: absent logprobs ⇒ `inconclusive`, never a flag (stacc commitment) |"; §5.2/§6 use `inconclusive` for "a channel absent on one side". Repo: CHANGELOG.md:959-963 "**The not-stacc fix**: absence of logprobs (every Anthropic response) now returns `classification \"unmeasured\", gate \"unmeasured\", measured: false` with the reason named — it used to classify missing data as `adversarial`/`fail` ... regression-pinned by `tests/test_unmeasured_not_adversarial.py`"; tests/test_unmeasured_not_adversarial.py:2 "Absence of evidence is not an accusation — the not-stacc fixture."; styxx/cognometrics.py:155-157 "classification": "unmeasured"; styxx/schema.py:23 `GATES = ["pass", "warn", "fail", "pending"]`. Spec §6 exit table 0/1/2/3 vs styxx/ci.py:95 "exit_code: int = 0  # 0=pass, 1=fail"; §11 "Removed, with one-minor-version shims". §15 'Open decisions' lists none of these. SCOUT.md:27 "Verdict vocabulary changes are operator-gated." Memory styxx-calibration-leg: "Rename = breaking vocabulary change; blast radius 1 of 39 receipts. Flobi's call."
- consequence: A fix that already shipped is re-promised as future work (a false statement about the codebase); two words for one epistemic state inside one package, so the spec's `inconclusive` exit-2 semantics and the vitals `unmeasured` gate diverge in the compliance view; and a committed spec commits the operator to four vocabulary changes he has not decided — the memory rule that a rename is his call is bypassed by the freeze.
- amendment: §7.4 logprob row → "logprob vitals gate | shipped as `unmeasured` (CHANGELOG 'not-stacc fix', tests/test_unmeasured_not_adversarial.py); v8 either keeps `unmeasured` or renames it — OPERATOR-GATED". OPERATOR-GATED: adopt `unmeasured` in §5.2/§6 for the absent-channel case (keep `inconclusive` only for 'no floor on record'), or amend schema.py GATES and the changelog vocabulary to `inconclusive` in a separate operator-signed commit. Add to §15: "6. Verdict vocabulary — OPERATOR-GATED: (a) `same/drift/identity/inconclusive` for verify; (b) `unmeasured`→`inconclusive`; (c) retirement of ci-test's 0/1 contract in favour of 0–3; (d) whether v0.1 statuses SAME_LINE/SKEW/DRIFT/HEAD_MISMATCH/TAMPER survive anywhere."
- sources: codebase-reconciliation, doctrine-and-commitments
- verifiers: STANDS: Every repo citation is confirmed: CHANGELOG.md:959-963 records the not-stacc fix returning `classification "unmeasured", gate "unmeasured", measured: False`, `styxx/cognometrics.py:155-157` implements it, `tests/test_unmeasured_not_adversarial.py` pins it, `styxx/schema.py:23` GATES lacks both words, `styxx/ci.py:95` is 0/1. Spec §7.4 lists 'fix: absent logprobs ⇒ `inconclusive`' under 'next cert', i.e. as pending work, which contradicts the shipped state and introduces a second word (`inconclusive`, §5.2/§6) for the state the package already calls `unmeasured`. One leg is weaker than claimed: the spec 'becomes binding when committed' (header) and §15 is titled 'Open decisions (flobi)', so committing the spec is itself the operator's signature — the freeze does not bypass him. But §15 still fails to surface the vocabulary changes as decisions, and the false 'future fix' statement plus the two-word state are an unbacked claim and a contradiction with the codebase; major stands.

### S5-08 · major · §5.1.4; §6; §12
**No 'canonical fingerprint' exists in the log, so an issuer moves the baseline after drift and every later verify reads `same`**

- claim: `verify --ref` compares against whichever cert the issuer names, and the compliance view's 'canonical' fingerprint is undefined in the log, so after a detected or undetected change the issuer logs a fresh fingerprint on the changed model and all subsequent verifies use it; no rule forces a `--diff` against the prior baseline.
- evidence: §5.1.4: "The canonical fingerprint (lowest run seed) carries `noise_floor.runs` referencing the others." (canonical within one floor set only). §6: "styxx verify --ref <fingerprint cert>  # re-run the recipe now, compare". §12: "documentation of the model as deployed | fingerprint (canonical + noise floor)" — which of several fingerprints for a subject is canonical is stated nowhere. §8.6: "Defends against: silent rewriting of published numbers".
- consequence: Month 1: fingerprint F1, verify same. Month 2: model swapped; issuer runs `fingerprint` again → F2 (a new cert; nothing wrong with it), and from now on runs `verify --ref F2` → same, same, same. The compliance view lists F1 and F2 under documentation and a clean verify history under change tracking. The log did not rewrite anything, yet the published change-tracking record is silent about the change. Only a reader who thinks to run `verify --diff F1 F2` sees it.
- amendment: §5.1.4 / §6: "A fingerprint for a subject that already has a fingerprint under a comparable recipe in the log must `refs` the most recent such cert and must be accompanied (same submission) by a `verify --diff` result cert against it; `log append` refuses a fingerprint that omits either. The compliance 'change tracking' category renders the chain of `--diff` results between consecutive fingerprints of the subject, in log order, and is empty otherwise."
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §5.1.4 defines 'canonical' only within one noise-floor run set, §6 lets `verify --ref` take any cert, and §12's change-tracking row lists 'result (verify), fingerprint history for the subject' with no rule forcing a `--diff` between consecutive fingerprints; §13's predecessor diff applies only to new-model dockets. Nothing elsewhere closes it. For alias subjects the attack is complete: the subject block is identical before and after a provider swap, so F2 is a fresh baseline and the change-tracking record stays clean without a single rewritten byte, which defeats §5's stated purpose ('make the model changed a falsifiable statement') and the §12 category's meaning. For weights subjects the swap changes weights_sha256 and thus the subject identity, which weakens but does not remove the gap since the spec never defines how 'history for the subject' groups certs. Real governance hole, no wrong verdict per se: major stands.

### S5-09 · major · §5.1; §9; App. D(5)
**Single-GPU noise floor cannot cover hardware variance; every cross-hardware challenge will exceed it and mark the lab's own certs 'disputed'**
