- claim: The null D_c is computed only over batch size and item order on one GPU/driver, while §9 counts challenges from 'distinct hardware' above that floor as disputes — the floor systematically underestimates the reproduction null for exactly the population that is allowed to dispute.
- evidence: §5.1: "varying only nuisance factors: batch size, item order, physical GPU / driver where available"; §4.2 δ2: "second GPU if available"; `nvidia-smi` shows one GPU. §9: "**Disputed** status ... ≥ 2 unresolved challenges above the target's own floor from independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)." App. D: "5. If the result exceeds the cert's noise floor on a confirmation run, log a challenge." Lab charter (`styxx-charter-epistemic-boundaries.md`): "VERIFICATION CORRECTNESS ≠ VERIFICATION COVERAGE".
- consequence: bf16 kernels differ across cuBLAS/driver/architecture; seqlp/topk distances on a 4090 or Linux CI will exceed a laptop-only floor; two honest reproducers make every fathom cert 'disputed' and the first published verdicts are wrong in the direction that harms the lab.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED: (a) add `noise_floor.covers: ["batch","order"]` and `not_covered: ["hardware","driver"]` to the cert; `verify` prints the coverage line and a challenge from hardware outside `covers` is classified `beyond-floor-coverage`, never `dispute`; or (b) require a second machine (Linux CI runner with GPU, or a rented instance) in every R-run set before a floor may be called a floor. Either way, rewrite §9 'Disputed' to condition on the floor's declared coverage.
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: The spec already names physical GPU/driver as a nuisance factor (§5.1 'where available', §4.2 δ2) and §2.2 puts `hardware` inside the subject, so under §5.3 a stranger on different hardware has a different S and the floor is void — `verify` exits 2 (`inconclusive`), not `drift`. The first verifier's 'false drift verdict' consequence therefore does not follow from the text; what follows is the mirror defect: §9's 'distinct hardware' disputes and Appendix D step 5 are unreachable because every cross-hardware run is inconclusive. Either way §9 and §5.3 are inconsistent and the floor's coverage is undeclared (charter memory: 'VERIFICATION CORRECTNESS ≠ VERIFICATION COVERAGE'), and `nvidia-smi -L` confirms one RTX 4070 Laptop GPU on this box. The escalation to blocker rested on an unreceipted empirical prediction (cross-architecture bf16 kernels preferentially flip low-margin canaries) — plausible, but by the lab's own doctrine a prediction without a receipt does not carry blocker weight, and §9's issuer `response` cert ('a new noise floor') is the author's stated remedy. Original major severity holds; amendment (a) coverage declaration plus reconciling §9 with §5.3 is the right fix.

### S6-01 · major · §6 exit codes; §8.6; App. D step 3 and closing sentence
**"Impossible for reasons outside the cert" (provider gone, revision deleted, gated repo) has no verdict, no exit code, and collides with the defect clause**

- claim: App. D says any impossible step means "the cert is defective and the spec has failed", while §8.6 says the spec "does not address providers who make reproduction impossible"; §6's exit codes 0-3 have no value for "subject unobtainable", so `verify --ref` has nothing truthful to return.
- evidence: App. D: "If any step is impossible from the cert alone, the cert is defective and the spec has failed."; §8.6: "Does not address: providers who make reproduction impossible."; §6 table: 0 same / 1 drift / 2 inconclusive "no noise floor, or a channel absent on one side" / 3 recipe mismatch; §2.2 gemma-2-2b and Llama are gated HF repos requiring a license click; `hf_repo`+`revision` is a mutable pointer that HF can delete.
- consequence: A stranger without HF gated access, or facing a deleted revision or a retired API alias, must either report exit 2 (which the CI contract reads as "no noise floor") or declare a sound cert defective; either statement is false.
- amendment: Add exit code 4 `unavailable` to §6 ("subject could not be obtained: gated, deleted, or provider retired; nothing was run") and a matching `result` kind = verify verdict `unavailable` that records what was attempted (repo, revision, HTTP status/provider error). Rewrite App. D's closing sentence: "If any step is impossible because of what the cert contains, the cert is defective and the spec has failed. If step 3 fails because the subject is unobtainable, `verify` exits 4, a `result` cert records it, and the cert's reproducibility status is `unavailable`, not defective." Add to §2.2 a required `"weights_source": { "gated": true|false, "mirror_sha256": ... }` field so the stranger knows in advance whether obtaining the subject requires a license acceptance.
- sources: stranger-reproducibility
- verifiers: STANDS: §6's CI contract enumerates only 0-3 and none names 'subject unobtainable'; §2.2's reference models (google/gemma-2-2b, Llama) are gated HF repos requiring license acceptance, and alias subjects get retired. The Appendix D collision is overstated — 'impossible from the cert alone' most naturally means the cert lacks information, not that the stranger lacks access, and §8.6's 'does not address' is consistent with that reading — but the missing exit code is material on its own: the obvious implementation (uncaught fetch exception) exits 1, which the §6 contract reads as `drift`, and no `result` cert records what was attempted, so reproducibility status is silent. The mutable-pointer point is weaker since weights_sha256 in the subject lets a mirror satisfy step 3. Exit-code gap producing a false drift in CI keeps this at major.

### S6-02 · major · §6; §11; §14
**§11 removes a `migrate` command that does not exist; the only migrate is `log migrate-provenance`, unrelated to comparing certs**

- claim: The CLI has no top-level `migrate`; `styxx log migrate-provenance` labels legacy audit-log entries with source provenance, so `verify --diff` neither replaces it nor "prints the replacement" for anything real.
- evidence: Spec §6: "`styxx verify --diff <cert A> <cert B>` ... (replaces `migrate`)"; §11 table "| `migrate` | `verify --diff` |"; §14 "Shims for `migrate`, `ci-baseline`, `ci-test`." Repo: full subparser enumeration of styxx/cli.py (regex over `add_parser(`) contains no `migrate`; styxx/cli.py:3265-3267 `p_migrate = log_sub.add_parser("migrate-provenance", help="label legacy entries with source provenance (0.7.1)")`.
- consequence: A shim would be written for a command nobody can invoke, while the real `log migrate-provenance` survives under a `log` namespace the spec repurposes (see the collision finding), or is deleted with no replacement.
- amendment: Delete the `migrate` row from §11 and the parenthetical in §6. If the intent is `log migrate-provenance`, write that name and state "removed; no replacement — it labelled entries of the 7.x audit log (chart.jsonl), which v8 does not read".
- sources: codebase-reconciliation
- verifiers: STANDS: Spec §6 line 189 says `--diff` "(replaces `migrate`)", §11 table row `| migrate | verify --diff |`, §14 "Shims for `migrate`, `ci-baseline`, `ci-test`." In C:\Users\heyzo\clawd\wt\v8\styxx\cli.py the only add_parser matching migrate is line 3265 `log_sub.add_parser("migrate-provenance", ...)` under the `log` namespace, handled by cmd_log_migrate_provenance (lines 2178-2234), which labels audit-log entries with provenance — nothing to do with comparing certs. `ci-test`/`ci-baseline` do exist (cli.py 2807-2820), so the other rows stand; only `migrate` is fictitious. No other passage resolves it; the spec becomes charon entry #0 asserting a 7.x surface that does not exist, and the only plausible referent survives under a `log` namespace the spec redefines, so an implementer cannot resolve it correctly from the text alone.

### S6-03 · major · §6; §7.2; §9
**`result` is defined as a prereg-bound statistical report, but verify and response results have no prereg and none of those fields**

- claim: The single `result` definition requires a prereg ref, endpoints, effect sizes, kill-gate status and `deviations`, which `kind = verify` and `kind = response` cannot supply.
- evidence: §7.2: "**result** — refs its prereg. Body: per-endpoint outcome, effect sizes with CIs, kill-gate status, raw-data hashes, every amendment with its own timestamp, and `deviations` (empty or not; never absent). `kind ∈ {confirmatory, pilot, robustness, verify, response}`." §6: "Emits a `result` cert, `body.kind = \"verify\"`, with per-channel `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}`". §9: "reply with a `result` cert, `kind = response`".
- consequence: Schema validation (§14) either rejects every verify result or is written loosely enough to accept a confirmatory result with no prereg — the thing §7.2 exists to prevent.
- amendment: Split by kind in §7.2: `confirmatory | pilot | robustness` require `refs[0]` = prereg and the listed fields; `verify` body = `{ ref, new_run, confirmation_run, per_channel, overall, floor_source }`; `response` body = `{ challenge, disposition ∈ {new_floor, cause_identified, concession}, evidence }`.
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: §7.2 line 213 gives one `result` definition: "refs its prereg. Body: per-endpoint outcome, effect sizes with CIs, kill-gate status, raw-data hashes, every amendment..., and `deviations` (empty or not; never absent). `kind ∈ {confirmatory, pilot, robustness, verify, response}`." §6 line 191 defines the verify result body as per-channel `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}` with no prereg; §9 line 279 has `kind = response` replying to a challenge with no prereg. Nothing else in the spec splits the body by kind. §14 requires "schema validation for all eight types"; a schema faithful to §7.2 rejects every verify/response result, and one loose enough to accept them lets a confirmatory result pass without a prereg. The contradiction is real and forces the implementer to depart from the text.

### S6-04 · major · §6; §5.1
**`ratio` in the verify result is undefined and divides by a floor that is often 0**

- claim: `ratio` appears once, is never defined, and the natural reading distance/floor is undefined when floor_c = 0 (typical for the exact channel on a canary battery and for R runs at temperature 0).
- evidence: §6: "with per-channel `{distance, floor, ratio, verdict}`"; §5.1 step 3: "`floor_c` = max(D_c)" — max of pairwise exact distances between deterministic runs is 0.
- consequence: Implementations emit `inf`, `NaN` (which JCS/RFC 8785 cannot serialize) or crash; the cert cannot be canonicalized.
- amendment: Define `ratio = distance / floor` when floor > 0, else `null`; note in §6 that JCS forbids NaN/Inf.
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: `ratio` appears exactly once (§6 line 191) and is defined nowhere. §5.1 step 3: `floor_c = max(D_c)` when fewer than 30 pairs (R=5 gives 10, R=8 gives 28, so max is the rule in every default). For the `exact` channel on a canary battery the floor is 0 by construction: §4.4 step 1 excludes every item with flip2>0, i.e. items unstable under exactly the nuisance factors §5.1 varies, so same-model runs produce identical outputs and D_exact = {0}. distance/0 is inf or NaN; RFC 8785 uses ES6 number serialization and cannot emit either, and the repo's JCS (styxx/attestation.py line 218 raises on NaN/Inf, line 257 "no NaN/Inf") confirms the cert would fail canonicalization, so it has no id (Appendix D step 1). Not speculative — it is the default path.

### S6-05 · major · §6; §5.2; §3.2
**§6 and §5.2 disagree on what `verify` does when a channel is absent on one side — exit code depends on which paragraph the implementer reads**

- claim: §6 says an absent channel yields exit 2, §5.2 says `drift` on any channel wins and `inconclusive` only if none drifted; a weights cert (5 channels) diffed against an alias cert (3 channels), or a seqlp-less API, is the common case and the two rules give different exits.
- evidence: §6 table: "2 | inconclusive — no noise floor, or a channel absent on one side". §5.2: "Overall verdict: `drift` if any channel is `drift`; `identity` overrides everything; `inconclusive` if any channel lacks a floor and none drifted; else `same`." §3.2: "seqlp ... (where the API returns logprobs; else channel absent, never zero-filled)".
- consequence: CI contract exit codes (the product's promise) differ between implementations; a drift on `exact` with `seqlp` absent is exit 1 under §5.2 and exit 2 under §6.
- amendment: Make §5.2 authoritative and rewrite the §6 row: "2 | inconclusive — no channel drifted AND at least one shared channel lacks a floor; channels present on only one side are listed under `skipped_channels` in the result cert and never affect the exit code." Add: `verify` prints `channels_compared`, `channels_skipped`, and `floor_owner` on every run.
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: §6 exit table line 197: "2 | inconclusive — no noise floor, or a channel absent on one side". §5.2 line 181: "Overall verdict: `drift` if any channel is `drift`; `identity` overrides everything; `inconclusive` if any channel lacks a floor and none drifted; else `same`" — absent channels are not mentioned, and drift on any channel takes precedence. §3.2 line 111 makes seqlp absence normal ("else channel absent, never zero-filled"), and §2.3 comparability ignores subject kind, so a 5-channel weights cert diffed against a 3-channel cert is a legitimate, common `--diff`. For drift on `exact` with `seqlp` absent on one side, §5.2 yields exit 1 and §6 yields exit 2; neither passage is declared authoritative. The exit code is the CI contract, so implementations diverge. Confidence medium only because one could read §6's row as a whole-comparison rule that pre-empts §5.2, but the spec does not say so.

### S7-01 · major · §7.1; §7.2; §1.4; §1.5; §2.1; §8.3; §12
**Certified tier is granted by mere log inclusion: any key can self-promote, `valid` is undefined, and the tier key omits `code_sha256`**

- claim: `tier = "certified" if a valid promotion cert covering that triple is in charon` — but `valid` means only recompute-or-signature, v1 charon is open submission from any key, nothing requires the promotion's evidence refs to resolve or the prereg to precede the result, and the tier key (instrument, model_family, task_family) ignores `code_sha256`, so an arbitrary third party can populate the certified section and the compliance view, and a promoted instrument keeps its tier after its code changes.
- evidence: §7.1: "tier(instrument, model_family, task_family) = \"certified\" if a valid promotion cert covering that triple is in charon else \"lab\"" and "If you want it certified, put a promotion cert in the log." and "never set by hand". §2.1: "Any cert failing recompute-or-signature is rejected at log append" (the only validity rule). §8.3 v1: "open submission. The log stores; it does not endorse. Rate-limited by issuer key. Inclusion means \"this cert existed at this time,\" nothing more." §1.5: "Inclusion ≠ validity." §7.2 promotion body carries `"code_sha256": "…"` but §7.1's tier function does not take it. §12: "Every sentence in the view either quotes a promotion `claim` string verbatim". Nothing requires evidence refs to resolve in the log, the prereg's log index to precede the result's, or `promotion.scope ⊆ prereg scope`.
- consequence: Attack A (v1): a vendor signs a promotion cert `{instrument: "depth", scope: {model_family: "gemma-2", ...}, claim: "<anything>"}` with a fresh key and appends it; every `styxx attest` run anywhere computes tier = certified, writes the readings into the certified section, and the compliance view quotes the attacker's claim string as the only permitted sentence about the instrument. Attack B (any version): fathom promotes depth 1.2.0 at hash H, later ships 1.3.0 with different code; tier lookup on (depth, gemma-2, factual QA) still returns certified. Attack C: a promotion whose `evidence` ids are never logged, or whose prereg is logged after the result, is 'in charon' and counts. Principle 4 ("put a promotion cert in the log") and principle 5 ("Inclusion ≠ validity") contradict each other as written and the acceptance test cannot decide which wins.
- amendment: Rewrite §7.1: "tier(instrument, code_sha256, model_family, task_family) = certified iff a VALID promotion cert for exactly that quadruple is included in an STH the verifier has verified and is signed by a key in the client's trust set (`--trust <keys file>`, default: the instrument's registry key listed in the plugin manifest plus `charon/keys/issuers.json`). A promotion is valid iff: signature verifies; all `evidence` ids are present in the log and resolve to a `prereg` (sealed or clear), a `result` with `body.kind = confirmatory`, and a `result` with `body.kind = robustness`, all issued by the same key as the promotion; the prereg's log index < every result's log index; each result's `refs` includes the prereg; the robustness result meets the prereg-set threshold (v8.1); `promotion.body.code_sha256 == prereg.body.instrument.code_sha256 == the running plugin's code hash`; `promotion.scope` equals the prereg's declared scope. Any code change returns the instrument to `lab` until re-promoted. The log's inclusion is necessary, never sufficient (§1.5). Tier is a function of (log, trust set); `styxx attest` prints the trust root it used."
- sources: crypto-envelope, adversary, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: §8.3 v0 — the only submission phase §14 schedules — is 'fathom's key only' with CI validation, so Attack A and the attacker-writable compliance view (§12) require v1 open submission, which the spec mentions in one line and never schedules; a v1 trust set is a v8.1 design item, not a v8.0 defect. §7.1's first sentence gives each plugin a `code_sha256`, §7.2 freezes `instrument.code_sha256` in the prereg and carries it in the promotion body, so the intent that tier binds to code is present and the omission from the tier function signature is a textual gap with an obvious fix; §11's `instrument promote <prereg> <result> <robustness>` is the only producer of promotions, so the author plausibly intends chain validity to be checked there. What survives: §2.1 is the only written definition of 'valid', Attack B (promote at hash H, ship H', tier persists) holds in v0, evidence-resolution and prereg-before-result ordering are unenforced, and principle 5 vs §7.1 is a definitional gap. Practically no instrument reaches certified inside v8.0 (depth is closed negative per S7-03, monochord awaits a result), so the wrong-tier verdict has no v8.0 instance. Major: 'valid promotion' must be defined and `code_sha256` added to the tier key before code, but the spec is committable with that erratum.

### S7-04 · major · §7.2 (sealed preregistration); §8; §8.6
**Sealed-prereg commitment is not computable by a stranger: salt length/encoding, concatenation, the sealed object and the reveal field are unspecified, and no client is obliged to check the reveal**

- claim: `commitment = sha256(salt || JCS(hypotheses))` leaves the salt's size and byte encoding (raw vs hex vs base64url), the encoding of the concatenation, the identity of `hypotheses` within the prereg body, the reveal cert's field carrying the salt, and any verifier obligation to recompute the commitment all undefined.
- evidence: §7.2: "`body.sealed = true` with `commitment = sha256(salt || JCS(hypotheses))`. The hypotheses are revealed later by a second prereg cert that refs the sealed one and includes the salt." §8: "two additions: challenge certs and sealed preregistration." Nothing in §2.1, §8.3 ("CI validates schema, recomputes id, checks signature") or App. D requires any client to recompute the commitment from the reveal. App. A.1 defines encodings for hashes, keys, signatures and timestamps only — not salts.
- consequence: Two verifiers compute different commitments from the same reveal (raw salt bytes vs hex vs base64url text prepended; `hypotheses` = the list under `body.hypotheses` vs the whole body), so a sealed prereg can neither be confirmed nor refuted. Because no verifier is obliged to check the reveal, a reveal cert whose hypotheses do not match the commitment is accepted, the result cert refs it, and the promotion claim string is quoted in the compliance view — the exact "post-hoc hypothesis changes" §8.6 says the log defends against. A short or reused salt makes the sealed hypotheses recoverable by dictionary attack against the public commitment (~10^4 plausible hypothesis JSONs is minutes), defeating "competitors learn nothing until reveal".
- amendment: Rewrite as: "`salt` = 32 bytes from a CSPRNG, generated per cert, never reused, carried in the reveal as base64url (no padding) text under `body.salt`. `commitment = sha256( "styxx-seal-v8\x00" || raw salt bytes || UTF-8 bytes of JCS(sealed_body) )`, encoded `sha256:<hex>`, where `sealed_body` is the entire prereg body that would otherwise appear in the clear. The sealed cert's body contains exactly `{sealed: true, commitment, instrument: {id, version, code_sha256}}`. The reveal cert has `type: prereg`, `body.reveals = <sealed id>`, `body.salt`, and the full sealed_body; a validator MUST recompute the commitment from the reveal and reject on mismatch at append and at `verify` (client-computed `invalid`, like §9); a result may only ref a reveal whose commitment checks, and a promotion's `evidence` MUST list the sealed cert id, not only the reveal."
- sources: crypto-envelope, transparency-log
- verifiers: STANDS: Checked §7.2 verbatim: `commitment = sha256(salt || JCS(hypotheses))` with the reveal 'includes the salt' — salt length/encoding, the identity of `hypotheses`, the reveal field and any recompute duty are absent, and App. A.1 covers only hashes/keys/signatures/timestamps; the lab's own salt precedent (styxx/redact.py: 256-bit hex via secrets.token_hex, string-tagged with ':' separators) does not even match the spec's byte-concat notation, so an in-lab implementer would diverge from a literal reader. Author's side is real, though: sealing is optional, no §14 week schedules it, entries #0-#2 are clear-text preregs, and §7.2 says 'the log timestamp is the proof of order', so §8.6's post-hoc-change defense rests on clear-text timestamps and does not collapse; no other section refs a sealed cert. Downgraded to major: it is off the 8.0 acceptance path (§14's four verify-* commands, App. D steps 1-5 never touch a sealed cert) and fixable by one paragraph or by deferring the feature to v8.1 — but §8 advertises sealed prereg as one of charon's 'two additions', so a reviewer holding blocker is defensible.

### S7-05 · major · §7.2; §8.6
**Sealing only `hypotheses` leaves endpoints, tests, exclusions and kill gates post-hoc changeable; no reveal deadline or reveal-before-result ordering, so forked preregs go unpunished**

- claim: The commitment covers `hypotheses` alone while the prereg body's evidentiary force comes from endpoints, statistical tests, exclusion rules and kill gates; nothing orders the reveal before the result, states whether a result refs the sealed or the reveal cert, or bounds how many sealed preregs an issuer may leave unrevealed.
- evidence: §7.2: "Body: hypotheses with one-sided directions, primary and secondary endpoints, exact statistical tests, datasets with loader-verified field names, exclusion rules, kill gates, confound gate, conjunction rule, and `instrument.code_sha256` frozen." then "`commitment = sha256(salt || JCS(hypotheses))`" — only `hypotheses` is inside the commitment. §7.2 result: "refs its prereg." — sealed or reveal is unstated. §8.6: "Defends against: ... post-hoc hypothesis changes". No rule orders reveal before result.
- consequence: Post-hoc: seal 'depth predicts truth', see the data, then choose the endpoint (AUROC vs ΔAUC vs partial AUC), the test, the exclusion rule and the kill-gate threshold that make it pass, and issue a reveal fully consistent with the commitment; the log proves order for a sentence, not for the analysis plan, and the promotion is quoted verbatim in the compliance view as "preregistered". Fork: seal H1..H20 on day 0, run everything, reveal only the three that worked; the seventeen unrevealed certs are indistinguishable from abandoned drafts. Ordering: a `result` may ref a still-sealed prereg with the reveal coming later, so 'log timestamp is the proof of order' proves nothing about the tests used.
- amendment: §7.2: "`commitment` is computed over `body_to_reveal` = the COMPLETE prereg body (hypotheses, endpoints, tests, datasets, exclusion rules, kill gates, confound gate, conjunction rule, `instrument.code_sha256`) per the S7-04 construction. The sealed cert carries `reveal_by` (RFC 3339). The reveal cert carries `body_to_reveal` and `salt` and refs the sealed cert; results and promotions ref the reveal cert, which must ref a sealed cert whose STH timestamp precedes the result's raw-data hashes. A `result` may ref a sealed prereg only if a reveal cert for it has a lower log index than the result; otherwise the result is `lab`. Clients list, per issuer key, sealed preregs past `reveal_by` with no reveal as `abandoned`, and the count is shown wherever that issuer's promotions are shown." If a partial seal is wanted for competitive reasons, OPERATOR-GATED: allow `sealed_fields` to be listed in the clear, and require every unsealed analysis field (endpoints, tests, exclusions, kill gates) to appear in the clear in the sealed cert itself so the log timestamps them.
- sources: crypto-envelope, adversary, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: §7.2 lists endpoints, tests, exclusions, kill gates etc. in the prereg body but commits only `JCS(hypotheses)`; the result cert "refs its prereg" without saying sealed or reveal; nothing in §7.2, §8 or §9 orders reveal before result or bounds unrevealed seals. One caveat the finding under-states: the spec does not say the non-hypothesis fields are removed from the sealed cert, so a reading where they stay in the clear (and are therefore timestamped) is possible — but that reading contradicts "competitors learn nothing until reveal," so the ambiguity is real either way. The ordering gap (a result may ref a still-sealed prereg) and the fork attack (seal many, reveal winners) survive both readings and are not mitigated anywhere; the §8.6 defense is unbacked for sealed preregs. Major, not blocker: it is an exploitable hole in an optional feature rather than a wrong verdict in the mandatory path.

### S7-06 · major · §7.1; §7.2; §12; §1.6; §2.2
**`model_family` is undefined and the compliance view shows promotions regardless of subject, so a narrowly scoped claim is displayed under an unrelated model**

- claim: The spec never says how a subject maps to a `model_family`, so a hostile issuer scopes a promotion as `"model_family": "transformer"`, and the compliance view lists `promotion` under 'evaluation and adversarial testing' with no rule that its scope match the viewed subject; the quoted claim is verbatim and the description is neutral, so the view's constraints pass while the reader is misled.
- evidence: §7.1: tier is a function of `model_family`; §2.2 subject has `hf_repo: "google/gemma-2-2b"` and no family field; no mapping is given. §7.2: "Depth certified on gemma-2 / factual QA says nothing about llama or about long-form reasoning, and the code will not let it." §12 table: "evaluation and adversarial testing | result (confirmatory, robustness), promotion". §12 constraints: "Every sentence in the view either quotes a promotion `claim` string verbatim or is a neutral description of a cert".
- consequence: `styxx attest --subject llama-3.1-8b --view compliance` renders, under 'evaluation and adversarial testing', the verbatim Gemma-2-2B claim followed by 'promotion cert sha256:…'; a reader of a llama compliance document sees an adversarial-testing record for an instrument. With family undefined, an issuer writing `scope.model_family: "decoder-only"` makes `tier` return certified for every subject the code cannot refuse, so 'the code will not let it' is unbacked.
- amendment: §2.2: add `"family": "<string>"` to the subject, set by the issuer and REQUIRED to equal the prereg's `scope.model_family` for a promotion to apply; add to §7.2 that `scope.model_family` must be a specific value listed in the prereg's datasets/models section, never a wildcard, and that a promotion's evidence results must have a subject whose `family` equals the scope. §12: "A promotion cert is shown for a subject only if `scope.model_family == subject.family`, and every quoted claim is rendered inside the fixed template `claim (scope: <model_family> / <task_family>; evidence certs: …)`. Promotions for other families are not shown."
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed textually: §7.1 defines tier over (instrument, model_family, task_family), §2.2 subjects carry hf_repo/revision/hashes but no family field, and no passage gives the subject→family mapping; §12 maps `promotion` into the adversarial-testing category with constraints on sentence form only, not on scope matching the attested subject. §7.2's "the code will not let it" is therefore an unbacked claim: the code's key input is undefined, so two implementers derive different tiers from the same log, breaking §1.1's "same evidence, same id, anywhere" in spirit and §1.6 in effect. The hostile wildcard-scope attack only matters after v1 open submission (§8.3), which is why this is major rather than blocker; but even fathom-only, the compliance view's subject filter for promotions is unspecified and `attest --subject` on a sibling model would show or hide the Gemma claim by implementer whim.

### S7-07 · major · §7.3
**The Goodhart adversary's held-out grader is an unevidenced instrument, and the search is a fixed-aperture fuzzer whose miss list is never published**

- claim: The grader that decides 'the property is absent' is not frozen, hashed, or validated in the prereg, and the adversary is a bounded search over a fixed literal list of edit families, so a hostile issuer chooses a grader that co-varies with the instrument and a search that cannot reach the attacks that matter, yielding a robustness cert with a flattering discrimination number and no measured power.
- evidence: §7.3: "a bounded search (prompt edits, few-shot scaffolds, formatting tricks; budget fixed in the prereg) that maximizes the instrument reading while a held-out grader says the property is absent. Outputs a `result` cert, `kind = robustness`, with the best attack found, the reading it achieved, and the instrument's discrimination under attack." Only the budget is prereg-frozen. Repo precedent: `experiments/glimmer_dayzero_2026_08_10/REPORT.md` — "comparator gemini-2.5-flash … single-vendor-calibration caveat applies to all instrument scores". Memory `differential-needs-calibration.md`: "Whenever a fuzzer draws from a fixed literal list, that list is the ceiling on what it can ever detect… publish the miss list in full, never just the rate."
- consequence: Issuer picks as grader an LLM judge prompted with the instrument's own definition (or a classifier trained on the same SAE features); attacks that raise the reading also flip the grader to 'present', so they are not counted; discrimination under attack reads 1.0; v8.1's threshold gate passes. Alternatively the search's three edit families never include the paraphrase/language/length attacks that break depth, and 'best attack found' is weak. The robustness cert is a receipt for the search's aperture, not the instrument's robustness — the exact failure the lab already withdrew a coverage number for.
- amendment: §7.3: "The prereg freezes `grader = { kind: gold-labels | human-panel | model, id, code_sha256 or label-set sha256 }`; a `model` grader may not share weights, features, or prompt definition with the instrument; the robustness result reports the grader's own error rate on a frozen labelled control set (receipt hashed into the prereg) and refuses (`inconclusive`) if that set is absent. The adversary result carries the FULL attack list with per-attack reading and grader outcome (the miss list), the search generator's `code_sha256`, and a positive-control set of attacks committed before the run that the search must recover; a search that recovers none is reported as `search underpowered` and does not satisfy the v8.0 disclosure requirement."
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: §7.3 freezes only the search budget in the prereg; the "held-out grader" has no id, hash, label-set or independence requirement, and the output is "the best attack found" — no full attack list. The repo citation is correct (experiments/glimmer_dayzero_2026_08_10/REPORT.md line 21: gemini-2.5-flash comparator, "single-vendor-calibration caveat applies to all instrument scores"), and the memory citation is correct (differential-needs-calibration.md lines 24-25 and 37: fixed literal list is the ceiling; publish the miss list in full). A competent implementer might put the grader in the prereg since §7.2 freezes instrument.code_sha256 and datasets, but the spec does not require it, and the grader-covaries-with-instrument failure yields a flattering discrimination number that v8.0 places in the compliance view's adversarial-testing record. That is the withdrawn-coverage-number pattern the lab's written doctrine forbids. Major stands.

### S7-08 · major · §7.4; §14
**"monochord (H1–H3 spectral)" and its "prereg v3" do not exist anywhere in the repository; §14 makes it charon entry #1**

- claim: The spec schedules a non-existent instrument's third prereg as the first real log entry; no v1, v2, code, data or document named monochord is in the tree.
- evidence: Spec §7.4: "| monochord (H1–H3 spectral) | lab | prereg v3 goes into charon as entry #1; result either way |"; §14: "#1 monochord prereg v3". Repo: `grep -rIl -i monochord` over *.py/*.md/*.json/*.jsonl/*.txt/*.yaml/*.toml → no files (SCOUT.md concurs: NOT FOUND in repo or clawd workspace .md).
- consequence: Week-1 sequencing depends on an artifact that must be authored from nothing while being labelled 'v3', which a stranger reads as two prior preregs that were never published.
- amendment: Delete monochord from §7.4 and §14 until a prereg document exists in papers/ with its sidecar; if it exists outside this repo, name the path and commit and label the log entry 'prereg v1 in this tree'.
- sources: codebase-reconciliation
- verifiers: STANDS: Spec §7.4 ("monochord (H1–H3 spectral) | lab | prereg v3 goes into charon as entry #1") and §14 ("#1 monochord prereg v3"; Weeks 8–12 "Monochord result cert either way") are quoted accurately. A case-insensitive grep over *.py/*.md/*.json/*.jsonl/*.txt/*.yaml/*.toml in C:\Users\heyzo\clawd\wt\v8 and over *.md in the wider clawd workspace returns nothing; SCOUT.md independently reports NOT FOUND. Nothing else in the spec names a path, commit or prior version. Materially, the first real log entry and the Week-1 sequencing depend on an artifact with no hypotheses, code or data on record, labelled 'v3' as if two prior preregs existed — an unbacked claim under the lab's every-claim-cites-a-receipt doctrine. Major rather than blocker: it produces a mislabelled dependency, not a wrong verdict.

### S7-09 · major · §7.4
**The logprob-gate 'fix' already shipped under the verdict `unmeasured`; renaming it to `inconclusive` is an operator-gated vocabulary change**

- claim: §7.4 lists as pending a fix that exists, and silently changes the shipped verdict word.
- evidence: §7.4: "logprob gate | lab | fix: absent logprobs ⇒ `inconclusive`, never a flag (stacc commitment)". `C:\Users\heyzo\clawd\wt\v8\CHANGELOG.md:959`: "**The not-stacc fix**: absence of logprobs (every Anthropic response) now returns `classification \"unmeasured\", gate \"unmeasured\", measured: false`". Memory `styxx-calibration-leg-2026-09-05.md`: "Rename = breaking vocabulary change ... Flobi's call." SCOUT doctrine: "Verdict vocabulary changes are operator-gated."
- consequence: Two words for the same state in one codebase; downstream consumers keyed on `unmeasured` break.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED: keep `unmeasured` (row reads "shipped; v8 keeps `unmeasured`") or rename to `inconclusive` in its own PR with the rename listed in §11's removed table.
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §7.4 reads "logprob gate | lab | fix: absent logprobs ⇒ `inconclusive`, never a flag" and nothing else in the spec addresses the MCP gate vocabulary. CHANGELOG.md:959-963 and styxx/cognometrics.py:152-158 confirm the fix already shipped as classification/gate `unmeasured`, measured:false, regression-pinned by tests/test_unmeasured_not_adversarial.py. The spec therefore lists a shipped fix as pending and, by using a different word, implies a rename; SCOUT doctrine says verdict-vocabulary changes are operator-gated, and the calibration-leg memory records the same gate for the SWORN-HELD rename. A contradiction with the shipped record plus an ungated vocabulary change is major; it does not itself break Appendix D.

### S8-02 · major · §8.1; §8.4; App. D
**STH signature coverage is unspecified; a stranger cannot verify an STH**

- claim: The spec lists STH fields and says 'ed25519 with the log key' but never defines the signed byte string (RFC 6962 signs a fixed TLS struct; this spec has no equivalent), so STH verification — the root of every inclusion and consistency check — is not implementable from the text.
- evidence: §8.1: "**Signed tree head (STH)** = `{ log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp, sig }`, ed25519 with the log key." No canonicalization, field set, or encoding of the message under the signature is stated anywhere; §2.1 defines signing only for certs ("`sig` = ed25519 signature over the raw 32-byte digest"). The existing `styxx/transparency.py:298-316` builds an UNSIGNED tree head with different fields (`tlog_version`, `size`, `root`) and `json.dumps(sort_keys=True)` canonicalization, not JCS.
- consequence: Two verifiers disagree on whether an STH is valid; §8.4's 'cryptographic proof the log misbehaved' cannot be established because nobody can verify that either STH was signed by the log key over the claimed fields.
- amendment: Add to §8.1: "STH signed message = UTF-8(JCS({log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp})) with `root_hash` as `sha256:<hex>`; `sig` = ed25519 over sha256(that JCS) (same construction as §2.1), encoded `ed25519:<base64url>`. `root_hash` for tree_size 0 = sha256 of the empty string, per RFC 6962 §2.1. Verify = recompute, check against `keys/log.pub`." State that this deliberately differs from RFC 6962 §3.5's TLS `TreeHeadSignature` struct and that styxx STHs are not CT STHs.
- sources: transparency-log, internal-consistency, adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed: §8.1 lists `{ log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp, sig }` and 'ed25519 with the log key' with no signed message; §2.1 defines signing only for certs; styxx/transparency.py:298-316 builds an unsigned head with fields tlog_version/size/root under json.dumps sort_keys and says signing is 'out of scope'. The untracked styxx/v8/keys.py and INTERFACES_foundations.md cite a v0.2 tag `styxx.v8/sth/1` distinct from `styxx.v8/cert/1` — evidence the author already knows the STH preimage must be domain-separated from cert ids (v0.1 mandates neither distinct keys, §15.1 is open, nor a tag), yet no v0.2 text exists and INTERFACES states 'Nothing here decides what is signed', so which fields under which canonicalization remain unfixed even in the next draft. Author's side: §2.1 supplies one natural template (JCS of the four non-sig fields → sha256 → ed25519 over the digest) that most implementers would follow. Major, agreeing with the first verifier: §8.4's 'cryptographic proof the log misbehaved' is unbacked until this is written, but the text is silent rather than contradictory and one obvious resolution exists.

### S8-03 · major · §8.2; §8.4; App. D step 2
**ndjson entry bytes are CRLF-fragile and the leaf-index mapping is unstated; a Windows mirror computes different leaf hashes (charon v0.1 E15 recurrence)**

- claim: The spec hashes raw stored bytes but does not pin line endings or say how a leaf index is derived from (file, line), so a mirror on a CRLF checkout (this lab's own box) fails every inclusion proof — the exact defect charon v0.1 catalogued as E15.
- evidence: §8.2: "entries/000000.ndjson   # 10,000 entries per file, one JCS cert per line" and "a mirror needs only the ndjson files and one STH to verify anything." No `.gitattributes` rule, no 'leaf index = file*10000 + line' sentence. `papers/charon/SPEC_charon_v01_2026_09_02.md` ERRATA E15: "A raw hash of a document or a receipt read from a checkout differs between a Windows reader and a Linux one, so 213 OATH lines would have read as moved on the other platform." SCOUT: "Repo is CRLF on Windows."
- consequence: `styxx log verify-inclusion` on the operator's own Windows machine reports every entry as absent from the tree; the 8.0 acceptance test passes or fails depending on the stranger's OS.
- amendment: Add to §8.2: "`charon/**` is `.gitattributes`-pinned `-text eol=lf`; entry_bytes are the bytes strictly between line terminators (LF only; a CR before LF is a malformed entry → TAMPER). Leaf index i lives in file `entries/{i // 10000:06d}.ndjson` at zero-based line `i % 10000`. A `prove`/`verify-inclusion` call takes the leaf index; the id→index map is derived by scanning, never trusted from a side file."
- sources: transparency-log
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed on this box: core.autocrlf=true, .gitattributes pins many byte-sensitive paths (none would carry to §8.2's new repo), and SPEC_charon_v01 E15 plus the centroids and styxx/sworn.py incidents record the exact CRLF/LF hash-divergence class; §8.2 says nothing about terminators or how `prove <id>` derives a leaf index. Author's strongest defense holds most of the weight: §8.1's entry is the JCS cert, and RFC 8785 output contains no raw CR or LF (control chars are escaped, no inter-token whitespace), so under the spec's own definition the terminator is never part of entry_bytes and a correct reader (text-mode universal newlines, or binary split with CR stripped) is platform-independent; the failure needs both a CRLF checkout and a reader that hashes the raw line including its terminator, which is already wrong under §8.1 on Linux too. Not refuted: INTERFACES itself states the rule ('Nothing hashes working-tree text through CRLF') that the spec omits, the lab has paid for this omission three times, and the id→index derivation for `verify-inclusion <id> <sth>` is unstated (charon v0.1 E13 permits duplicate lines, so id→index is not even unique). Major, matching the first verifier: a defined correct behavior exists, but the spec leaves the documented trap open on the acceptance path.

### S8-04 · major · §8.3; §9; §11; §14; App. D step 5
**App. D step 5 is impossible at 8.0: v0 log accepts fathom's key only, open submission is unscheduled, and no command creates or holds a stranger's key**

- claim: A stranger cannot "log a challenge" because the only log that exists at 8.0 accepts appends signed by fathom's key, §14 never schedules v1 open submission, and the CLI has no key generation or cert-signing command, so a stranger's own fingerprint/result/challenge certs cannot even be signed under §2.1.
- evidence: §8.3: "v0: fathom's key only. Append = PR; CI validates schema, recomputes id, checks signature, appends"; §8.3 "v1: open submission" has no week in §14; §14 Weeks 8-12 lists "Challenge certs, disputed-status computation" but not open submission; App. D step 5: "log a challenge"; §9: "Anyone can dispute a cert by reproducing it."; §1.5: "Challenges are first-class." §11: no `key`/`sign`/`issue` command; `styxx verify --ref` "Emits a `result` cert" which per §2.1 needs a `sig` under `issuer.key`.
- consequence: Every challenge at 8.0 must be re-signed or relayed by fathom, so "independent issuers (distinct keys)" in §9 can never be satisfied and the one remedy §8.6 offers against a dishonest issuer ("challenges (§9) and independent reproduction") is gated by that issuer. A stranger following App. D produces an unsigned or self-signed result cert that v0 refuses at append; under App. D's own sentence every cert is defective on step 5 and the acceptance test cannot be passed by anyone but the operator.
- amendment: Add `styxx key generate|show` (ed25519, stored under `~/.styxx/keys/`) to §11; all cert-emitting commands sign with it (`styxx verify` generates a key on first use). Either move open submission (PR from any key; CI checks schema, id, signature, and that all `refs` resolve) into §14 Weeks 1-4 and delete the v0 key restriction, or rewrite §8.3 v0 as "append = PR from any party; CI validates schema, id and signature under the submitter's own key; the append gate is PR review, not key identity; the only grounds for rejecting a foreign-key PR are schema, id, signature, or unresolved refs; refusal for any other reason is itself logged as a `result`, kind = response. v1 removes review." Rewrite App. D step 5 as "publish a challenge cert; submit it to charon by PR, which the log must accept from any key" and state in §14 that challenges require v0-with-review at minimum.
- sources: stranger-reproducibility, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Citations check: §8.3 'v0: fathom's key only'; §14 never schedules v1 open submission; §9 disputed status needs 'distinct keys'; §11 lists no key command though `verify --ref` emits a signed `result`. Author's side carries more than the first verifier allowed: §8.2 labels v0 as 'weeks 1–4' storage, §14 weeks 8–12 schedules 'Challenge certs, disputed-status computation' which under §9 ('Anyone can dispute') and §1.5 is only meaningful with open submission, so v1-before-tag is the intended sequence merely left unstated; the key-generation sub-point is weak in substance — ed25519 keys are commodity (openssl/node), §2.1 only requires a sig under `issuer.key`, and the untracked styxx/v8/keys.py already has generate()/save_private_pem — so §11's omission is a doc gap, not an impossibility; App. D step 5 is conditional on drift and its failure sentence is about what is impossible 'from the cert alone', which step 5 is not (log policy, not the cert, blocks it); and §14's own tag criterion is the four verify-* commands, which a stranger can run at v0. What survives: §8.3 v0 as written makes §9's 'distinct keys' and §8.6's dishonest-issuer remedy unattainable until an unscheduled v1, an internal inconsistency fixed by one sentence in §14 or §8.3. Major, not blocker: a scheduling omission with the intended resolution legible from the text, not a design hole.

### S8-05 · major · header; §8.1; §14 weeks 1–4; §15.1
**Binding condition depends on undecided log key, unwritten code, and a mutable draft — spec cannot bind before ~week 4**

- claim: The spec says it becomes binding only when its hash is charon entry #0, but entry #0 needs a signed STH (log key = open decision #1, unmade), the Merkle/STH code (weeks 1–4 deliverable), and a frozen byte string — so all weeks 1–4 code is written against a spec that is by its own rule not binding, contradicting the lab law that specs freeze in their own commit before code.
- evidence: Header: "becomes binding when committed and its hash is logged as charon entry #0". §14: "Charon v0: git-backed log, Merkle tree, STH ... First entries, in order: #0 this spec's hash". §8.1: "Signed tree head (STH) ... ed25519 with the log key". §15: "1. Log key custody. Recommend: ..." (undecided). SCOUT.md doctrine: "Specs are frozen in their own commit BEFORE code." memory `sworn-v02-and-the-plan-2026-09-02.md`: "`SPEC_sworn_output_v02_2026_09_02.md` frozen first, then `styxx/sworn.py`".
- consequence: Four weeks of envelope/fingerprint/charon code ship under a document that is amendable at will; any reviewer amendment between now and #0 changes 'this spec's hash', and nothing says which of the intermediate drafts the code was built to. The Appendix D acceptance test has no fixed spec to test against.
- amendment: Replace the header clause with: "becomes binding at the commit that adds this file as a sworn document (`.sworn.json` sidecar + receipt), at git blob <sha>. Charon entry #0 is a `result` cert, kind `document`, whose body carries {path, commit, git_blob_sha256, sworn_receipt_sha256}; it is appended when the log exists and does not retroactively change the binding commit." Add to §14 week 1, item 0: "decide §15.1 and generate the log key before any STH is signed; no STH, no entry."
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Header (line 3) binds on 'committed AND its hash is logged as charon entry #0'; §14 weeks 1-4 builds the Merkle/STH code and lists '#0 this spec's hash' in the same block; §8.1 needs an ed25519 STH under a log key that §15.1 leaves to flobi. Best author defense: the document is titled DRAFT v0.1, §15 openly lists undecided items, and 'committed' can be read as the freeze with #0 as a later public witness — but the conjunction is explicit, no commit or blob is named, and the spec never declares itself a sworn document, so nothing in the text pins which draft the weeks 1-4 code targets. That contradicts the lab law confirmed in sworn-v02 memory ('SPEC frozen first, then styxx/sworn.py'; 'swear path: hashes to the blob') and SCOUT doctrine. Major, not blocker: it is a provenance/sequencing gap fixed by one header sentence plus a week-1 'generate the log key before any STH' item; no verdict, number, or Appendix D step is wrong.

### S8-07 · major · §8.2; §3.1; §5.1(4)
**10,000 certs per ndjson file breaks GitHub's 100 MB file limit and Pages' 1 GB site limit within the first month**

- claim: A fingerprint cert as specified is ~1–2.5 MB (per item: three sha256 strings, output_text, 8 positions × 5 ids + 5 JCS doubles ≈ 1.3 KB, plus a 26-double resid profile), every noise-floor run is its own cert (8 per (S,B)), so one docket ≈ 20–40 MB and `entries/000000.ndjson` at 10,000 entries would be 5–25 GB.
- evidence: §8.2: "entries/000000.ndjson   # 10,000 entries per file, one JCS cert per line" and "A git repository, published on GitHub Pages." §3.1 body: `output_text`, `topk` "positions 0..7" with 5 ids and 5 lps each, `resid.profile [[..L..] per item]`; gemma-2-2b `config.json` `num_hidden_layers` = 26. §5.1: "Every run is logged as its own fingerprint cert." §4.4: docket N = 1024 + K = 64. GitHub hard file limit 100 MB; Pages published site limit 1 GB.
- consequence: The first push of a docket-size file is rejected by GitHub; a mirror clone (`styxx log mirror`) must pull gigabytes; Appendix D step 2 fails for any stranger without the file.
- amendment: Store one file per entry (`entries/<tree_index>/<cert id>.json`), keep `output_text` and `topk` in a content-addressed blob `blobs/<sha256>` referenced by hash from the cert body (the cert stays recipe-complete; A.4 comparisons load the blob), and cap `pool` items in battery certs at the selected N+K. State a size budget: cert ≤ 256 KB, blob ≤ 4 MB.
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: I built a spec-shaped cert (1088 items, 8 positions x 5 ids + 5 doubles, 260-char output_text, 26-layer resid profile) through styxx.attestation.jcs: 2.55 MB with resid, 2.0 MB without, 0.94 MB for a 512-item fixed-v1 cert. With §5.1(4) making each of R=8 runs its own cert, a §13 docket is roughly 8x2.5 + 8x0.94 ≈ 28 MB before the q4 certs, so §8.2's single 'entries/000000.ndjson # 10,000 entries per file' crosses GitHub's 100 MB hard push limit around docket 3 (weeks 4-8), not 'the first month'. Author defense: §8.2 is explicitly 'Storage v0 (weeks 1-4)', resid.profile is optional, and redaction drops output_text — but topk dominates the size, and the 10,000-per-file rule is stated as the layout, with 'a mirror needs only the ndjson files' depending on it. Major: the layout is un-pushable once a few dockets exist and Appendix D step 2 fails operationally for the affected entries, but no verdict or number is wrong and the operator hits it at push time, not a stranger.

### S8-08 · major · §8.1; §8.3; §11 `log sth`; §15.1; §8.4
**STH signing locus is contradictory (CI on every PR vs hardware token on one machine vs 100-entries/hourly cadence), and no maximum merge delay bounds unproven entries**

- claim: 'Every 100 entries or every hour' assumes a running signer with the log key; §8.3 puts STH signing in CI on each PR merge (so the private key lives in a GitHub Actions secret, and fork PRs in v1 have no secrets); §15.1 keeps the private key on a hardware token on one machine — three incompatible signing loci, and nothing bounds how long an appended entry may go without an STH covering it.
- evidence: §8.1: "Published every 100 entries or every hour, whichever first." §8.3 v0: "Append = PR; CI validates schema, recomputes id, checks signature, appends, publishes STH." and "v1: open submission ... Rate-limited by issuer key." §15.1: "generate offline, keep the private key on a hardware token, sign STHs from one machine." §11: "styxx log ... | sth | ..." (a local command). `papers/PLAN_the_next_level_2026_09_02.md` leg 3 item 4: "on pull requests from forks the minting job is the claimant's". Memory `styxx-session-recovery-2026-08-24.md`: "no branch touching `.github/` can be pushed by either token" (the CI workflow itself must be pushed by Flobi).
- consequence: Either the log key ends up in GitHub Actions secrets (contradicting §15.1; a compromised runner or malicious PR step signs a forked tree head — the misbehaviour §8.4 exists to detect, now caused by the operator) or STHs are signed by hand and entries sit unprovable for an unbounded time; RFC 6962's MMD guarantee — the thing that makes 'this cert existed at this time' meaningful — does not exist here. The workflow file is also an unscheduled operator step in weeks 1–4.
- amendment: Rewrite §8.3 v0 as: "CI validates and appends entries only; it does NOT sign. STHs are signed by `styxx log sth` on the custody machine and pushed as a separate commit. MMD = 24h: every appended entry MUST be covered by a signed STH within 24h of its merge commit; `mirror` treats an entry set without a covering STH as pending and reports any entry older than MMD without an STH as `WITHHELD`." Drop 'every 100 entries or every hour' or restate it as a target from v1, not a rule (in v0 an STH per append-batch). Add a delegated-signer field (`sth.signer`, a delegation cert type or `keys/delegations.json` signed by the log key) so §15.1's rotatable key is expressible. Add 'Flobi pushes `.github/workflows/charon.yml`' as a named week-1 gate. If CI must sign, say so in §15.1 and drop the hardware token.
- sources: transparency-log, feasibility-and-sequencing, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: All three loci are quoted correctly: §8.1 'every 100 entries or every hour', §8.3 v0 'CI validates ... appends, publishes STH', §11 a local `styxx log ... sth` command, §15.1 'keep the private key on a hardware token, sign STHs from one machine'. §15 is labeled an open decision, but §8.3's normative text already presupposes a CI-resident signing key, which forecloses the recommended option; the plan of record (leg 3 item 4, fork PRs mint as the claimant) and memory (no token can push .github/) confirm the CI path has its own unscheduled operator gate. The spec claims to 'follow RFC 6962' but states no maximum merge delay, so 'this cert existed at this time' (§8.3) has no bound. A contradiction plus a missing guarantee the spec explicitly invokes: major stands.

### S8-10 · major · §8.1; §14 weeks 1–4
**Existing Merkle implementation (styxx/transparency.py + web/styxx_verify.js) uses string domain tags; spec mandates 0x00/0x01 'exactly as RFC 6962' without acknowledging or retiring it**

- claim: The repo already ships an RFC 6962 Merkle log with a documented deviation (ASCII string tags) and a JS mirror of it; the spec's 'exactly as RFC 6962' creates a third, incompatible tree hashing in the same package and the week-1–4 item 'Merkle tree, STH, inclusion + consistency proofs' reads as new work.
- evidence: §8.1: "Domain separation exactly as RFC 6962." vs `styxx/transparency.py:21-27`: "Deliberate, documented deviation from RFC 6962: domain separation uses ASCII string tags (``styxx-tlog-leaf:`` / ``styxx-tlog-node:``) instead of the 0x00 / 0x01 byte tags"; `styxx/transparency.py:60-61` `LEAF_TAG = "styxx-tlog-leaf:"`; `web/styxx_verify.js:127-128` `TLOG_LEAF_TAG = "styxx-tlog-leaf:"`; `tests/test_transparency.py` (16 tests). §14: "Charon v0: git-backed log, Merkle tree, STH, inclusion + consistency proofs, `mirror`." The spec never mentions `styxx.transparency`.
- consequence: Two Merkle codepaths with different leaf hashes under one package name; a reader using the shipped JS verifier gets a different root from the charon Python code for the same entries and concludes the log misbehaved.
- amendment: Add to §8.1: "Supersedes `styxx.transparency` (TLOG 1.0, string tags); that module and `web/styxx_verify.js` tlog section are removed in 8.0 (or kept under an explicit `tlog_version` guard, never used for charon). Proof generation and verification algorithms are RFC 9162 §2.1.3 / §2.1.4 verbatim; leaf/node tags are the single bytes 0x00/0x01." Add the removal/retag to the §11 removed-commands table.
- sources: transparency-log
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed in the worktree: styxx/transparency.py:21-27 documents string tags `styxx-tlog-leaf:`/`styxx-tlog-node:` (TLOG_VERSION 1.0), web/styxx_verify.js:127-128 mirrors them, tests/test_transparency.py has 16 tests, and styxx/__init__.py:468-469,879 exports `TransparencyLog` as public package API since 7.7.12. The spec says 'Domain separation exactly as RFC 6962' (0x00/0x01), never mentions styxx.transparency, and its §11 removed-commands table (which exists to inventory replacements) omits it while §14 schedules the Merkle tree as new work. Result: two incompatible tree hashings under one package name and a shipped portable JS verifier whose tlog section will disagree with charon roots, so a stranger using the lab's own verifier at Appendix D step 2 sees a false mismatch. Not a wrong verdict, but a real contradiction between spec and shipped code: major.

### S8-11 · major · §8.1; §8.2; §8.4; §15.1
**`log_id` is never defined, and key rotation (§15.1) has no representation in the log**

- claim: `log_id` appears in the STH with no definition (RFC 6962 §3.2 defines it as the hash of the log's public key); nothing ties `log_id` to a key, §8.2's mirror claim omits the log public key from what is needed, and combined with §15.1's rotatable delegated signing key a client cannot tell which key an STH must verify against — no cert type or STH field announces a rotation.
- evidence: §8.1: "`{ log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp, sig }`" with no definition of `log_id` anywhere in the document. §8.2: "a mirror needs only the ndjson files and one STH to verify anything." and a single `keys/log.pub`. §15.1: "The Alienware should hold a delegated signing key that can be rotated." §2 lists eight types; none is a key/rotation record.
- consequence: A mirror that trusts a `keys/log.pub` fetched from the same origin as the STH verifies an attacker's STH; after a rotation, old STHs no longer verify against `keys/log.pub`, so a mirror running §8.4 consistency checks across the rotation boundary either fails or must skip signature checks — silently weakening the only primary tamper-evidence layer.
- amendment: Define: "`log_id` = sha256 of the raw 32-byte ed25519 log public key, encoded `sha256:<hex>`; it identifies the key, so a rotation is a new log_id. A mirror pins the log public key out of band (README + a sworn span) and rejects an STH whose `log_id` does not match." §8.2: "... the ndjson files, one STH, and the pinned log public key." OPERATOR-GATED: (a) forbid rotation in v8.0 (one log key, hardware-held, STH signing is the only offline step) or (b) add a `keys/` history file plus a signed rotation record that the new key countersigns and the old key signs, and require `mirror` to verify STHs against the key valid at that tree_size.
- sources: transparency-log, crypto-envelope, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: `log_id` appears exactly once in the spec (§8.1 STH tuple) with no definition; §8.2 says a mirror 'needs only the ndjson files and one STH' and lists a single `keys/log.pub` served from the same origin as the STHs; §15.1 recommends a rotatable delegated key, and §2's eight types include no key or rotation record. Nothing tells a mirror to pin the log key out of band, so an attacker controlling the Pages origin can serve a matching key+STH pair and pass §8.4's checks, and after any rotation older STHs stop verifying against `keys/log.pub` with no rule for which key applies at which tree_size. This is an exploitable hole in the layer the spec calls primary tamper-evidence, and RFC 6962 (which the spec claims to follow) defines log_id as the key hash; major stands.

### S8-12 · major · §8.2; §8.4; §8.6
**'A mirror needs only the ndjson files and one STH' and 'keys/log.pub in the repo' re-introduce the killed sentence: the trust anchor is served by the party being checked**

- claim: Verifying anything requires the log public key and at least one STH obtained from a channel the operator cannot rewrite; the spec places both inside the operator's repo, so an operator who force-pushes a rebuilt log plus a new key plus fresh STHs produces a log that verifies perfectly — charon v0.1's 'the head must be pinned outside the log' in Merkle clothing.
- evidence: §8.2: "keys/log.pub  # log key" and "a mirror needs only the ndjson files and one STH to verify anything." §8.6 'Mitigates … log-operator misbehavior — consistency proofs, mirrors, anchoring' while §15.5 says "Skip [anchoring] until a mirror exists." `papers/charon/ATTACKS_charon_v01_battery_2026_09_02.md`: "A forger who rebuilds the whole chain produces an honest-looking log. Only a head pinned outside the log catches it." Memory `charon-v01-ferry-log.md`: "do not let any of them come back."
- consequence: With zero mirrors and no anchoring (the v0 state), §8.6's 'defends against silent rewriting of published numbers' is false: the operator can rewrite any number and re-issue everything; consistency proofs only detect rewriting relative to an STH someone else retained.
- amendment: §8.6 must read: "Defends against silent rewriting ONLY relative to an STH and log key a reader obtained and retained through a channel the operator does not control (a mirror's own repo, a sworn span in a third party's document, a chain anchor). Until at least one independent mirror exists, the log proves internal consistency only." Require the log key fingerprint and the first STH to be published in at least one place outside the repo before entry #1; require `mirror` to persist STHs in the mirror's own storage and refuse to overwrite them.
- sources: transparency-log, crypto-envelope
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually exact: §8.2 lists `keys/log.pub` inside the repo and says a mirror needs 'only the ndjson files and one STH to verify anything'; §8.6 claims the log 'defends against silent rewriting of published numbers'; §15.5 defers anchoring 'until a mirror exists'; §14 makes the first mirror clone the 8.0 tag gate, i.e. weeks 1–12 run with zero mirrors. §8.4 does say a mirror 'records the STHs it has seen', but never requires that record to live outside the operator's control, and nothing requires the log key fingerprint or first STH to be published off-repo before entry #0. `papers/charon/ATTACKS_charon_v01_battery_2026_09_02.md:73` says verbatim that a forger who rebuilds the whole chain produces an honest-looking log and only an externally pinned head catches it; memory `charon-v01-ferry-log.md` lists that as a killed sentence not to let back. With the key and every STH served by the checked party, a rebuild-and-resign passes every command in §11 and Appendix D step 2, so §8.6's 'defends against' is an unbacked claim in the v0 state the spec actually schedules.

### S8-13 · major · §8.1; §8.2; §8.4; §8.6; §15.5
**Log operator split-view with unequal tree sizes, rollback without an external pin, and silent withholding are all undetected in v0**

- claim: Gossip is specified only for the same-`tree_size` collision; an operator who serves each mirror a different fork at different sizes is never caught, a fresh clone has no STH pinned outside the operator's own repo so a rollback is undetectable, and there is no inclusion promise so a withheld challenge cert leaves no trace — while the operator and the sole issuer are the same party.
- evidence: §8.4: "A mirror that observes two STHs with the same `tree_size` and different `root_hash` publishes both — that is cryptographic proof the log misbehaved. Gossip in v0 is just mirrors comparing STH files". §8.2: "a mirror needs only the ndjson files and one STH to verify anything." §15.5: "Skip [anchoring] until a mirror exists." §8.3: "v0: fathom's key only." §8.6: "log-operator misbehavior — consistency proofs, mirrors, anchoring." Memory `charon-v01-ferry-log.md`, sentence killed by the red team: "a truncated prefix is a valid log … the head must be pinned outside the log" — the v8 text re-promises the killed sentence in "This is what makes 'append-only' checkable rather than promised."
- consequence: Split view: mirror M1 receives STHs at sizes 100, 200, 300 on fork A; M2 receives 150, 250 on fork B. Each mirror's consecutive consistency proofs pass; no two STHs share a size; §8.4 never fires. Rollback: operator force-pushes the repo to size 200 and appends a different entry #201; a new mirror clones and sees a perfectly consistent log; old mirrors that only 'compare STH files' see a size-300 STH and a size-201 STH — different sizes, no rule says to demand a consistency proof between them. Withholding: in v1 a challenger submits; the operator (who issued the challenged cert) never appends; nothing in the log or the spec records the submission.
- amendment: §8.4: "Mirrors exchange their newest STHs; for ANY two STHs (m ≤ n) a consistency proof m→n must exist and verify; a pair for which the operator cannot produce one is published as misbehaviour. Each mirror pins its first STH in a record it controls (a sworn span or a commit outside the charon repo); `styxx log mirror` refuses to advance to an STH not consistent with the pinned one." §8.6: add "Withholding is not detectable in v0/v1: the log issues no inclusion promise. A submitter keeps the submitted bytes and the PR/commit reference; non-inclusion after N days is a public claim the submitter makes outside the log. In v0 the log operator and the sole issuer are the same party; the operator-misbehaviour mitigations begin only when a mirror run by someone else exists."
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: §8.4 as written defines misbehaviour proof only for 'two STHs with the same `tree_size` and different `root_hash`' and specifies mirror verification only 'between consecutive STHs'; gossip is 'just mirrors comparing STH files'. Two forks fed to two mirrors at disjoint sizes never trigger the stated rule and each mirror's consecutive proofs pass, so the split view is undetected under the text. A fresh clone has no external pin (§8.2 'one STH', §15.5 no anchoring), so rollback-plus-new-entry is a consistent log to the newcomer. No SCT/MMD or inclusion promise exists anywhere in §8, and §8.3 v0 makes the operator the sole submitter and issuer. `styxx/transparency.py:28-33` already states in the repo that the log alone cannot stop equivocation without gossip/witnessing. Overlaps S8-12/S8-14 on the pin point, but the any-pair consistency rule and the withholding gap are distinct, real holes.

### S8-14 · major · §8.6; §7.2; §8.3; §14
**Threat model omits entry withholding, timestamp trust, issuer-key compromise and operator forks; 'the log timestamp is the proof of order' overstates what an index proves**

- claim: The threat model does not name four attacks the design is exposed to: (1) a log that accepts an entry but never issues an STH covering it (no SCT, no MMD), (2) an operator-asserted STH/cert timestamp with no external time source, so 'proof of order' is order of logging and 'existed at this time' is a clock claim, (3) issuer key compromise/loss with no revocation representation, (4) an operator fork (two histories, one per audience) which only gossip between independent mirrors detects and v0 has none.
- evidence: §8.6: "Defends against: silent rewriting …; post-hoc hypothesis changes; unverifiable claims …" — no mention of withholding, clocks, key compromise, or forks. §7.2: "The log timestamp is the proof of order; that is charon's first real use." §8.3: "Inclusion means 'this cert existed at this time,' nothing more." §8.4: "Gossip in v0 is just mirrors comparing STH files; automate later." `styxx/transparency.py:28-33`: "It does NOT by itself stop an operator who never publishes a tree head from equivocating … that needs tree-head gossip / witnessing."
- consequence: A prereg logged at index 1 with a timestamp typed by the operator is presented as 'timestamped preregistration' when a stranger can only establish that it precedes index 2 in the operator's log; a prereg-before-data claim (§7.2) is not established by the log alone, contradicting §1 'reproducible by a stranger'.
- amendment: Add to §8.6 a 'Does not defend against' list: entry withholding before an STH (state MMD from the amended §8.3), clock manipulation (STH/cert timestamps are the signer's assertion until §8.5 anchoring exists), issuer key compromise (no revocation in 8.0; a compromised issuer's certs stay in the log and must be disputed via §9), and operator forks absent ≥2 independent mirrors exchanging STHs. Rewrite §7.2 to: "The log index proves the prereg was logged before the result was logged; it proves nothing about when data existed."
- sources: transparency-log
- verifiers: STANDS: §8.6's 'Defends against' list contains none of withholding, clock trust, issuer-key compromise, or forks (grep of the whole spec: no 'revoc', no MMD/SCT, no withholding; §15.1 mentions rotating a delegated signing key but no revocation representation in the envelope or log). §7.2 says 'The log timestamp is the proof of order' and §14 calls entries #1–#2 'timestamped preregistration', while §8.3 concedes inclusion means only 'existed at this time'; STH `timestamp` is signed by the operator's own key with no external time source. A stranger can establish only index order in the operator's log, not that the prereg preceded the data — and §14 runs keystone v2 on GPU in weeks 1–4 while the log that would hold its prereg is still being built. The 'prereg-before-data' assurance the spec sells is therefore not reproducible by a stranger (§1 rule), an unbacked claim. `transparency.py:28-33` quote confirmed verbatim.

### S8-15 · major · §8.6
**§8.6 threat model overclaims against the lab's own measured limits**

- claim: 'Defends against: silent rewriting of published numbers; post-hoc hypothesis changes; unverifiable claims' is stronger than what a log of certs provides, and contradicts both the OATH contract and the charon v0.1 spec, and the spec's own §14 sequencing.
- evidence: Spec §8.6: "Defends against: silent rewriting of published numbers; post-hoc hypothesis changes; unverifiable claims; "we tested it" with no recipe." OATH_CONTRACT.md:179-183: "**A document can keep this contract perfectly and be completely wrong.** Anyone who tells you otherwise — including us — is overclaiming"; papers/charon/SPEC_charon_v01:19-22 "Charon cannot stop that and does not try ... *A larger set makes HELD strictly easier.*"; ATTACKS_charon_v01:73-76 (log operator rewriting); spec §14 logs 'keystone v2 prereg' after its result, i.e. the log as specified admits the post-hoc case it claims to defend against. PLAN:57 "maximal in scope, minimal in claim".
- consequence: The sentence becomes the security claim reviewers quote; the first demonstration of a re-signed log, a verdict bought with a larger battery, or a prereg logged after its data (which §14 itself schedules) reads as a broken promise rather than a stated limit.
- amendment: §8.6 → "Makes visible (does not prevent): rewriting of published numbers, when a reader holds an earlier STH from outside the log; hypothesis changes after a prereg cert, when the prereg was logged before the data existed (the log cannot tell a late-logged prereg from an early one); claims with no recipe. Does not address: whether any cert is true; verdicts bought by choosing the battery or the receipt set; a log operator who re-signs everything and controls every mirror." Delete "unverifiable claims".
- sources: doctrine-and-commitments
- verifiers: STANDS: §8.6 line 261 reads exactly as quoted. It contradicts the spec's own §1.5 and §8.3 ('Inclusion ≠ validity'; 'existed at this time, nothing more') — a log that does not endorse cannot 'defend against unverifiable claims'. OATH_CONTRACT.md:179-183 ('A document can keep this contract perfectly and be completely wrong... overclaiming') and SPEC_charon_v01:22-27 ('Charon cannot stop that and does not try... A larger set makes HELD strictly easier') are confirmed verbatim, as is PLAN:57 'maximal in scope, minimal in claim'. One overstatement in the finding: §14 does not literally schedule the keystone prereg after its result; it schedules keystone v2 GPU runs in weeks 1–4 concurrently with building the log that holds entry #2, which still means the log cannot distinguish that prereg from a late-logged one. The sentence is the quotable security claim and it is stronger than the mechanism; major as an unbacked claim contradicting written doctrine.

### S8-16 · major · §8.3; §9
**v0→v1 open submission is unspecified and contradicts 'the log stores; it does not endorse' under a PR-merge flow**

- claim: Open submission via PRs to an operator-owned repo makes every inclusion an operator merge decision (censorship by inaction), 'rate-limited by issuer key' is vacuous because keys are free (Sybil), and no record marks where the policy changed, so a reader cannot tell which entries were admitted under which rule.
- evidence: §8.3: "v0: fathom's key only. Append = PR … v1: open submission. The log stores; it does not endorse. Rate-limited by issuer key." §9: "Disputed … ≥ 2 unresolved challenges … from independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)" while `issuer.key` is self-generated and `environment.hardware` is free text in the challenge body.
- consequence: One party with two keypairs marks any cert 'disputed'; conversely the operator can decline to merge a hostile but valid challenge and the log shows nothing — the incident record (§12) is then curated, which is what §1.5 says it is not.
- amendment: Specify v1 as: a policy-change entry (the amended `prereg`/`doc` type) marks the first open-submission index; admission is mechanical (schema+sig+size cap) and any rejection is itself logged with reason; state plainly that in v1 'distinct keys' is NOT independence and drop 'distinct hardware' from the disputed rule or make it advisory. OPERATOR-GATED: whether v1 open submission belongs in 8.0 at all given no hosted service.
- sources: transparency-log
- verifiers: STANDS: §8.3 says exactly 'v1: open submission... Rate-limited by issuer key' and §9 defines disputed as ≥2 challenges 'from independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)' where `issuer.key` is self-generated and `environment.hardware` is free text; nothing elsewhere ties a key to an identity, so one party with two keypairs marks any cert disputed, and the compliance view (§12) surfaces that as the incident record. The 'PR-merge flow' is an inference — §8.3 gives 'Append = PR' only under v0 and leaves the v1 mechanism unspecified — but that unspecification is itself the defect: §9 'Anyone can dispute' and §14's week 8–12 challenge certs sit inside 8.0 while §8.3 v0 admits only fathom's key, and no entry type records the policy boundary. Sybil-forged disputed status is an exploitable hole in a reader-facing status; the operator-gated question of whether v1 belongs in 8.0 is correctly flagged as such.

### S8-17 · major · §8; §5.2; §11; §14
**No reconciliation with charon v0.1: same name, different object, and the v0.1 log/sworn RESULT/vocabulary are left in an undefined state**

- claim: `styxx.charon` already exists as a hash-chained JSONL ferry log with a 243-line committed log, a sworn RESULT pinning its head, a status vocabulary (SAME_LINE/MOVED_VERIFIER/SKEW/DRIFT/UNRESOLVED/HEAD_MISMATCH/TAMPER), and a `papers/charon/` home; the spec reuses 'charon', 'drift', and a new `charon/` directory without saying whether v0.1 is superseded, migrated, or kept, and its 'drift' verdict (§5.2, model behavior) collides with v0.1 DRIFT (bytes moved under the same verifier).
- evidence: §8: "Charon — transparency log. Follows RFC 6962"; §8.2: "charon/ entries/… sth/… keys/…". Repo: `styxx/charon.py:103` `STATUSES = ("SAME_LINE", "MOVED_VERIFIER", "SKEW", "DRIFT", "UNRESOLVED", "HEAD_MISMATCH", "TAMPER")`; `papers/charon/charon.log.jsonl` (244 lines incl. header); `papers/charon/RESULT_charon_v01_ships_2026_09_02.md` sworn: "Its head is `1647a26abadb…`", "It carries 243 lines". §5.2 verdict table: `drift`. Memory `a-receipt-is-history-too.md`: "Never regenerate a committed receipt in place." SCOUT: "Verdict vocabulary changes are operator-gated."
- consequence: A reader of `papers/charon/` and a reader of the v8 spec hold two incompatible definitions of 'charon' and 'DRIFT'; if the v8 log is built into `papers/charon/` or `build_log.py` is re-run, the sworn RESULT's hash/head spans stop re-deriving; the v0.1 ferry-log function (verdicts re-derived from bytes, receipt-set size on every line) silently disappears from the product with no negative result or retirement record.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED: (a) rename the v8 log (e.g. `styxx.tlog`/`ledger`) and keep charon v0.1 as the re-derivation instrument whose lines are later logged as `result` certs, or (b) declare charon v0.1 superseded: freeze `papers/charon/` as history (never rebuilt), add a §8.7 stating the v0.1 log's final head as the first entry-body of the new log, and rename §5.2's `drift` to `behavior-drift` (or rename v0.1's) so one word has one meaning. Either way the SKEW/DRIFT vocabulary decision needs Flobi's signature per doctrine.
- sources: transparency-log
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed in the repo: `styxx/charon.py:103` STATUSES tuple incl. DRIFT; `papers/charon/charon.log.jsonl` is 244 lines; the sworn RESULT pins head `1647a26abadb…` and '243 lines'; memory `a-receipt-is-history-too.md` says never regenerate a committed receipt. The spec (grep) never mentions the ferry log, sworn, v0.1, SAME_LINE, or `styxx/transparency.py` (which already implements an RFC 6962 log under a third name) — it redefines 'charon' as a Merkle/STH log and introduces lowercase `drift` as a §5.2 verdict, so one word now names two objects and two verdicts in the same package. The 'rebuild into papers/charon' consequence is speculative (§8.2 names a root `charon/` dir), but the undefined status of a shipped, sworn-documented instrument and the verdict-vocabulary collision are textual facts, and doctrine (calibration memory: verdict rename is OPERATOR-GATED) makes the rename a Flobi decision, not an implementer's. Major as a contradiction plus a doctrine gate.

### S8-18 · major · §8.2; §11; §14
**'The four verification commands' / 'four `log verify-*` commands' — §11 defines two**
