- claim: The 8.0 release gate is stated in terms of four commands that do not exist as named.
- evidence: §8.2: "README.md               # the four verification commands, nothing else"; §14: "8.0 tagged when the four `log verify-*` commands pass on a fresh mirror clone"; §11: `styxx log          append <cert> | prove <id> | verify-inclusion <id> <sth> | verify-consistency <sth1> <sth2> | sth | mirror | challenge <target> <own>` — only `verify-inclusion` and `verify-consistency` are `verify-*`.
- consequence: The release criterion is uncheckable; a tagger can claim it passed with two commands or block on two that were never specified.
- amendment: §11 `log` → `verify-cert <cert> | verify-inclusion <id> <sth> | verify-consistency <sth1> <sth2> | verify-sth <sth>`; §8.2 and §14 name exactly those four.
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually exact: §8.2 line 250 says "the four verification commands", §14 line 361 says "the four `log verify-*` commands", and §11 line 305 enumerates the `log` subcommands with only `verify-inclusion` and `verify-consistency` matching `verify-*`. No other passage names a third or fourth; Appendix D has five steps and is about certs, not log commands. The release gate therefore references an unenumerated set, which is a contradiction between §11 and §14 in the acceptance criterion itself. Major stands: an implementer cannot know whether the tag condition is satisfied.

### S9-02 · major · §9; §8.3
**Disputed status and 'earned weight' are sybil-free: keys are free and hardware is self-reported**

- claim: 'Independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)' is satisfied by one party generating two ed25519 keys and typing two hardware strings, so any cert can be made Disputed, and any issuer can mint unlimited 'successful reproductions' of its own certs.
- evidence: §9: "**Disputed** status (client-computed): a cert with ≥ 2 unresolved challenges above the target's own floor from independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)." Challenge body: `"environment": { "hardware": "…", "runtime": "…" }` (free text). §9: "Successful reproductions are how a cert earns weight." §8.3 v1: "open submission. The log stores; it does not endorse. Rate-limited by issuer key." §2 issuer: `{ "name": "fathom lab", "key": "ed25519:…" }` — `name` is unverified free text.
- consequence: Attack A (defame): two keys, two fabricated fingerprint certs with distances above floor (no execution needed; the bytes are self-signed), two challenges → Disputed on any competitor's cert. Attack B (inflate): fifty keys, fifty 'reproductions' below floor → the compliance view shows a heavily reproduced cert. Attack C: `issuer.name = "fathom lab"` under a foreign key; any view that prints names displays it as fathom's.
- amendment: §9: delete "distinct hardware"; state: "The log does not verify key independence; Disputed and reproduction counts are computed only over issuer keys in the client's trust file (`trust.json`, outside the log, default = the fathom key plus keys the client operator adds). Counts over untrusted keys are displayed separately and labelled `unvetted`." §2: "Clients display the key fingerprint; `issuer.name` is a label and is never used for identity or display without the key." OPERATOR-GATED: whether v1 open submission needs a per-key cost (proof of a prior logged fingerprint from the same key, or a fee) — trade-off: raises the bar for honest outside challengers.
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually exact: §9 line 280 counts "independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)", §9 line 273 has `environment.hardware` as free text, §8.3 line 255 says v1 open submission is rate-limited only by issuer key, and §2/§2.1 bind signatures to `issuer.key` while `issuer.name` is unbound free text. §8.6 acknowledges fabricated results as mitigated-not-prevented, but does not address the specific defect: "distinct hardware" implies an independence check the log cannot perform, so the Disputed verdict and reproduction weight are minted for free once v1 opens, and name spoofing is unaddressed anywhere. In v0 (fathom key only) the attack is dormant, but §14 schedules challenge certs and disputed-status computation in weeks 8-12 without gating them on a trust model. Real attack against a displayed status that feeds the §12 incident record; major stands.

### S9-03 · major · §9
**Disputed status depends on 'unresolved', which is never defined; `recipe_match` is self-asserted in the body while also 'computed by clients'**

- claim: Nothing in §9 says what resolves a challenge, and the challenger's own `recipe_match: true` is stored beside a rule that says validity is client-computed.
- evidence: §9 body: `"recipe_match": true`; rules: "A challenge whose fingerprint cert fails the recipe-match check is `invalid` (computed by clients, not stored)." "**Disputed** status (client-computed): a cert with ≥ 2 unresolved challenges above the target's own floor from independent issuers". "The original issuer may reply with a `result` cert, `kind = response`".
- consequence: One client treats any response as resolution (a one-word denial clears a dispute), another treats only concessions as resolution; 'disputed' is not a computable status.
- amendment: Delete `recipe_match` from the body (clients compute it from the two recipes). Define: "A challenge is resolved when a `response` result from the target's issuer refs it with `disposition ∈ {new_floor, cause_identified, concession}` AND, for `new_floor`, the challenge distance is ≤ the new floor. Otherwise it remains unresolved; the response is displayed beside it."
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §9 line 280 is the only occurrence of 'unresolved' in the spec (grep for resolved/resolution finds nothing else), and §9 lists three response contents (new floor, identified cause, concession) without saying which, if any, clears a challenge. The body example does carry `recipe_match: true` while the rule beneath says validity is client-computed, so the stored field is a self-assertion that can disagree with the computed one. Material: 'Disputed' is a client-computed status that §12 surfaces in the 'incident and dispute record' category, and §1 principle 5 makes challenges first-class; with resolution undefined, two conforming clients will disagree on whether a one-line 'response' clears a dispute, so the status is not computable from the log. This is not an ambiguity a reasonable implementer resolves the same way — the three listed response kinds have genuinely different force. Major stands.

### S0-05 · minor · title; §0; §3.2; §5; §8.1; §12; §13; A.3
**Forbidden and marketing vocabulary: checksum-as-guarantee, 'pitch', 'science and marketing', 'protocol', 'checkable rather than promised'**

- claim: The spec uses vocabulary the claim ledger forbids or that overclaims relative to what the mechanism does: 'checksum' for a hash of greedy outputs on a battery the spec itself says can fail to move under quantization, two marketing sentences, 'protocol', and the phrase 'checkable rather than promised' for append-only.
- evidence: Spec line 2 "### checksums for model behavior"; §0 "a reproducible checksum for a model's behavior"; §3.2 "The battery-level hash is *the checksum*."; A.3 "so the checksum is order-independent"; §4.1 "A random battery mostly measures items the model answers with a wide margin; those don't move under quantization, fine-tuning, or a silent swap." (so equal hash does not imply same model); §12 "That is the whole pitch"; §13 "Science and marketing on the same GPU hours."; §5 "## 5. Noise-floor protocol"; §14 "Noise-floor protocol end to end"; §8.1 "This is what makes "append-only" checkable rather than promised." PLAN:127-129 "**Must not be said.** "First." "Nobody." "Wire", "protocol" ..."; SCOUT.md:27 "Never say ... 'wire', 'protocol' ... checksum-as-guarantee"; styxx-charter: "repo positioning docs must cite receipts for every number" and no marketing noise.
- consequence: The committed spec becomes the lab's positioning copy: 'checksum for model behavior' will be read as 'same hash ⇒ same model' (false by §4.1's own admission), and 'marketing'/'pitch' sentences in a frozen engineering spec are quoted back at the lab by the next reviewer.
- amendment: Title/§0/§3.2/A.3: 'checksum' → 'battery hash' (and add: 'equal hashes mean equal greedy outputs on this battery, nothing about the weights'). §12: delete "That is the whole pitch;" keep "anything stronger is counsel's sentence to write". §13: delete "Science and marketing on the same GPU hours." §5/§14: 'Noise-floor protocol' → 'Noise-floor procedure'. §8.1: → "This is what lets a reader holding an earlier STH from outside the log check that the log extended it."
- sources: doctrine-and-commitments
- verifiers: STANDS: All quoted phrases are present verbatim (title line 2, §0, §3.2, A.3, §5, §8.1, §12 'That is the whole pitch', §13 'Science and marketing on the same GPU hours', §14), and PLAN_the_next_level lines 127-129 do list 'Wire', 'protocol' under 'Must not be said'. But the spec already disclaims what 'checksum' would overclaim (§2.2 alias weakness, §4.1 wide-margin items, §5.2 floor-relative verdicts, §8.6), and 'noise-floor protocol' names a procedure, not the forbidden 'the protocol every agent is checked against' sense; 'checkable rather than promised' is an accurate description of consistency proofs. No verdict, number or Appendix D step is affected. What remains is two positioning sentences in an engineering spec and a doctrine word — a stylistic/doctrine fix, so minor.

### S11-03 · minor · §11 removed table; §6; §14 weeks 1–4
**Shim table and §6 name a top-level `migrate` command that does not exist in 7.47.0**

- claim: There is no top-level `styxx migrate`; the only similarly named command is `log migrate-provenance`, which relabels audit-log entries and is not replaced by `verify --diff`.
- evidence: §11: "| `migrate` | `verify --diff` |"; §6: "(replaces `migrate`)". `styxx/cli.py:3265`: `p_migrate = log_sub.add_parser("migrate-provenance", ...)`; `styxx/cli.py:2178-2234` `cmd_log_migrate_provenance` "(audit log empty — nothing to migrate)"; `grep -n '"migrate"' styxx/cli.py` → no match. Tests referencing migrate: 0 files in `tests/`.
- consequence: Week-1–4 task 'Shims for `migrate`' is a no-op on a nonexistent command; the promised shim prints a replacement for a command nobody had.
- amendment: Delete the `migrate` row and the '(replaces `migrate`)' clause in §6. Add a row for `log migrate-provenance` only if `styxx log` is being repurposed (see S11-01).
- sources: internal-consistency, feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed: grep for a top-level "migrate" parser in styxx/cli.py returns nothing; pyproject.toml declares only the styxx console script; the sole similar name is log migrate-provenance (cli.py:3265, cmd_log_migrate_provenance at 2178), which relabels audit-log entry provenance and has nothing to do with verify --diff. The scout note asserting migrate exists is wrong. §6 '(replaces migrate)', the §11 row, and the §14 week 1-4 task all repeat the false statement. Severity lowered: no verdict, cert or number is affected and an implementer would discover the absence immediately and drop the shim, but a spec that becomes binding as charon entry #0 should not carry a factual claim about a command that does not exist.

### S11-04 · minor · §11
**`cogn_audit_on_send` is a Python function in `styxx.middleware`, not a CLI command, so a CLI shim cannot exist for it**

- claim: The removal table lists `cogn_audit_on_send` → `agent emit` among CLI commands with printed-replacement shims, but it is a middleware Python API and the shim mechanism does not apply.
- evidence: §11 table: "| `cogn_audit_on_send` | `agent emit` |"; `cogn_audit_on_send` is defined in `styxx/middleware.py`; no `add_parser("cogn_audit_on_send"` in `styxx/cli.py`.
- consequence: The `cogn_audit_on_send` shim cannot be a CLI shim; Python callers get no deprecation path.
- amendment: Rewrite the row: "Python API: `styxx.middleware.cogn_audit_on_send` → `styxx.agent.emit()`; deprecation warning for one minor".
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: cogn_audit_on_send is defined only at styxx/middleware.py:119 as a Python function; there is no add_parser for it in styxx/cli.py. §11 lists it under 'CLI surface' with 'one-minor-version shims that print the replacement', and §10 calls action certs its successor without specifying a Python deprecation path. The textual point is accurate and the spec resolves it nowhere. Materiality is limited: any implementer would emit a DeprecationWarning naming styxx.agent.emit rather than attempt a CLI shim, so the ambiguity is likely resolved correctly; the row is mislabeled rather than harmful.

### S11-05 · minor · §11
**"Ten candidate instruments in one namespace" has no referent — the registry has six plus one fingerprint-only, the cognometric set four**

- claim: No enumeration in the repo yields ten instruments; the number is unbacked.
- evidence: Spec §11: "| ten candidate instruments in one namespace | `instrument` registry with computed tiers |". Repo: `python -c "from styxx.attack.registry import list_instruments; print(list_instruments())"` → ['deception', 'goal_drift', 'loop', 'overconfidence', 'plan_action', 'sycophancy']; styxx/attack/fingerprint.py:58 `_FINGERPRINT_ONLY_INSTRUMENTS = {"refusal"}`; styxx/cognometrics.py:59 `COGN_INSTRUMENTS = ["sycophancy", "deception", "overconfidence", "refusal"]`.

The spec never states what the ten are, so the number is unbacked as written. Real gap underneath: the spec gives no list of which instruments enter the registry at 8.0. Minor as stated by the reviewer; the amendment should enumerate rather than merely delete the number.

### S14-05 · minor · §14 weeks 8–12; §8.4
**'8.0 tagged when ... someone who isn't flobi' is an external dependency with no owner and a zero-replication history**

- claim: The release gate depends on an unnamed third party performing a mirror verification; the lab's own record says no external replication has ever occurred, and tagging also triggers a PyPI publish that memory says is operator-only.
- evidence: §14: "8.0 tagged when the four `log verify-*` commands pass on a fresh mirror clone by someone who isn't flobi." `REPLICATIONS.md:6`: "Zero replications after six months is also an answer". `papers/PLAN_the_next_level_2026_09_02.md`: "external replications remain zero". `.github/workflows/publish.yml:3`: "Tag-driven release pipeline. Pushing a tag of the form `vX.Y.Z`"; memory `charon-v01-ferry-log.md`: "`release/SHIP_v710_CHECKLIST.md` says publish steps are Flobi's".
- consequence: 8.0 has no reachable definition of done inside the 12 weeks; if the gate is satisfied by a lab-run CI job, the sentence overstates it in exactly the way the lab's claim ledger forbids ('first', 'nobody', 'replicated').
- amendment: Rewrite: "8.0 is tagged when (a) the four `log verify-*` commands pass in a fresh clone on Linux CI (not this box), and (b) a named outside reviewer has been asked in writing (link the request) — their result, or its absence after 14 days, is recorded in REPLICATIONS.md. (b) does not block the tag; it labels it."
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Citations check out: REPLICATIONS.md:6 'Zero replications after six months is also an answer', PLAN_the_next_level:147 'external replications remain zero', publish.yml is tag-driven, and charon memory says publish steps are Flobi's. But the spec's phrase 'someone who isn't flobi' does not itself claim external replication, and a lab-run Linux CI job or a named reviewer both satisfy it as written; the overstatement only arises if the lab later describes the tag as stranger-verified, which is speculative. The gate having no owner is a real planning gap but the operator will resolve it when tagging, so minor.

### S2-06 · minor · §2; §14
**`eval` is one of the eight types and is defined nowhere**

- claim: The type enum lists `eval`; §3–§10 define fingerprint, battery, prereg, result, promotion, action, challenge only; §14 requires schema validation for all eight.
- evidence: §2: `"type": "fingerprint | battery | eval | prereg | result | promotion | action | challenge"`; §2 header: "One schema, eight types."; §14 weeks 1–4: "schema validation for all eight types." No section titled or describing an `eval` body exists.
- consequence: The week-1 deliverable is unimplementable as written; any implementer invents an `eval` schema and two implementations disagree on what validates.
- amendment: Delete `eval` from the enum and change "eight" to "seven" in §2 and §14; or add a §-numbered definition of the `eval` body before commit.
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Verified by grep over the spec: `eval` occurs once, in the §2 enum at line 32; every other type has a body definition (§3, §4, §7.2, §9, §10) and §12's 'evaluation and adversarial testing' row maps to result/promotion, so there is no hidden definition and 'eight' in §2 and §14 is a stale count. Author's side: nothing else in the spec depends on an eval cert, no verdict, floor, or number is affected, and a schema validator that rejects unknown types is the only sane implementation, so two implementers converge rather than diverge. It is a real self-contradiction in the binding envelope that must be fixed before the spec is hashed as entry #0, but it is a one-word fix with no runtime consequence unless an eval cert is ever issued; minor rather than the first verifier's major.

### S2-11 · minor · §2.2; §5.1.1; §5.3; §6; App. D steps 3-4
**Alias floors: `observed_at`/`observed_model_id` make each run its own subject, the window is non-stationary and never consulted by `verify`, and there is no expiry**

- claim: `observed_at` and `observed_model_id` live in the alias subject, so by §5.3's literal rule every alias run is its own S and no floor is ever on record; a floor from 'the shortest window that yields R runs' is a lower bound on the null over the weeks across which strangers in other regions will run `verify --ref`, and `verify` has no rule about elapsed time, region mismatch or `observed_model_id` change.
- evidence: §2.2 alias subject: `"observed_model_id": "<if returned in responses>", "observed_at": "2026-09-07T18:00:00Z", "region": "..."`. §5.1.1: "for alias subjects, time-of-day and region within the shortest window that yields R runs". §5.3: "Floors are per (S, B, recipe)." and "A provider that changes the model *during* the R-run window contaminates the alias floor. The cert records the window; readers judge." §6 exit codes have no alias-specific outcome.
- consequence: Literal reading: alias verify always exits 2. Intended reading: five calls in one hour measure batching/routing jitter of one deployment; a stranger in another region three weeks later sees infrastructure changes (kernel, batching, region migration) that exceed a one-hour floor, the confirmation run reproduces it → `drift` (exit 1) with no way to separate provider infra from provider model; or `same` while `observed_model_id` in responses differs from the cert's. Conversely a floor contaminated by an in-window model change is inflated and every later verify is `same` — the spec offers 'readers judge' where §1 principle 3 promised a measured null.
- amendment: (1) Define the S used for floor lookup as (provider, alias, region) — exclude `observed_at` and `observed_model_id` from the floor key, keep them in the cert. (2) `noise_floor.window: { start, end, runs }` is mandatory for aliases. (3) `verify --ref` on an alias subject re-measures R runs NOW (cheap API calls), computes floor_now, and decides against max(floor_then, floor_now); it must compare `observed_model_id` and `region` and print them beside the verdict; a differing `observed_model_id` is reported as `identity` (exit 1) regardless of distances (the one case where identity has evidence); a differing region or a run outside the cert's window is labelled `floor scope: <window>, <region>` and the verdict is capped at `same (out-of-window)` / `drift (out-of-window)` so the CI contract carries the caveat. (4) Alias floors expire: after `window.end + 30 d` (OPERATOR-GATED) a `--ref` without re-measurement exits 2.
- sources: noise-floor-statistics, stranger-reproducibility
- verifiers: STANDS: The quotes are accurate: the alias subject carries `observed_at`/`observed_model_id`/`region`, §5.3 keys floors on (S, B, recipe) and voids the floor if any changes, and §6's exit table has no alias-specific outcome or window/expiry rule. But most of the consequence is either resolved by the spec's own framing or is a design choice: §2.2 and §8.6 explicitly say alias fingerprints are black-box, cannot separate provider infra from model, and "stay that way"; a one-hour floor is still a measured null of the stated nuisance; expiry and re-measure-now are OPERATOR-GATED policy, and the proposed key (provider, alias, region) contradicts §5.1 which varies region as a nuisance. What survives is an ambiguity (which alias-subject fields form S for floor lookup) that a reasonable implementer resolves by keying on provider+alias, and the small gap that a changed `observed_model_id` on re-run is not surfaced beside the verdict. Minor, not major.

### S2-12 · minor · §2; §2.1; App. A.1; App. D step 1
**Envelope says the signature is over 'id bytes'; §2.1 says over the raw 32-byte digest — two verifiers will disagree**

- claim: `id` is the ASCII string `sha256:<64 hex>` while §2.1 signs "the raw 32-byte digest"; a second implementation (the lab's own browser-verifier practice) that signs the UTF-8 of the id string will fail step 1 against the Python one.
- evidence: §2: `"sig": "ed25519:<base64url signature over id bytes>"`; §2.1: "`sig` = ed25519 signature over the raw 32-byte digest."; memory `styxx-leg1-receipt-binding-2026-09-05.md`: "the browser verifier, a 2nd implementation agreeing on 1689/1689 vectors".
- consequence: A browser verifier signing/verifying the UTF-8 bytes of the `sha256:…` string and a Python verifier using the 32 raw bytes reject each other's certs at App. D step 1; a stranger's independent verifier rejects every cert or accepts a mis-signed one depending on which sentence it implemented.
- amendment: Delete "over id bytes" in §2 and write in §2, §2.1 and A.1: "sig = Ed25519(sk, D) where D is the 32 raw bytes of sha256(JCS(cert minus id, sig)); the `id` string is `"sha256:" + lowercase hex(D)`; the signed message is the 32-byte digest (with the domain prefix of S2-03), never the `sha256:` string"; add a conformance vector (cert, D, sig) to App. A.
- sources: stranger-reproducibility, internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: §2 does say `sig` is "over id bytes" and §2.1 says "over the raw 32-byte digest"; since `id` is the ASCII string `sha256:<hex>`, the two sentences admit different messages. The memory citation (browser verifier agreeing on 1689/1689 vectors) is accurate, but the claim that the lab's browser-verifier practice "signs the UTF-8 of the id string" is speculation — sworn.py states nothing in v0.2 verifies a signature and capsule.py has no signatures, so no existing practice is contradicted. §2.1 is the normative, explicit definition and a competent implementer follows it over a gloss inside a JSON example, so the ambiguity is likely resolved correctly; but a second implementer working from the envelope alone could diverge, and the lab's own conformance history shows spec glosses do produce dual-implementation disagreements. Real but minor: strike "over id bytes" and add a vector.

### S2-25 · minor · §2; App. A.1
**Ed25519 key/sig byte encoding and hash-string case are not pinned**

- claim: `ed25519:<base64url>` does not say whether the public key is the raw 32-byte RFC 8032 key or SPKI/DER, and `sha256:<64 lowercase hex>` has no rule that uppercase or non-normalized forms are rejected before hashing.
- evidence: App. A.1: "Keys and signatures as `ed25519:<base64url, no padding>`." `cryptography` (installed, per SCOUT.md) requires the caller to choose `Encoding.Raw` vs `Encoding.DER`; WebCrypto imports raw or SPKI. App. A.1: "Hashes as `sha256:<64 lowercase hex>`" with no validator rule; JCS does not normalize string case, so `sha256:ABC…` and `sha256:abc…` in `refs` yield different ids for the same target.
- consequence: A Python issuer writing SPKI and a JS verifier importing raw fail signature checks; a ref written in uppercase silently fails to resolve or double-counts.
- amendment: App. A.1: "`ed25519:` prefixes base64url (RFC 4648 §5, no padding) of the raw 32-byte public key or raw 64-byte signature per RFC 8032; SPKI/PEM forms are rejected. Validators reject any `sha256:` string that is not exactly 64 lowercase hex characters and any `ed25519:` string of the wrong decoded length."
- sources: crypto-envelope
- verifiers: STANDS: App. A.1 says only 'Keys and signatures as `ed25519:<base64url, no padding>`' — raw RFC 8032 bytes vs SPKI/DER is genuinely unstated, and §2.1's 'use a library' leaves the choice to whichever library the implementer picks (`cryptography` and WebCrypto both accept either). That half is a real interop ambiguity, especially once a JS verifier exists. The hash-case half is weaker than claimed: A.1 already pins `sha256:<64 lowercase hex>` as the normative form and §14 schedules 'schema validation for all eight types', so a validator pattern of `^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$` is the natural reading; the spec does not say uppercase is rejected, but a competent implementer would. Net: minor, correctly rated.

### S2-26 · minor · §2; §2.2; §7.2; §12
**`created` and `observed_at` are issuer-chosen; only the log index is evidence of time, and the spec never tells clients to order by it**

- claim: An issuer can backdate `created`/`observed_at` in the cert body; the log timestamp only upper-bounds, and views that sort or label by `created` display fabricated history.
- evidence: §2 envelope: `"created": "2026-09-07T18:00:00Z"`; §2.2 alias: `"observed_at": "2026-09-07T18:00:00Z"`; §7.2: "The log timestamp is the proof of order" (said for prereg only). §12 'change tracking / version control' and 'fingerprint history for the subject' give no ordering rule.
- consequence: In September an issuer logs an alias fingerprint with `observed_at: 2026-01-01` claiming 'the model behaved like this in January'; the compliance view's history, if ordered or labelled by the body dates, shows a January observation that was never made at the time.
- amendment: §2: "`created` and `observed_at` are issuer assertions. Clients order and label every history by log index and STH timestamp; body timestamps are displayed only beside the STH timestamp in the form `asserted <created>, logged <sth.timestamp>`."
- sources: adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: `created` (§2) and `observed_at` (§2.2) are issuer-written, §7.2's 'The log timestamp is the proof of order' is stated only in the prereg paragraph, and §12's 'change tracking / version control' row ('fingerprint history for the subject') gives no ordering or labelling rule. §8.3 ('Inclusion means "this cert existed at this time," nothing more') and §8.6 (issuer fabrication is mitigated, not prevented) show the spec already treats body dates as non-evidentiary, so the principle is present; only the client rendering rule is missing, and an implementer building a history view would plausibly sort by the field in the cert. That makes it a display/ambiguity defect rather than a wrong verdict — minor stands, not refuted.

### S4-05 · minor · §4.3; App. C; §2.3; §4.4 step 5
**margin(i) and the anchor criterion are first-token quantities, but flip1/2/3 and the `exact` channel compare the whole 64-token greedy sequence**

- claim: The boundary proxy is measured at one position while stability is judged over the entire greedy path, so a high first-token margin says nothing about whether the sequence flips at token 30, and under a chat template the first answer token is often a formatting token with a huge, uninformative margin.
- evidence: §4.3: "For item i, at the first answer token under reference precision: margin(i) = lp(top1) − lp(top2)"; "flip1(i) = fraction of δ1 variants whose greedy output ≠ reference". §2.3 decoding: "max_new_tokens": 64, "stop": ["\n"]. §4.4.5: "the highest-margin items with zero flips under every δ". App. B: "exact | 1 − (matching items / items)".
- consequence: Anchors chosen for top-64 first-token margin still carry dozens of low-margin later positions; their sequence-level nuisance flip rate is not small, which feeds the `identity` false-positive rate (S4-02). The 0.4 margin term in s(i) ranks instruction-following/format items by the margin of a token like "Sure" or "**", so the family cap in §4.4.3 stratifies on noise. Two implementations that disagree on what "first answer token" is (with/without template BOS, leading whitespace token) produce different s(i) and different batteries.
- amendment: Compute margin from the same greedy pass over all emitted positions and define `margin(i) = min_t (lp_top1,t − lp_top2,t)` over the greedy tokens of item i (store the per-position vector in the battery cert); anchors additionally require `margin(i) ≥ M` with M in params (default 3 nats). State precisely which token is position 0 (first token after the chat template's assistant header, after the template hash in §2.3).
- sources: canary-selection
- verifiers: STANDS: Accurate as text: §4.3/App. C define margin(i) at 'the first answer token' while flip1/2/3 and the `exact` distance (App. B; output_sha256 over max_new_tokens 64) are whole-sequence, and no passage defines position 0 relative to the chat template (§3.2's topk channel uses the same undefined 'first 8 answer positions'). The headline consequence does not follow: App. C requires anchor(i) to have zero flips under q8_0, q4_k_m, every δ2 nuisance variant and three temp-0.2 samples over the entire sequence, so an anchor's later positions have already been stress-tested far harder than a first-token margin would; the margin only orders the survivors. The formatting-token case ('Sure', '**') collapses the 0.4 term toward a constant and degrades s(i) to its flip terms, which is a selection-power loss, not a wrong verdict, and the family cap stratifies on family labels, not on margin. The two-implementations concern is answered by §2.3: the recipe pins `harness.commit`, so the position-0 convention is fixed by the one pinned implementation even though the prose should state it. Survives at minor as a recipe-wording gap the author should close in App. C.

### S4-17 · minor · §4 title; §4.1–4.6; §11; App. C
**"canary" collides with the sworn measurement machinery spec committed two days earlier, where a canary is a planted known-false span with its own gate G-C**

- claim: The same repo now has two incompatible meanings of "canary" and "canary rate/gate" in two binding documents, and verdict/vocabulary changes are operator-gated by doctrine.
- evidence: papers/sworn/SPEC_sworn_measurement_machinery_2026_09_05.md line 243–245: "## The canary rule — A **canary** is a well-formed sworn span whose receipt is known ... not to hold what the sentence says"; line 473: gate "G-C ... `k` = canaries whose verdict is FAILED, `n` = canaries planted"; line 56 `canaries.py the canary inserter`. v8 §4: "canary-v1 — model-specific, selected against the subject to be sensitive by construction"; §11: "styxx battery select ... # canary-v1". SCOUT: "Verdict vocabulary changes are operator-gated."
- consequence: A reader of a RESULT that says "canary rate 0.97" cannot tell whether it is the sworn verifier's recall on planted falsehoods or the share of battery items that flipped; CLI help, cert `kind` strings, and future papers will cross-cite the wrong object.
- amendment: OPERATOR-GATED: rename the v8 object (options: `sentinel-v1`, `edge-v1`, `probe-v1`) throughout §4, §11, §12, §13, App. C and the battery `kind` enum; or keep "canary" in v8 and rename the sworn planted spans, which requires re-issuing a sworn spec. Do not commit entry #0 with the collision in place.
- sources: canary-selection
- verifiers: STANDS: Confirmed: papers/sworn/SPEC_sworn_measurement_machinery_2026_09_05.md line 243 defines 'A **canary** is a well-formed sworn span whose receipt is known ... not to hold', line 473 gate G-C counts planted canaries, line 56 lists canaries.py, and line 609 even uses the phrase 'canary rate'. The v8 spec (§4, §4.5 kind enum, §11, §13, App. C) uses 'canary' for boundary-sensitive battery items, so two binding documents in one repo do collide; a third prior usage exists in experiments/glimmer_dayzero_2026_08_10/probes_manifest.md ('canary suite', 'canary inputs' = glimmer probes, which v8 §7.4 folds into δ1). Downgraded: the doctrine citation is overstated — memory styxx-calibration-leg-2026-09-05.md gates only the specific SWORN-HELD verdict rename and the rounding floor, not vocabulary in general — and no verdict, number, or cert verification is wrong under the collision; the consequence is reader/cross-citation confusion, so this is a naming ambiguity to fix before entry #0 freezes the hash, not a major defect.

### S4-18 · minor · §4.4; §4.5; §4.6; §15.3; §3.1; §8.6
**The public battery is a ≤1088-item distillation/pinning target: clear-text prompts plus clear-text outputs let the monitored party hold the battery at `same`, and the 'Re-select after any fine-tune' remedy presupposes the detection it replaces**

- claim: The battery cert stores prompts in the clear and the fingerprint cert stores output_text in the clear in a public GitHub-Pages log, while a redacted battery is barred from public claims; so any public drift claim requires publishing the exact (prompt, greedy output) pairs the monitored party must keep stable, and a party who fine-tunes, distils or caches against them zeroes both `drift` and `identity`; re-selection requires already knowing the fine-tune happened.
- evidence: §4.5: "Prompts are stored in the clear unless the battery is marked redacted. A redacted battery cannot be used for a public claim". §3.1: "output_text": "… (omitted when redacted=true)". §4.6: "A model fine-tuned *on* its own canaries would defeat them. Re-select after any fine-tune; the selection cost is one δ-sweep." §4.4.5: "Anchors should never change. An anchor flip means a tokenizer or weights swap, not drift". §15.3: "canaries — selected per model, after release — can't be trained against in advance." §8.2: "A git repository, published on GitHub Pages." §5.2 has no row comparing subject hashes; §8.6 lists nothing about a party special-casing the published battery.
- consequence: Steps: (1) obtain the canary battery cert from the log; (2) add the (prompt, reference greedy output) pairs as a KL/behaviour-cloning term when training the replacement, or for an open-weight model served by a provider, cache/special-case those prompts; (3) ship. `exact`, `seqlp` (if matched within floor), and all 64 anchors read unchanged while the model changes elsewhere. For a weights subject only `weights_sha256` would catch it, and `verify --ref` is never told to compare subject hashes. The mitigation is unavailable to a third-party monitor who does not know a fine-tune happened: the drift detector is defeated by reading the log it publishes to.
- amendment: Replace the §4.6 bullet with: "canary-v1 detects changes that were not optimised against the published battery. A party that trains or distils against the battery defeats it; publication makes this possible and the spec accepts it. Re-selection is a response to a *detected* change, not a defence against a hidden one." Add the same to §8.6. Add to §5.2 the subject-hash row (see S4-02). OPERATOR-GATED beyond that: (a) sealed canaries via the §7.2 commitment mechanism — publish `sha256(salt || JCS(items))` in the battery cert, keep prompts sealed, reveal (with salt) only in the result cert that claims drift, then re-select; (b) two batteries per subject: public fixed-v1 for public claims, sealed canary-v1 for detection with reveal-on-drift, amending §4.5 so a sealed (not redacted) battery can back a public drift claim once revealed; (c) hold back a sealed half of each canary battery (commitment logged, prompts revealed at rotation), displayed as a note since it is not stranger-reproducible until reveal. The `output_text` default follows the same decision.
- sources: canary-selection, adversary
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §4.5 stores prompts in clear, §3.1 stores output_text, §8.2 publishes on GitHub Pages, §15.3 says canaries 'can't be trained against in advance', and §4.6's remedy 'Re-select after any fine-tune' presupposes knowing the fine-tune happened. But §4.6 already discloses the defeat ('A model fine-tuned on its own canaries would defeat them'), and the attack is inherent to principle 1 (a stranger-reproducible battery must be public); the spec never claims to detect optimisation against the published battery, and `verify` still returns the correct verdict for the question it asks. The non-gated part of the amendment is a wording change to §4.6/§15.3 and a threat-model note; the sealed-battery designs are new work, correctly marked operator-gated. No verdict or number is wrong as written, so minor.

### S4-22 · minor · §4.5; §11; §14
**`battery select` cannot take a reference fingerprint and nothing issues fixed-v1**

- claim: The canary battery body requires `selected_against` but the CLI has no input for it; §14 says a fixed-v1 battery cert is issued and no command can produce it.
- evidence: §11: `styxx battery      select --pool <file> --subject <spec> --n 256 --k 64      # canary-v1`; §4.5: `"selected_against": "sha256:<reference fingerprint cert id>  (canary only)"`; §14: "fixed-v1 from styxx-bench after the gold fix; battery cert issued."
- consequence: Week-1 deliverable (fixed-v1 cert) has no producer; canary battery certs are emitted with an unfillable required field.
- amendment: §11: `styxx battery select --pool <pool cert id> --reference <fingerprint cert id> --n 256 --k 64` and `styxx battery fixed --source <path> --version v1` (emits `kind: fixed-v1`, no anchors).
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §11 gives `styxx battery select --pool <file> --subject <spec> --n 256 --k 64` with no reference-fingerprint input, §4.5 requires `selected_against` (canary only), and no §11 command emits `kind: fixed-v1` although §14 week 1 says 'battery cert issued'. Downgraded because a competent implementer resolves both: `select` already takes `--subject` and must run the reference subject for the δ-sweep (§4.2 'run on the reference subject, reference precision'), so it can mint or locate the reference fingerprint it selected against; and the fixed-v1 producer is an obvious missing subcommand, not a design gap that yields a wrong verdict or an unverifiable cert. The amendment (explicit `--reference` and `battery fixed`) is the right fix but is a CLI-surface omission likely resolved correctly anyway.

### S4-23 · minor · §4; §13.4; §2.3; §15.3
**Yearly rotation of fixed-v1 makes predecessor diffs impossible across the boundary — §2.3 forbids the comparison §4 promises**

- claim: Certs are comparable only with identical `battery` ids, so a model fingerprinted on fixed-v2 has no comparable predecessor cert on fixed-v1; 'old versions stay valid for old certs' does not give the new model a predecessor diff.
- evidence: §4: "Rotated yearly (fixed-v2 …); old versions stay valid for old certs." §2.3: "Two certs are **comparable** iff `battery`, `decoding`, `chat_template_sha256`, and `system_prompt_sha256` match exactly. Otherwise `verify` exits 3". §13: "4. **predecessor diff** — `verify --diff` against the prior model in the family on fixed-v1 only". §15.3: "Contamination is handled by yearly rotation".
- consequence: Every docket in the year after a rotation exits 3 on item 4, or the operator re-runs the predecessor on fixed-v2 (unscheduled GPU hours, and a 'predecessor' cert dated after the successor).
- amendment: Add to §4: "For 12 months after a rotation, dockets run both fixed-v(n) and fixed-v(n−1); the predecessor diff is on the older battery; the newer battery's predecessor diff is issued when the predecessor is re-fingerprinted (GPU budget counted in §13)." Drop 'contamination is handled by yearly rotation' from §15.3 — the items are public from day one.
- sources: feasibility-and-sequencing
- verifiers: STANDS: Textually accurate: §4 rotates fixed-v1 yearly, §2.3 makes certs comparable only on identical `battery`, §13 item 4 names fixed-v1 only, §15.3 says contamination is 'handled' by rotation; nothing elsewhere schedules a predecessor re-fingerprint or a dual-battery window. But the consequence is a process gap, not a wrong verdict: §2.3 exit 3 is the honesty rule working, and any competent operator resolves it the obvious way by fingerprinting the predecessor on fixed-v(n) (a cert about a model is not invalidated by being dated after its successor's; no spec text forbids it). The only real defect is the unqualified §15.3 sentence: items are public from day one, so rotation bounds the contamination window rather than handling it. Downgrade from major to minor.

### S4-24 · minor · §4.4 step 2; App. C
**Top-N ranking has no tie-break; s(i) takes few distinct values, so the selected battery is not deterministic**

- claim: flip1 ∈ {0, 0.5, 1} (two δ1 variants) and flip3 ∈ {0, 1/3, 2/3, 1} make the 0.6 term take at most 12 distinct values, so many items tie at the cut and "Rank the remainder by s(i)" leaves the battery membership implementation-dependent.
- evidence: §4.2: "δ1 | precision: bf16 → q8_0 → q4_k_m" (reference plus two variants); "δ3 | temp 0.2, n=3 samples". §4.4.2: "Rank the remainder by s(i)." No tie rule anywhere in §4 or App. C.
- consequence: Two runs of `battery select` over identical inputs, differing only in sort stability, emit batteries with different membership at the boundary and therefore different battery cert ids.
- amendment: App. C: "ties in s(i) are broken by margin ascending, then item_id ascending (A.3 order)"; state it in params as `tie_break`.
- sources: canary-selection
- verifiers: STANDS: §4.4 step 2 and App. C rank 'by s(i)' with no tie rule, confirmed; params in §4.5 carry tau/w_flip/w_margin/n/k_anchors/max_family_share and no tie_break. The finding's premise overstates the problem: the 0.6 term does take at most 12 values, but the 0.4 margin term is continuous, so s(i) is not few-valued. Ties are still realistic (bf16 logits quantize margins to ~3 significant digits, and identical-margin items near the cut are plausible), and step 3's stratification has no stated algorithm either, so two implementations can emit different battery membership and hence different battery cert ids from identical inputs, against §1.1's 'same evidence, same id, anywhere'. Stays minor: App. D never requires a stranger to reproduce selection, only to verify the cert as issued.

### S4-27 · minor · §4.5; §8.1; §1.2
**Redacted battery contradicts 'the recipe stays complete'**

- claim: A redacted battery withholds prompts, so a fingerprint on it is not recipe-complete, yet §8.1 says redaction never touches the recipe.
- evidence: §4.5: "Prompts are stored in the clear unless the battery is marked `redacted`." §8.1: "Redacted certs store hashes of outputs, never the outputs; the recipe stays complete." §1.2: "If it doesn't, `verify` refuses to compare and says so."
- consequence: `verify --ref` on an internal fingerprint must refuse (§1.2) but §4.5 permits such fingerprints to exist and be logged.
- amendment: §8.1: "Redaction applies to outputs only. A battery with redacted prompts makes every fingerprint on it non-verifiable; such certs carry `redacted_battery: true` and `verify --ref` exits 3 on them."
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: The claimed contradiction rests on a misread: §8.1's 'redacted certs store hashes of outputs, never the outputs; the recipe stays complete' describes the fingerprint `redacted: true` flag of §3.1 (output_text omitted), not battery redaction, and for output redaction the statement is true. What survives is smaller: §4.5 permits a prompt-redacted battery for an 'internal' black-box fingerprint, bans public claims on it, yet no field marks the resulting fingerprint and no `verify` rule names the case, so §1.2's refusal is left to implementer judgement and the 'public claim' ban is unenforced in code. A stranger running App. D step 4 on such a cert would fail, but §4.5 already says such certs support no public claim. Real but minor ambiguity; the amendment's `redacted_battery` marker plus exit 3 is the right shape.

### S5-05 · minor · §5.1 step 3
**The 99th-percentile branch is dead for both defaults and, where reachable, unspecified and stricter than max**

- claim: |D_c| is 10 or 28 for the only two R values the spec names, both below 30, so the percentile rule never fires; if R ≥ 9 it fires with an undefined percentile method, and its value on n values is below the max, not a robust alternative to it.
- evidence: §5.1: "R = 5 minimum (10 pairwise distances). R = 8 for dockets (28 pairs)." and "`floor_c` = max(D_c) if |D_c| < 30, else the 99th percentile of D_c."
- consequence: For 28 values numpy gives 27.73 (`linear`, the default), 28.0 (`nearest`, `higher`, `hazen`, `weibull`), 27.0 (`lower`) on the sequence 1..28 — a stranger re-deriving the floor with a different (equally valid) percentile definition gets a different floor, a different verdict at the boundary, and a different cert id. Where it fires, the linear 99th percentile of 36 values is ≈0.65·max + 0.35·second-max, i.e. LOWER than max — the branch raises the false-positive rate (simulated 5.2% vs 4.0% at R=8) rather than trimming outliers as the wording implies.
- amendment: Delete the percentile branch (floor = max(D_c) for all R, with the α from S5-03 printed), or if a trimmed floor is wanted state it exactly: `floor_c = D_c sorted ascending [⌈0.99·|D_c|⌉ − 1]` (equivalently numpy `method="higher"`), with the |D_c| threshold set to a value some named R actually reaches (R ≥ 9 gives 36 pairs).
- sources: noise-floor-statistics
- verifiers: STANDS: Accurate: §5.1 names R=5 (10 pairs) and R=8 (28 pairs), both below the `|D_c| < 30` threshold, so the percentile branch is dead for every scheduled run, and §11 exposes `--runs` so R ≥ 9 (36 pairs) reaches it with no percentile method named. Two implementations (the lab already keeps a Python and a JS verifier in parity) would derive different floors from the same stored distribution, so a stranger re-deriving the floor can disagree with the cert at the boundary. Downgraded to minor: no default or docket ever fires the branch, the floor value is carried in the signed cert bytes so ids and verdicts of issued certs are unaffected, and 'percentile is below max' is a design choice rather than a defect. Real ambiguity, unreached in v8.0 as scheduled.

### S5-11 · minor · §5.2; App. A.4
**`d ≤ floor_c` is decided by exact double comparison of quantities recomputed on different machines**

- claim: Floors and distances are means of floating-point sums whose last bits depend on summation order and hardware; a comparison with no tolerance makes boundary verdicts depend on that noise.
- evidence: §5.2: "d(new, ref) ≤ floor_c | `same`". App. A.4: "Log-probs are stored as IEEE-754 doubles rendered by JCS. They are compared numerically". App. B distances are all means over items.
- consequence: A stranger recomputing the reference's own distance on their machine can get d = floor + 1 ulp and see `drift`/`transient` where the issuer saw `same`; the confirmation-run row 'borderline' case is then a coin flip on float order.
- amendment: Compare after rounding both sides to a stated precision per channel (e.g. `round(x, 9)`), with the precision recorded in the result cert; define all App. B means with a fixed summation order (item_id ascending, math.fsum).
- sources: noise-floor-statistics
- verifiers: STANDS: §5.2's `d ≤ floor_c` has no tolerance and Appendix B defines every distance as a 'mean over items' without fixing summation order or method. The finding's mechanism is partly wrong — the floor is read from the cert (§5.1.3 stores it), not recomputed by the stranger, and for continuous channels (seqlp, topk, resid) a fresh distance landing within one ulp of the floor has negligible probability. But for discrete channels (exact: 1 − m/N; lens: integer |Δ|/L averaged) exact ties d == floor are common, and mean(|Δ_i|/L) under sequential vs pairwise (numpy) vs fsum summation can differ in the last bit, so a tie flips between `same` and exceedance depending on implementation. The confirmation run only converts that to `same (transient)`, and A.4's 'compared numerically' does not fix it. Narrow but real; minor stands.

### S8-19 · minor · §8.1; §8.3; §11
**Duplicate appends of a content-addressed cert are neither forbidden nor handled; `prove <id>` is ambiguous**

- claim: Because the id is content-derived and charon v0.1 explicitly permitted duplicate lines, the spec must say whether the same cert may occupy two leaves (the same cert re-submitted; a second signature by a different `sig` encoding); if it may, `prove <id>`/`verify-inclusion <id>` has no unique leaf index, and inclusion proofs are defined by cert id rather than leaf index.
- evidence: §8.1: "**Inclusion proof** — audit path leaf → root for a given cert id and STH." §11: "prove <id> | verify-inclusion <id> <sth>". `papers/charon/SPEC_charon_v01_2026_09_02.md` E13: "duplicates are permitted: a line is a crossing, not an artifact".
- consequence: Two implementations pick different leaves for the same id; a proof generated by one fails in the other for the same STH, and a mirror may verify a proof for a leaf the reader did not look at.
- amendment: Add to §8.3: "Append rejects a cert whose id already occupies a leaf (exit 4, duplicate)." §8.1: "Inclusion proofs are stated for `(leaf_index, leaf_hash, tree_size, root_hash)`; proof commands take a leaf index; `prove <id>` resolves to the unique index (printing it) or refuses."
- sources: transparency-log, crypto-envelope
- verifiers: STANDS: Accurate that the spec is silent: §2.1 rejects only bad id/sig at append, §8.3 says nothing about duplicate ids, and §8.1/§11 state proofs and `prove`/`verify-inclusion` by cert id rather than leaf index, while the repo's own `styxx/transparency.py` (lines 123-134) addresses proofs by `leaf_index`. The E13 citation is real (SPEC_charon_v01 line 218) but weak evidence, since the scout notes record that v8's charon is a different object under the same name. Materiality is lower than claimed: A.1 (no-padding base64url) plus deterministic ed25519 means a re-submitted cert yields an identical leaf hash, and `verify-inclusion <id> <sth>` takes no proof argument, so any valid audit path for that leaf hash verifies against the root regardless of which index was chosen; no verdict is wrong and any implementer would take the first occurrence for "existed at this time". Downgrade to minor: a real interop ambiguity in proof addressing, not a wrong-verdict hole.

### S9-04 · minor · App. B; §9
**resid JSD is unspecified (log base, zero handling), so a challenger's distance and the target's floor are not guaranteed to be in the same units**

- claim: Jensen–Shannon divergence has two common bases (e, 2) and the lab's existing implementation adds 1e-12 smoothing; none of this is fixed in the spec, and §9 compares a challenger's `distance` (their implementation) to the target's `floor` (the issuer's).
- evidence: App. B: "mean over items of the Jensen–Shannon divergence between normalized per-layer residual-norm profiles". `C:\Users\heyzo\clawd\wt\v8\styxx\thought.py:936-943` computes JSD with natural log and `+ 1e-12` smoothing. §9 body: `"exact": { "distance": 0.12, "target_floor": 0.02 }`.
- consequence: A log2 implementation reports distances 1.44× the natural-log ones; a challenge computed in log2 against a floor computed in nats exceeds the floor ~44% more often than it should; the `disputed` status (§9) then fires on an implementation difference. A layer with exactly zero residual-update norm (possible under bf16 for degenerate items) is either handled by smoothing or raises, depending on the implementation.
- amendment: App. B: 'JSD in nats (natural log), computed as 0.5·KL(p‖m)+0.5·KL(q‖m) with 0·log 0 = 0 and no smoothing; profiles are L1-normalized after clamping negatives to 0 (norms are non-negative by construction).' Require every distance in a challenge cert to be produced by the `styxx` version named in `recipe.harness` and record `harness.version` in `body.environment`.
- sources: noise-floor-statistics
- verifiers: STANDS: App. B only says 'Jensen–Shannon divergence between normalized per-layer residual-norm profiles' with no base, no smoothing, no zero rule; the cited file C:\Users\heyzo\clawd\wt\v8\styxx\thought.py (the `js` branch of `_vec_distance`, ~lines 935-943) does compute it with math.log (nats) and 1e-12 smoothing, as claimed. App. B's closing sentence ('floor and distance are always in the same units') only covers floor vs distance inside one implementation; §2.3 comparability deliberately excludes `harness`, so a challenger on a different harness version or a second implementation (e.g. scipy's jensenshannon, which returns the square root) is 'comparable' yet in different units, and §9 compares that distance to the target's floor. Partly mitigated: the recipe pins harness name/version/commit, so a stranger following Appendix D step 4 runs the named styxx and gets identical arithmetic — hence not a blocker. Minor is correct: an under-specified distance function for a spec whose principle 1 is 'same evidence, same id, anywhere' and whose doctrine (PR #64, second verifier agreeing on conformance vectors) demands independent reimplementation.

### S9-05 · minor · §9; §11
**`log challenge` and `verify --ref` overlap; `instrument run` output type is unstated**

- claim: A challenge is 'just' a cert built from a stranger's fingerprint plus the target, which is what `verify --ref` already computes, and `instrument run <id>` does not say which cert it emits.
- evidence: §11: `log ... challenge <target> <own>`; §6: `styxx verify --ref <fingerprint cert>  # re-run the recipe now, compare, confirm on drift`; §11: `instrument   list | prereg <file> | run <id> | adversary <id> | promote ...`.
- consequence: Two commands produce two different certs (result kind=verify vs challenge) from the same computation; readers cannot tell which the stranger should log.
- amendment: §6: add `styxx verify --ref <cert> --challenge` which emits the challenge cert (refs the verify result it derived from); remove `log challenge`. §11: `instrument run <id> --subject <spec>` → emits `result` `kind = reading` (or per the docket decision).
- sources: internal-consistency
- verifiers: STANDS: Half of this refutes: the 'which should the stranger log' consequence is answered by §9 plus Appendix D step 5 ('log a challenge'), and `log challenge <target> <own>` vs `verify --diff` emitting different cert types from the same App. B arithmetic is a design choice (a public dispute carries refs + environment; a verify result does not), not a defect any implementer would get wrong. What survives is the `instrument run <id>` output: §11 names the subcommand, §13 item 5 requires 'instrument readings' to be logged as certs, but §2 enumerates eight types and §7.2's result kinds are {confirmatory, pilot, robustness, verify, response} — 'reading' is nowhere, and readings otherwise appear only inside action-cert bodies (§10) and the attest projection (§7.1). An implementer must invent a type or kind for a docket-mandated cert. Kept at minor (an unstated payload, likely resolved as result kind=confirmatory refs prereg), with the `log challenge` removal amendment rejected.

## Operator decisions

Every amendment below is a design choice with trade-offs; the draft leaves the v0.1 text in place and marks the spot. Nothing here is decided by the review.

- **S0-02** (blocker, §0 header; §8.1; §8.3; §10; §13; §14) — OPERATOR-GATED on the type set (verdict/type vocabulary changes are operator-gated per doctrine). Option 1 (least change): entry #0 is a `result` cert, `body.kind = "document"` (or `spec`), `body = {path, git_blob_sha1, content_sha256 (LF-normalized UTF-8), commit}`, signed by the lab key; the hourly roll is an `action` cert with `body.action_kind = "roll"` and `body.sublog_sth = {log_id, tree_size, root_hash, timestamp, sig}` whose `sig` uses the sub-log STH domain string; the summary is a `result` kind `document`. Option 2: add a new type `attachment` with body `{ "sha256": "...", "media": "text/markdown | application/json", "role": "spec | sublog_sth | summary" }` and use it for entry #0, the hourly sub-log STH, and the docket summary, and say so in §2. In either option: state the LF-normalization rule in A.1, pin the spec with `.gitattributes` `eol=lf` as the sworn documents already are, and rewrite §0: "becomes binding when committed; its sha256 is logged as charon entry #0 when the log first exists (§14 week 4)."
- **S2-01** (blocker, §2.2; §2.3; §5.1; §5.3; §9; App. D steps 4-5) — OPERATOR-GATED. First, in all cases: split the subject into identity fields `S_floor = (weights_sha256, config_sha256, tokenizer_sha256, precision)` for weights subjects and `(provider, alias)` for alias subjects, and environment fields (`runtime`, `hardware`) that are recorded and printed but never keyed; add identity fields to the §2.3 comparability rule (mismatch → exit 3, a different subject is never `drift`); rewrite §5.3 as "Floors are per (S_floor, B, recipe_core)" naming exactly which fields void the floor; add `noise_floor.nuisance_varied: ["batch_size", "item_order", "gpu", "driver", ...]` so a reader sees what the floor covers. Then choose: (A) hardware is nuisance — a public fingerprint's floor is valid only if `noise_floor.runs` spans ≥ 2 distinct (gpu, driver) pairs and ≥ 2 runtime versions, recorded as `noise_floor.hardware_span`; otherwise the cert is labelled `floor_scope: single-hardware` and a cross-hardware `verify --ref` can report only `same`/`inconclusive`/`reproduction (hardware not in floor)`, never `drift`; or (B) hardware is identity — a stranger runs their OWN R-run floor on their box and `drift` requires exceeding max(floor_cert, floor_stranger); cross-hardware runs are `challenge`/`reproduction` events and §9's disputed rule is restated on that basis.
- **S4-01** (blocker, §4.4 step 1; §5.1; §5.2; §5.3; §6; App. B) — (1) Delete "they go into the noise-floor pool (§5)"; replace with "they are excluded and listed in `body.excluded` with their flip2". The floor MUST be computed on battery B itself. (2) Add to §5.1: "Floor runs are fresh runs, disjoint from every run used in §4 selection; `noise_floor.runs` ∩ `battery.body.selection_runs` = ∅ and `verify` exits 2 if the intersection is non-empty"; add `selection_runs: ["sha256:…"]` to the battery body. (3) Add §5.3 bullet: "For canary-v1 the floor is measured on items selected for flip2 = 0 and is a lower bound on nuisance variation; the cert records `noise_floor.selection_biased: true` and `verify` prints it." (4) Define `ratio` as null when floor = 0 (never a non-finite double). (5) OPERATOR-GATED floor quantum for discrete channels: option A — `floor_exact = max(max(D_c), q/N)` with q ≥ 2 flips so a single flip is never drift; option B — floor from a one-sided binomial upper bound (e.g. 95% Clopper-Pearson) on the per-item flip rate given (R choose 2)·N item-comparisons with k observed flips, stored as `floor_upper`; option C — accept floor 0 and rename the verdict for 1..q flips `changed-below-power` with no drift vocabulary. Also OPERATOR-GATED whether R ≥ 8 is required for canary floors. Record the chosen rule in the fingerprint cert.
- **S4-02** (blocker, §4.4 step 5; §5.2; §6; §1 principle 3; App. C) — (1) `identity (hash: <which>)` is emitted only when a subject field (`weights_sha256`, `tokenizer_sha256`, `config_sha256`) differs between ref and new — add that row to §5.2, reported always, independent of anchors. (2) Anchor flips become a separately reported count `anchors_changed: k/64` with verdict `anchor_change` (no cause asserted); delete "not drift" in §4.4.5 and write "reported in addition to the channel verdicts". (3) `anchor_change` requires the same confirmation run as `drift`; a single-run flip is `anchor_change (unconfirmed)`. (4) Give anchors their own floor from the R fresh runs: `floor_anchor` = max over run pairs of the number of anchors that differ, recorded in `noise_floor.per_channel.anchor`; escalation to exit status only when k exceeds `floor_anchor` on both runs and k ≥ K_id (OPERATOR-GATED, e.g. 8 of 64, chosen so the measured single-anchor cross-environment rate gives P(false identity) < 1%). (5) Record |δ2| explicitly in the battery cert and require |δ| ≥ 10 so `flip = 0` bounds the item's flip probability below 26% at 95%. (6) §5.2: "Overall verdict is the SET of per-channel verdicts plus `identity` when present; `identity` never replaces a channel verdict"; text output prints `identity + drift(exact, seqlp)`. (7) Fix the override order: subject-hash mismatch on a comparability field ⇒ exit 2 with a named reason. §6: exit 1 = drift; add exit code 4 = identity (with or without drift).
- **S4-06** (blocker, §4.1; §4.2 δ2; §5.1; §9; App. D steps 4–5) — OPERATOR-GATED. Options: (a) require the floor to include at least two distinct hardware/driver configurations before a canary cert may carry a public drift claim, and tag single-hardware floors `floor_scope: single-hardware`, with challenges from other hardware classified by clients as `out-of-scope reproduction`, not disputes; (b) define challenge validity against a challenger-side floor: a challenge is valid only if the challenger also submits its own R-run floor on its hardware and d(new, ref) exceeds max(target floor, challenger floor); (c) make the `exact` canary distance first-token or prefix-k only to lower numeric sensitivity, accepting reduced drift power. Whichever is chosen, §8.6 must list cross-hardware numerics as a known limit.
- **S7-03** (blocker, §7.2; §7.4; §14 (Weeks 1–4, Weeks 8–12); §8.6) — Replace the §7.4 depth row with: "depth (mean attributed layer) | lab | CLOSED_NEGATIVE on gemma-2-2b / short-form QA (papers/depth-truth/FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth_2026_07_03.md, sha256 of its certificate); first log entries for depth are a `result` cert of kind `confirmatory` wrapping the committed FINDING and its certificate, refs = sha256 of PREREG_v2.md bytes. Any NEW depth prereg is v3, refs that certificate, states which aggregation changed, and must name a different (model_family, task_family) or a different endpoint." §14: delete "#2 keystone v2 prereg"; entry #2 is either "result cert over the committed keystone negative" or a v3 prereg written fresh. §14 GPU line: OPERATOR-GATED — (a) run a v3 depth prereg with a changed aggregation, or (b) reassign the GPU to canary δ1 sweeps and the plan-of-record measurement.
- **S0-01** (major, §0 header; §0 non-goals; §3 header line; §7.2; §8; §14) — OPERATOR-GATED: (a) rename the v8 log (any name not `charon`, e.g. 'the ledger' or 'charon v2 — a Merkle log, not the ferry log') and add §8.0: "styxx.charon v0.1 (`styxx/charon.py`, `papers/charon/charon.log.jsonl`, head 1647a26…) is history and is not rebuilt, renumbered or reinterpreted by this spec; its 243 lines stay under their own schema; its head is pinned as a sworn span and becomes the FIRST entry of the new log; new-log `result` kind `verify` entries MAY be ingested into the ferry log as a fourth artifact kind in a later charon version"; or (b) number the v8 log charon v1.0 with an explicit migration section: the v0.1 log is frozen at head 1647a26…, its file/HEAD is pinned by an early v1 entry, the seven v0.1 statuses map to named v8 verdicts, v0.1 lines are appended as `attachment` certs with `role: charon_v01_line`, append keeps re-derivation (§8.3 must say 'CI re-derives body from recipe where the cert type allows it', not only 'checks signature'), and "Charon v0 is a git repo" is reworded because v0 names a superseded log at a5cf9ec. In both options: bind this spec today by its own commit and a v0.1 line of kind `sworn` over the spec's sidecar, not by a future entry #0 (the spec's hash is entry #1 or the header, never #0); delete both occurrences of "first real use" — the lab has timestamped preregistrations by commit since PREREG_v2 (`6af88ed`) and the EXTERNAL-1 sealed key digest.
- **S1-01** (major, §1.1; §2; §2.1) — OPERATOR-GATED: (a) keep §2.1 and rewrite §1.1 to "Same bytes, same id" (drop the evidence claim); or (b) define two addresses: `evidence_id = sha256(JCS({styxx, type, subject, recipe, body}))` and `id = sha256(JCS(cert minus id, sig))`; `refs` and `selected_against` point at `id`, reproduction/dedup compare `evidence_id`; or (c) mirror the lab rule verbatim — §2.1: "`id` = sha256(JCS(cert with `id`, `sig` and `created` removed)); `created` is disclosed as unsealed metadata; the log's STH timestamp is the only proof of time" (§7.2 already calls the log timestamp "the proof of order") — and optionally also drop `issuer` from the preimage, signing the full envelope; if `issuer` stays in the digest under (c), cross-issuer ids remain distinct and §1.1 must be reworded to "Same bytes, same id" as well.
- **S1-02** (major, §1.5; §8.1; §8.2; §8.4; §15.5; §14) — §8.1 add: "A consistency proof establishes append-only only between two STHs the reader obtained from independent channels. With one operator key and no external pin, the log establishes internal consistency and nothing more, and `styxx log verify-*` prints that sentence." Require, before the word 'append-only' appears in any output: an STH pinned outside the repo (a sworn span in another committed document, a second-party mirror record, or an anchor). §15.5 → OPERATOR-GATED: anchor from the first STH (cost is a fee) versus wait for a mirror; note that until either exists v0 is 'a signed list'.
- **S11-01** (major, §11; §7.1; §8) — OPERATOR-GATED: either (a) rename the v8 verbs so 7.x names keep their meaning until removed — `styxx cert fingerprint`, `styxx cert verify`, `styxx charon ...` for the transparency log (matching `styxx/charon.py` and the papers), `styxx view compliance`; 7.x `fingerprint` → `cogn fingerprint` (or removed, shim); `log tail|stats|…` → `audit …` — or (b) put v8 under a new console script (`styxx8`) for one minor version. In either case add Appendix E: all 47 top-level 7.x subcommands (and every existing `log` subcommand) with one of {kept, shim→X, removed-no-replacement}.
- **S13-01** (major, §13; §14 weeks 4–8; §2.2) — OPERATOR-GATED: (a) restrict v8.0 dockets to models that fit 8 GB in bf16 (gemma-2-2b, Llama-3.2-3B, Qwen2.5-3B — all cached) and state the ceiling in §13; (b) rent a cloud GPU for the 9B/8B dockets and record its `hardware` in the subject (then the noise floor is that machine's, not this box's); (c) define the reference precision for >4B models as q8_0 under llama.cpp and drop bf16 from §13.1. In every case add 'free ≥60 GB disk' as a week-1 task.
- **S13-02** (major, §13.3; §5.3; §6) — Add to §6: "`--diff A B` uses A's floor (the `--ref`/left cert's (S,B,recipe) floor), prints `floor_owner: A`, and prints B's floor beside it. If A has no floor for its own (S, B, recipe), exit 2. Cross-subject diffs are labelled `cross-subject` in the result cert and are never `same`; the verdict vocabulary for them is `within-floor(A)` / `beyond-floor(A)`." (Vocabulary change is operator-gated per lab doctrine.)
- **S14-03** (major, §14; §0; §13) — Add a section 'Relation to PLAN_the_next_level_2026_09_02' stating one of — OPERATOR-GATED: (a) v8 is scheduled after legs 2 and 4 of the plan of record, which remain first and are not edited; (b) v8 supersedes the plan of record, named and dated, with the reasons; (c) v8 runs in parallel with a stated GPU/attention split. In every case: inherit the claim ledger verbatim by reference, and either commit the '90-day plan' as a document with a path or delete every reference to it.
- **S2-05** (major, §2.2; §4; §4.5; §5.1; §8.6; §15.3) — Add to §4.6 and §8.6, verbatim: "For alias subjects, a `same` verdict is evidence only against a provider that does not condition on the request. A provider that recognises published prompts and serves them from a pinned model or a cache defeats every black-box channel; the spec has no defence and does not claim one." Label alias verify results in §6 output and §12 as `same (published-prompt)`. OPERATOR-GATED: whether to add an alias-only `probe` mechanism (a small set of never-published paraphrase items generated per verify run, reported as a separate advisory channel with no floor) — trade-off: it violates principle 2 (not reproducible by a stranger) and so must be a note, not a claim.
- **S2-14** (major, §2 `issuer`; §2.1; §8.2 `keys/log.pub`; §8.3; §8.4; §9; §15.1; App. D steps 1-2) — OPERATOR-GATED. Minimum: state that issuer identity IS the key (`name` is a display hint, never matched); add `charon/keys/issuers.json` (the v0 allowlist CI reads) and have step 1 check that `issuer.key` appears in the log's issuer roster entry (a roster cert signed by the log key, id fixed in this spec) or report `unknown issuer`; pin the log public key's fingerprint in this spec's text (entry #0) and in the README and require `styxx log mirror --expect-log-key <fp>`; define rotation/revocation as log entries — a `result` cert with `body.kind = "key"` signed by the old key naming the new key (rotation) or itself (revocation), reffing the old key's last STH and effective from the STH that includes it; validators treat certs whose issuer key is revoked and whose inclusion STH postdates the revocation as invalid. Options for Disputed: (a) replace "independent issuers (distinct keys, distinct hardware)" with "two challenges from distinct issuer keys; independence of issuers and hardware is self-reported and displayed as such"; (b) count only challenger keys that have an included fingerprint cert of their own older than the target; (c) require out-of-band key registration (GitHub account or DNS TXT) before a key's challenges count.
- **S3-08** (major, §3.2; §10; §13) — OPERATOR-GATED: (a) add `result` `kind = reading` with body `{ instrument, version, code_sha256, tier, tier_basis: <promotion id | null>, readings }`; or (b) reuse `action` with `action_kind: docket`. Either way state: "`lens` and `resid` are channels, never instrument readings; remove `lens` from the §10 example."
- **S5-01** (major, §5.1; §5.2; §5.3; §9) — §5.1: (a) the nuisance plan (R, the enumerated nuisance factors and their values) must be logged as a cert BEFORE the runs (a `prereg` with `kind = noise-plan`, or a field in the battery cert), and every run under that plan is a logged fingerprint whose `refs` name the plan; a floor whose run count is below the plan's R, or whose runs are not all in the log, is void (exit 2). (b) Add per-channel floor caps for weights subjects at temperature 0 (e.g. `exact` floor > 0.02 marks the fingerprint `low-power`; cap values OPERATOR-GATED). (c) §9: a challenge is evaluated against the floor cert in force at the target's log index; a later `response` floor never reclassifies an earlier challenge; clients also compute and display a challenger-side floor from the challenger's own logged runs (require the challenger's fingerprint to carry R ≥ 2). (d) §5.3: replace "readers judge" with the machine rule: a floor whose runs' `observed_at` span exceeds a window recorded in the plan is void.
- **S5-02** (major, §5.2; §5.3; §6; §13; App. B) — OPERATOR-GATED. Option A: give `--diff` its own verdict vocabulary `{same, exceeds_floor, identity, inconclusive}` with `exceeds_floor` explicitly labelled unconfirmed (`drift` reserved for `--ref` with confirmation); `floor`/`ratio`/`verdict` are null unless A and B share S and A carries a floor. Option B: let `--diff` accept `--confirm <cert C>` (a second existing cert of the same new subject) to satisfy the confirmation rule. Option C for cross-subject diffs: report the distance against BOTH sides' floors (`exceeds_floor_A`, `exceeds_floor_B`) with the fixed sentence 'not a drift claim; subjects differ in <fields>'. In every case add to §5.2: "For a cross-subject diff the floor used is the floor of cert A (the reference); the result cert names it in `floor_source`"; rewrite §13 items 3-4 to say what they output; and add a §6 exit code (or reuse 2) for 'distances printed, no verdict'.
- **S5-03** (major, §5.1.3; §5.2; §6; App. D step 5) — (1) Put the nominal size in the result cert: `null: { runs: R, pairs: m, alpha_single: 1/(m+1), alpha_confirmed: ... }` computed under exchangeability, and print it beside every verdict; alternatively use the empirical CDF with the exceedance probability printed (`p_null = fraction of D_c ≥ d`) and the drift rule `p_null < α` with α fixed in the cert (`noise_floor.alpha`). (2) OPERATOR-GATED on the decision rule: option A — raise R to 8 minimum everywhere (single 4%, confirmed 0.5%); option B — declare overall `drift` only when ≥ 2 channels drift, or apply Bonferroni over channels present, or designate a single primary channel (`exact`) with the rest reported; option C — keep the rule and rename the verdict `exceeds-floor` with the α printed. (3) Use all R reference runs, not one: define d(new, ref) = min over reference runs (or compare to the canonical AND report the min); comparing to a single run discards R−1 samples of the very null that was measured.
- **S5-06** (major, §5; §6; §13; §15.4; App. B) — §5 add a step 5: "Sensitivity receipt. Before any `same` verdict for (S, B, recipe), a `result` cert of kind `sensitivity` (a.k.a. `power`) must be on record, ref'd by every fingerprint that carries a noise floor: for each of a fixed positive-control set on the same (B, recipe) — at minimum q8_0, q4_k_m, the predecessor revision, and where available one small LoRA / fine-tuned sibling (OPERATOR-GATED list) — the per-channel distance, the floor, detected/missed, and the list of channels that did NOT detect it (the miss list). Absent that cert, verify prints `inconclusive (no sensitivity on record)` and exits 2." `verify` prints `power: detects {q4_k_m, predecessor}; misses {q8_0}` beside every `same`, and the compliance view may only describe a `same` verdict together with that miss list. Amend §15.4 accordingly (a noise-floor run does not size the battery; a sensitivity run does). Publish the miss list in full.
- **S5-07** (major, §5.2; §6; §7.4; §11) — §7.4 logprob row → "logprob vitals gate | shipped as `unmeasured` (CHANGELOG 'not-stacc fix', tests/test_unmeasured_not_adversarial.py); v8 either keeps `unmeasured` or renames it — OPERATOR-GATED". OPERATOR-GATED: adopt `unmeasured` in §5.2/§6 for the absent-channel case (keep `inconclusive` only for 'no floor on record'), or amend schema.py GATES and the changelog vocabulary to `inconclusive` in a separate operator-signed commit. Add to §15: "6. Verdict vocabulary — OPERATOR-GATED: (a) `same/drift/identity/inconclusive` for verify; (b) `unmeasured`→`inconclusive`; (c) retirement of ci-test's 0/1 contract in favour of 0–3; (d) whether v0.1 statuses SAME_LINE/SKEW/DRIFT/HEAD_MISMATCH/TAMPER survive anywhere."
- **S5-09** (major, §5.1; §9; App. D(5)) — OPERATOR-GATED: (a) add `noise_floor.covers: ["batch","order"]` and `not_covered: ["hardware","driver"]` to the cert; `verify` prints the coverage line and a challenge from hardware outside `covers` is classified `beyond-floor-coverage`, never `dispute`; or (b) require a second machine (Linux CI runner with GPU, or a rented instance) in every R-run set before a floor may be called a floor. Either way, rewrite §9 'Disputed' to condition on the floor's declared coverage.
- **S7-05** (major, §7.2; §8.6) — §7.2: "`commitment` is computed over `body_to_reveal` = the COMPLETE prereg body (hypotheses, endpoints, tests, datasets, exclusion rules, kill gates, confound gate, conjunction rule, `instrument.code_sha256`) per the S7-04 construction. The sealed cert carries `reveal_by` (RFC 3339). The reveal cert carries `body_to_reveal` and `salt` and refs the sealed cert; results and promotions ref the reveal cert, which must ref a sealed cert whose STH timestamp precedes the result's raw-data hashes. A `result` may ref a sealed prereg only if a reveal cert for it has a lower log index than the result; otherwise the result is `lab`. Clients list, per issuer key, sealed preregs past `reveal_by` with no reveal as `abandoned`, and the count is shown wherever that issuer's promotions are shown." If a partial seal is wanted for competitive reasons, OPERATOR-GATED: allow `sealed_fields` to be listed in the clear, and require every unsealed analysis field (endpoints, tests, exclusions, kill gates) to appear in the clear in the sealed cert itself so the log timestamps them.
- **S7-09** (major, §7.4) — OPERATOR-GATED: keep `unmeasured` (row reads "shipped; v8 keeps `unmeasured`") or rename to `inconclusive` in its own PR with the rename listed in §11's removed table.
- **S8-11** (major, §8.1; §8.2; §8.4; §15.1) — Define: "`log_id` = sha256 of the raw 32-byte ed25519 log public key, encoded `sha256:<hex>`; it identifies the key, so a rotation is a new log_id. A mirror pins the log public key out of band (README + a sworn span) and rejects an STH whose `log_id` does not match." §8.2: "... the ndjson files, one STH, and the pinned log public key." OPERATOR-GATED: (a) forbid rotation in v8.0 (one log key, hardware-held, STH signing is the only offline step) or (b) add a `keys/` history file plus a signed rotation record that the new key countersigns and the old key signs, and require `mirror` to verify STHs against the key valid at that tree_size.
- **S8-16** (major, §8.3; §9) — Specify v1 as: a policy-change entry (the amended `prereg`/`doc` type) marks the first open-submission index; admission is mechanical (schema+sig+size cap) and any rejection is itself logged with reason; state plainly that in v1 'distinct keys' is NOT independence and drop 'distinct hardware' from the disputed rule or make it advisory. OPERATOR-GATED: whether v1 open submission belongs in 8.0 at all given no hosted service.
- **S8-17** (major, §8; §5.2; §11; §14) — OPERATOR-GATED: (a) rename the v8 log (e.g. `styxx.tlog`/`ledger`) and keep charon v0.1 as the re-derivation instrument whose lines are later logged as `result` certs, or (b) declare charon v0.1 superseded: freeze `papers/charon/` as history (never rebuilt), add a §8.7 stating the v0.1 log's final head as the first entry-body of the new log, and rename §5.2's `drift` to `behavior-drift` (or rename v0.1's) so one word has one meaning. Either way the SKEW/DRIFT vocabulary decision needs Flobi's signature per doctrine.
- **S9-02** (major, §9; §8.3) — §9: delete "distinct hardware"; state: "The log does not verify key independence; Disputed and reproduction counts are computed only over issuer keys in the client's trust file (`trust.json`, outside the log, default = the fathom key plus keys the client operator adds). Counts over untrusted keys are displayed separately and labelled `unvetted`." §2: "Clients display the key fingerprint; `issuer.name` is a label and is never used for identity or display without the key."
