OPERATOR-GATED: whether v1 open submission needs a per-key cost (proof of a prior logged fingerprint from the same key, or a fee) — trade-off: raises the bar for honest outside challengers.
- **S2-11** (minor, §2.2; §5.1.1; §5.3; §6; App. D steps 3-4) — (1) Define the S used for floor lookup as (provider, alias, region) — exclude `observed_at` and `observed_model_id` from the floor key, keep them in the cert. (2) `noise_floor.window: { start, end, runs }` is mandatory for aliases. (3) `verify --ref` on an alias subject re-measures R runs NOW (cheap API calls), computes floor_now, and decides against max(floor_then, floor_now); it must compare `observed_model_id` and `region` and print them beside the verdict; a differing `observed_model_id` is reported as `identity` (exit 1) regardless of distances (the one case where identity has evidence); a differing region or a run outside the cert's window is labelled `floor scope: <window>, <region>` and the verdict is capped at `same (out-of-window)` / `drift (out-of-window)` so the CI contract carries the caveat. (4) Alias floors expire: after `window.end + 30 d` (OPERATOR-GATED) a `--ref` without re-measurement exits 2.
- **S4-17** (minor, §4 title; §4.1–4.6; §11; App. C) — OPERATOR-GATED: rename the v8 object (options: `sentinel-v1`, `edge-v1`, `probe-v1`) throughout §4, §11, §12, §13, App. C and the battery `kind` enum; or keep "canary" in v8 and rename the sworn planted spans, which requires re-issuing a sworn spec. Do not commit entry #0 with the collision in place.
- **S4-18** (minor, §4.4; §4.5; §4.6; §15.3; §3.1; §8.6) — Replace the §4.6 bullet with: "canary-v1 detects changes that were not optimised against the published battery. A party that trains or distils against the battery defeats it; publication makes this possible and the spec accepts it. Re-selection is a response to a *detected* change, not a defence against a hidden one." Add the same to §8.6. Add to §5.2 the subject-hash row (see S4-02). OPERATOR-GATED beyond that: (a) sealed canaries via the §7.2 commitment mechanism — publish `sha256(salt || JCS(items))` in the battery cert, keep prompts sealed, reveal (with salt) only in the result cert that claims drift, then re-select; (b) two batteries per subject: public fixed-v1 for public claims, sealed canary-v1 for detection with reveal-on-drift, amending §4.5 so a sealed (not redacted) battery can back a public drift claim once revealed; (c) hold back a sealed half of each canary battery (commitment logged, prompts revealed at rotation), displayed as a note since it is not stranger-reproducible until reveal. The `output_text` default follows the same decision.

## Refuted

Kept because a refuted sentence is history too. Each line: id, title, the refuter's reason.

- **S15-01** · §15.4; App. C; §4.5 · Battery sizes are called 'guesses' to be adjusted after the noise-floor runs, but the canary-v1 params are frozen into the battery cert and 'changing them makes canary-v2' — The finding misreads App. C. The versioning sentence names exactly 'Weights 0.6 / 0.4 and τ' as the parameters whose change makes canary-v2; N and K are not in that sentence. §4.4 step 4 already has three N values (64 quick / 256 standard / 1024 docket) coexisting under the single name canary-v1, so the spec already treats N as a per-battery field rather than part of the version, and §15.4's 'adjust once, then freeze' concerns exactly N/K. Comparability is keyed on the battery cert id (§2.3), so any battery re-selection breaks cross-cert comparison regardless of what the version string says — that is by design, not a defect. K=64 appearing hard-coded in App. C's anchor rule is a small ambiguity a reasonable implementer resolves by reading `k_anchors` from §4.5 `params`.
- **S12-02** · §12; §13 · 'Constraints enforced in code' are not code-checkable as stated, and 'no adjectives' forbids the spec's own labels — Textually accurate — §12 does label 'neutral description of a cert' as a 'constraint enforced in code' and §13.6 says 'no adjectives' — but not material. §12's own parenthetical example ('fingerprint cert X, 256 canaries + 64 anchors, noise floor from 8 runs') is a slot-filled template of cert fields, and §1.6 plus §7.2 already fix the only free text allowed (the promotion `claim` string quoted verbatim); any competent implementer enforces 'neutral' by rendering exclusively from templates over cert fields, which is precisely the amendment. The 'numerals are adjectives' and 'black-box is an adjective' objections are grammatical pedantry: no implementer would read 'no adjectives' as forbidding the spec's own required labels or the §7.1 banner. The residual consequence ('a reader trusting enforced in code is misled') is stylistic wording, not a wrong verdict, unverifiable cert, or doctrine violation.
- **S12-03** · §12; §14 · Compliance view is unscheduled, names GPAI obligation categories, and 'not a product' does not remove the exposure it creates — The 'unscheduled' claim misreads §14: §11 defines `styxx attest --subject <spec> --view research|compliance`, and §14 weeks 4–8 schedules '`attest` with the lab banner' — the compliance view is a projection produced by that command, so it is scheduled with it; the word 'compliance' need not recur. The exposure argument is already handled in-text: §0 non-goals ('No conformity assertions ... counsel decides'), §12's fixed header ('It does not assert conformity with any regulation'), §1.6 scope-limited claims, and §12's rule that lab-tier readings never appear in the documentation category. Naming a counsel reviewer is a lab process matter, not a spec defect, and 'a downstream user hands the output to an authority' is speculative; the doctrine 'maximal in scope, minimal in claim' is satisfied by organizing evidence under a disclaimer. Nothing here yields a wrong verdict, an unverifiable cert, or a false claim.
- **S10-02** · §10; §0; §12 · Action sub-log proves only what the agent chose to include; dropped actions leave no trace, and the cert is self-attested, which §0 calls a note — The facts are correct (the agent owns and rolls its own sub-log; readings are self-reported) but the consequence is not a defect of the spec as written. §10 claims only that an included action is "provable with two inclusion proofs"; it never claims completeness or proof of absence, and the inability to prove that an unlogged action did not occur is inherent to every transparency log including RFC 6962 (a CA that never logs a cert is not caught by the log). The compliance view already excludes action certs: the §12 table lists fingerprint, battery, result, promotion, challenge and never `action`, and §12 confines lab-tier readings to the lab banner, so `readings.lab.depth` in a logged cert is exactly the §7.1 lab-section treatment, not a receipt-less positioning number. The witnessed-gate memory line ("We ADJUDICATE, we do not record") is about trace recorders for the evidence leg, not agent-emitted attestations. The proposed disclaimer sentence is a reasonable minor addition, but no implementer following the spec would produce a wrong verdict, a false claim, or a §12 violation without it.
- **S3-02** · §3.1; App. A.4; App. B · seqlp/topk channels carry `vector_sha256` over doubles while App. A.4 forbids comparing log-probs by hash, and no serialization is given — Textually accurate: §3.1 lists `"seqlp": {"vector_sha256"}` / `"topk": {"vector_sha256"}` and A.4 says log-probs are "compared numerically (Appendix B), never by hash." But there is no operational contradiction: §3.3 ("Every channel has exactly one distance function; `verify` never picks") and App. B bind `verify` to numeric distances over the per-item `seq_logprob`/`topk` arrays, so no implementer following the spec would compare by hash and no verdict can be wrong. The preimage is under-specified, but A.4 ("IEEE-754 doubles rendered by JCS") plus A.3 make sha256(JCS(vector)) the obvious reading, and a stranger never needs to recompute the field to verify (id/sig cover the issuer's bytes; the stranger issues their own cert). A dead summary field with an unstated preimage is an ambiguity worth cleaning, not a major defect.
- **S3-03** · §3.2; §5.2; §6 exit 2; App. D step 4-5 · Any absent channel forces the overall verdict to `inconclusive`, so alias fingerprints and strangers without white-box hardware or provider logprobs can never reach `same`/`drift` — The headline claim ("`same` is unreachable for a black-box exact-only fingerprint") misreads §5.2/§6: §6 exit 2 is for "a channel absent on ONE side", and §3.2 explicitly treats absent channels as a normal state ("else channel absent, never zero-filled"); the ref cert's `noise_floor.per_channel` only carries channels the cert has, so a competent implementer evaluates the channels present in the ref and an alias exact-only cert reaches `same`. The remaining cases (stranger without `--white-box` against a white-box cert; provider drops logprobs) are exactly what §6 says — exit 2 — and that matches the spec's own commitments (§6 "Silence beats a misleading number"; §7.4 "absent logprobs ⇒ `inconclusive`, never a flag"). A conservative `inconclusive` is not a wrong verdict, and a stranger can still issue a recipe-matching black-box fingerprint (recipe comparability in §2.3 does not include the white-box flag) and a §9 challenge with only `exact` in `per_channel`. Per-channel restriction (`--channels`, `channels_absent`) is a feature request; the spec should state `exact` is mandatory, but that is an ambiguity most implementers resolve correctly.
- **S3-06** · §3.2; §0 table · §3.2 'lens' smuggles depth back into v8.0 as a checksum channel with an unreceipted 'proxy for computational depth' claim — The quoted texts are accurate (§3.2 "Cheap proxy for computational depth."; FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth line ~51 "std 0.0558"; TICKET line 43 "claims depth flags hallucination ... the receipt that it must not"). But the ticket forbids copy claiming depth flags hallucination; the spec makes no truth/hallucination claim for `lens` — it is a distance-only checksum channel (App. B), and §12 restricts compliance output to promotion `claim` strings or neutral cert descriptions, so the phrase cannot reach the compliance view under a competent implementation. The lab has a receipt using logit-lens explicitly as a depth proxy (papers/grounded-honesty-axis/FINDING_depth_grounding_whitebox_2026_05_29.md: "SAE-free logit-lens DLA depth proxy"), so the wording has precedent, and no number is asserted. The degenerate-floor worry is speculative and not specific to lens: any deterministic channel (exact included) has floor 0 by design. Deleting the phrase is a harmless stylistic tightening.
- **S3-09** · §3.2; App. B · seqlp distance is length-weighted and, after a flip, compares log-probs of different sequences — The reading is accurate (App. B seqlp = mean |Δ seq_logprob| over items, §3.2 sum log-prob of the greedy sequence), but the consequence is a loss of power in one channel, not a wrong verdict or false claim: App. B computes the floor with the same function, so length-weighted noise raises the floor in the same units and §5.2 still cannot fire `drift` below a measured null. A flip on a short item is caught by the `exact` channel (1/N quantum) and §5.2 makes the overall verdict `drift` if any channel drifts, so the 'masking' is not a missed detection. Comparing log-probs of different strings is semantically muddled but the spec never claims seqlp is a same-sequence shift; missing units (nats) is stylistic. The teacher-forcing amendment is a design preference and is unavailable for alias subjects anyway.
- **S0-06** · §0; §2.2; §8.6; §12; App. D · By §0's own rule alias fingerprints are notes, yet §2.2/§12 issue and display them as certs — Quotes are accurate, but the spec resolves the tension elsewhere: §2.2 labels every alias-derived cert tier: black-box and says the spec 'does not pretend otherwise'; §5.1 defines the alias noise floor over time-of-day/region; §5.3 records the window and leaves the reader to judge; §8.6 explicitly scopes out providers who make reproduction impossible; and Appendix D step 3 already branches on 'or note that it's an alias'. Step 4 remains executable against a live alias (yielding same/drift/inconclusive exit 2), and a withdrawn alias is the provider's act, not a defect 'from the cert alone'. The §12 documentation-category concern is handled by the tier label propagating to every downstream cert. Whether black-box certs should be demoted to 'notes' is a policy choice for the operator, not an ambiguity an implementer would get wrong.
- **S0-07** · §0 non-goals; §8.2; §8.3; §14 · 'No hosted service' contradicts GitHub Pages hosting, and v1 'rate-limited by issuer key' has no mechanism on a PR-based log — §0 defines its own term in the same breath: "No hosted service until a design partner asks for one. Charon v0 is a git repo." — read with §14 ("If a design partner wants one, the partner's need designs it") this plainly means no service operated by the lab, and any implementer reads GitHub Pages as static publication of that repo, not a contradiction. §8.2 already says a mirror "needs only the ndjson files and one STH to verify anything", so availability rests on git clone of the repo, not on Pages bandwidth; the bandwidth/build-limit consequences are speculative (hourly STH is far under Pages' build limits). "Rate-limited by issuer key" is v1 and outside the §14 build; CI already recomputes id and checks signature (§8.3), so it knows the key and a per-key quota in the merge check is a trivially available mechanism — the claim that a PR-based log cannot rate-limit by key is false. Multi-key spam is inherent to any open-submission log and is not a spec defect. No verdict, cert, claim, or doctrine is affected.
- **S5-04** · §5.2; §6; App. D step 5 · The confirmation run does not test noise in any regime the spec deploys it in (identical re-run on deterministic hardware confirms every false positive), and `same (transient)` hides a falsified floor — The headline claims overreach. 'Does not test noise in any regime' is contradicted by the reviewer's own numbers (confirmation cuts the stochastic-null rate ~10% → ~2.8%), and the persistent-environment leg is handled elsewhere: §2.2 puts `hardware`/`driver`/`runtime` in the subject, so a verifier on a different box is a different S and §5.3/§5.2 row 1 exit 2, not `drift`. 'Hides a falsified floor' misreads a sample maximum as a claimed bound — an exceedance at ~1/(m+1) is expected by construction, and the result cert (§6) records the distance and the `same (transient)` verdict, so the event is not hidden; the full null distribution is also stored per §5.1 step 3. What survives is minor: the confirmation run's nuisance variation is unspecified and `same (transient)` has no explicit exit-code row, but any implementer maps it to 0 ('same') and re-runs pick up fresh kernel/provider nondeterminism in exactly the regimes where the floor is non-zero.
- **S5-10** · §5.2; §6 · Verdict-priority conflicts between §5.2 and §6 (absent channel vs drift; identity vs inconclusive) — §5.2's overall-verdict sentence already states the precedence the finding says is missing: 'drift if any channel is drift; identity overrides everything; inconclusive if any channel lacks a floor and none drifted; else same' — identity > drift > inconclusive > same is explicit. §6's row 2 ('no noise floor, or a channel absent on one side') describes what the inconclusive label covers, not a competing override; an absent channel is a channel without a floor, and 'none drifted' makes drift win in the first scenario. In the second scenario 'identity overrides everything' governs row 1's exit 2, and anchors need no floor because identity is an exact-match test. A competent implementer following §5.2's ordered sentence produces one exit code; the residual gap (channels absent on both sides) is an ambiguity any implementation resolves by ignoring the channel. Stylistic clarification at most.
- **S5-12** · §5.2; §6 · `same (transient)` has no exit code — `same (transient)` is a per-channel verdict in the §5.2 table; the overall-verdict rule in the same section collapses it: a transient channel is not `drift`, does not lack a floor, so the overall verdict is 'else `same`', which §6 maps to exit 0. §6's header 'confirm on drift' shows the confirmation step is built into `verify --ref` and its outcome feeds the overall verdict. No implementer reading §5.2 and §6 together would return 1 for a verdict whose overall label is `same`; recording `transient: true` in the result cert is a nice-to-have, not a defect.
- **S8-09** · §8.2; §8.1; §8.3; §8.4 · STH filenames at hour granularity collide and cannot be ordered by tree_size — §8.2's `sth/2026-09-07T18.json` is an illustrative directory listing, not a naming rule, and the STH already carries `tree_size` (§8.1), which any implementer would use as the order key; consistency proofs are defined for any m<n, not only adjacent STHs. The claimed consequence does not follow: a same-hour overwrite of a smaller-tree STH by a larger one is a normal extension, not the same-tree_size/different-root event §8.4 defines as misbehaviour, §8.4 mirrors 'record the STHs they have seen', and git history retains the file. Real but minor ambiguity that a reasonable implementer resolves correctly; the amendment is fine as a clarification but does not fix a wrong verdict, unverifiable cert, or attack.
- **S8-20** · §8.1; §8.3; App. A.1 · Timestamp encoding in existing tree heads and the spec's A.1 differ; 'existed at this time' has no defined instant — The cited code is real (`styxx/transparency.py:306` emits `isoformat()` with `+00:00`), but that is pre-spec code, not the spec; A.1 line 372 unambiguously pins `Z`, so an implementer following the spec produces `Z` and the existing module is simply out of conformance, which is not a spec defect. The "two different signatures for the same instant" consequence is moot because only the log key signs STHs and a reader verifies the bytes it is given. On the log-time question, §7.2 line 211 already says "The log timestamp is the proof of order", ruling out cert `created`, and any RFC 6962-literate implementer derives the (previous STH, first covering STH] interval the amendment proposes; no verdict or number depends on it.
- **S8-21** · §8.4; §11 · `mirror` source and failure semantics undefined: git clone vs Pages fetch, and no exit/status on a broken consistency proof — §8.4 line 257 does define `mirror`'s behavior: clone, verify every consecutive consistency proof, record seen STHs, and on equivocation publish both STHs; §8.2 line 252 says the ndjson files plus one STH suffice, so git-clone versus Pages-fetch yields the same bytes and is not material. What is missing is only an exit-code table, and §6 establishes the CI exit-code pattern that any competent implementer would extend (a command that "verifies every consistency proof" and exits 0 on failure would contradict §6's "silence beats a misleading number" posture). The pass/fail gap in the §14 release gate is already captured by S8-18. Speculative and stylistic; no wrong verdict follows from the spec as written.
- **S2-07** · §2; §2.3; §4.2; §4.5 · Battery cert needs a recipe.battery, canary battery needs a reference fingerprint, and the fingerprint needs a battery — circular and unsatisfiable — The 'unsatisfiable' claim needs `recipe.battery` to be mandatory on battery certs and `selected_against` to require a fingerprint over the pool; neither is in the text. §2's envelope shows `"..."` placeholders keyed to §2.2/§2.3, and §7.2 prereg certs plainly carry no battery, so presence is already per-type by implication. §13 fixes the order: step 1 issues the fixed-v1 fingerprint (whose `recipe.battery` is the fixed-v1 battery cert, which exists), step 2 selects canaries against that subject; §4.2 says the δ-sweep runs 'on the reference subject, reference precision', which the fixed-v1 fingerprint identifies, so `selected_against` resolves without any cycle. What survives is editorial (a per-type presence table would remove the ambiguity) and the real residue — the pool is a bare `pool_sha256`, δ-runs are unlogged — is S4-03's finding, not this one.
- **S2-08** · §2.1; §14 weeks 1–4; App. A.4 · "Use a library; do not hand-roll" reverses the recorded JCS decision: no RFC 8785 library is installed and the parity-pinned `styxx.attestation.jcs` is what every committed digest depends on — The finding's load-bearing premise is false: `rfc8785` 0.1.4 is installed on this box (`site-packages/rfc8785`), contradicting SCOUT.md:10, and the untracked in-progress `styxx/v8/jcs.py` plus `INTERFACES_foundations.md` already use it with domain validation, a `styxx.attestation.jcs` fallback and a mandated differential/mutation test. The cited repo facts are real (attestation.py code-point key sort, sworn_verify.js:417 `Object.keys().sort()`, ATTACKS lines 82–83, the parity test) but the consequence is inverted: `Object.keys().sort()` is UTF-16 order, which is what RFC 8785 and the library do, so a library makes Python agree with the JS verifiers better than the hand-rolled sort does. I ran the differential: identical output on the ordinary domain, divergence only on non-BMP-mixed keys and ints beyond 2^53, which committed artifacts do not contain (ASCII keys are refused at charon ingest). Nothing in §2.1 says to replace the legacy canonicalizer for existing digests, so the 'charon head stops re-deriving' consequence is speculative. What remains is a stylistic/ambiguity gap: the spec could state the numeric domain and that legacy artifacts keep their own canonicalizer; minor.
- **S2-19** · §2; §4; §5.2; §8; §9; §10 · Field and vocabulary collisions with shipped schemas: `verdict`, `subject`, `kind`, DRIFT and `canary` already mean other things in the same package — Every citation checks out (certify.py:1337 OATH-HELD/FAILED; sworn.py:121 VERDICTS; charon.py KINDS/STATUSES with DRIFT = bytes moved per charon_verify_result.json note; charon entry `subject` = document; capsule gate CONTRADICTED; schema.py GATES; sworn measurement spec 'canary twins'), so the collisions are textually real. But they are collisions across separately-schemed objects (`styxx.charon/entry/v1` vs `styxx: 8.0`), and the v8 spec changes none of the existing vocabularies; the doctrine actually recorded in memory (styxx-calibration-leg-2026-09-05.md) gates renaming an existing verdict such as SWORN-HELD, not introducing an enum in a new format. No implementer following the spec produces a wrong verdict, an unverifiable cert, or a false claim because `drift` also appears in charon v0.1 reports; the harm is reader confusion in a shared tree, which is naming/stylistic. Worth a vocabulary appendix, but minor. (The reuse of the name 'charon' for a different object, flagged in SCOUT, is a stronger point but is not this finding's claim.)
- **S2-20** · §2.2; §7.1 · `tier: black-box` has no field to live in and collides with the instrument tier vocabulary — Textually correct: §2.2 says alias subjects are 'labeled `tier: black-box` in every downstream cert', §7.1 defines `tier` as certified|lab over instruments, and no body in §3.1/§4.5/§6/§7.2/§9/§10 carries a `tier` key. But every downstream cert already carries the envelope `subject` with `kind: alias`, so the label is derivable without a field and any implementer will render it from `subject.kind`; the sentence reads as a display instruction, not a schema field. No verdict, number, or claim goes wrong if the sentence is read that way, and the axis collision is a wording problem. Minor ambiguity, likely resolved correctly; delete-or-clarify is appropriate but not major.
- **S4-19** · whole document; §4; §4.5; §5.1; §7.2; §9; §3.1 · The spec is not a sworn document and carries numeric and empirical claims a stranger cannot distinguish from measured ones — The premise is false: SCOUT.md:12 says every SPEC in papers/ is sworn, but the repo shows sidecars for RESULT/PLAN/DESIGN files only (38 *.sworn.json) and NO sidecar for any SPEC — papers/charon/SPEC_charon_v01_2026_09_02.md, SPEC_oath_capsule_v01/v02, SPEC_oath_receipt_binding, and all 20 papers/sworn/SPEC_*_v01_*.md lack one, including the sworn measurement machinery SPEC itself. The lab's convention is that specs are not sworn documents; the v8 draft follows it. The tooling point also fails: styxx/corpus_audit.py enumerates *.certificate.json and papers/charon/build_log.py enumerates *.sworn.json/*.capsule.html/*.certificate.json, so an unsworn spec is neither seen nor accused — 'tooling never sees it' is the norm, not a defect. The one genuinely dangerous number (§7.2 ΔAUC 0.041 example, contradicted by papers/depth-truth/FINDING_depth_does_not_predict_truth_2026_07_03.md) is already covered by S4-15; the remaining values (R=5, N=256, pool_size 12000, mean 14.2) are design defaults and JSON placeholders any implementer reads as such.
- **S4-25** · §4; §7.4 · 'gold fix' and 'strict-gate normalization' applied in place to styxx-bench would move committed bench results — The cited README (bench/README.md:54-62) does list scorer.py, leaderboard.py and results/, but none of them exist in the worktree: `git ls-files bench` returns only README.md, suite.yaml and four tasks/*.jsonl (84 lines; the listed adversarial.jsonl is also absent), and there is no bench/results/ directory anywhere in the repo. No committed receipt or sworn sidecar hashes tasks/*.jsonl (grep over papers/, conformance/, styxx/ finds no digest of them; oath_external_corpus.json lists only bench/README.md). The one paper citing bench/tasks (papers/cognitive-monitoring-without-logprobs.md:211) is unsworn, has no .sworn.json or receipt, and cites by path and count, not by digest. So the 'committed receipts' the finding says would move do not exist, and a-receipt-is-history-too concerns committed receipts. The spec also already binds fixed-v1 to bytes via §4.5 `pool_sha256`, and bench/README.md already versions scoring via suite.yaml schema_version. Good hygiene to version the task files, but not a defect in the spec as written.
- **S4-26** · §4; §4.5; §15 · §15 lists as open two decisions the body has already made — Quotes are accurate: §4 says fixed-v1 is public, §4.5 says the default is clear, and §15.2/§15.3 present both as open with 'Recommend'. But §15 is explicitly titled 'Open decisions (flobi)' and each recommendation matches the body's default exactly, so there is no contradiction and no implementer produces anything different whichever passage they treat as binding. The header already states the document is a DRAFT that becomes binding only when committed and logged as charon entry #0, which is precisely the mechanism by which the open items close. Purely editorial.
